# Task briefs

Several agents work on MachineRoom at the same time. Each one works on its own branch in its own git worktree, and Claude reviews and merges every branch. The rules every agent follows are in `docs/agents/HOUSE-RULES.md`. Who works where, on which model, and which paths each agent may not touch are in `docs/agents/lanes.json`. The bridge builds the prompt for a task from those two files and the brief below (`GET /api/control/brief-prompt`, task T1a), so nobody has to write or paste prompts by hand.

A brief is one paragraph that starts `**ID. Title (Owner).**`. Keep that shape: the parser reads it.

## Done

- OpenCode B1 to B4 (merged as 0.1.5): incremental pipeline, bridge server with agent mapping, tree, SSE with replay after `Last-Event-ID`, tree watcher, and tests against a fake Paperclip.
- AGY F1 to F6 (merged as 0.1.7): HUD pass, run markers, event log drawer, terminal styling, mode switch and help overlay.
- Claude (0.1.4 to 0.1.8): one shared pipeline, bridge client in the viewer, markers, log and mode switch wired, live transport, simulated feed, layout, follow mode and labels.
- OpenCode B5 (merged as 0.1.21): four recorded scenarios under `public/fixtures/scenario-*`, `scripts/frames-from-runs.mjs` (rebuild frames from Paperclip's stored runs), renames and deletes count as writes. Findings are folded into P5 and P6 below.
- AGY F7 (merged as 0.1.11) and Claude E1 (a real Paperclip run compared with the terminals, 0.1.8 to 0.1.9).
- Claude A1 (0.1.20): the assistant core.
- AGY C3a (merged as 0.1.22): the control drawer with dispatch, tasks, approvals and meters, running on the mock.
- Claude D1 (0.1.27): the decision model seam, probe sets and comparison script. Waiting for the owner to run it.
- AGY A2 (merged as 0.1.28): the floating assistant terminal in the HUD.
- Claude A3 (0.1.31): the dispatcher. The assistant now moves the camera (agent, terminal, folder or file, overview, follow), controls replay, and runs confirmed writes (task, budget) through the control client. Opening a panel or a task thread needs AGY's C3b (`controlTarget`).

## Open work

On 2026-10-25 every older brief is merged, done by its parts or retired with Paperclip. Still open: **P4** (licence and public release, the owner's decision) and the newest briefs at the end of this file. The sections below keep the history in the order it was written.

**B6. Late join replay, agent roles and unique keys (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.57).** A viewer that opens during a run sees only frames that arrive after it connects. Let `/events` send the kept history to a client that connects without `Last-Event-ID`, behind a `?history=1` query so the viewer can ask for it, and make `src/live/bridge.js` ask for it on its first connect (not on a reconnect, which sends `Last-Event-ID`). The pipeline already drops frames it has seen. Also, for the room redesign (V4): the agent list carries each agent's Paperclip `role` (`ceo`, `engineer` and so on, or null), in `mapAgent` in `server/index.js` and in the matching mapping in `src/live/source.js`. And keys must stay unique: today `mapAgent` keys an agent by its adapter, so Paperclip's Orchestrator (a `claude_local` agent) gets the key `claude`, the same as the Claude Code agent, and the room would merge the two. The first agent with a known adapter keeps the adapter key, and any later agent whose key is taken gets the slug of its name (and a number if that is taken too), with the same parser. Do the same in both places, in list order, so a recording and the live bridge agree. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js` (history on and off, a reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`, two `claude_local` agents, the role) and in the live source tests. Files: `server/index.js`, `src/live/bridge.js`, `src/live/source.js`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, the live source test file, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**E2. Paperclip plugin page (Claude, researched by E2a; on 2026-10-22 the owner kept the separate app and E2b to E2d are not built).** Check whether the viewer can ship as a page inside Paperclip instead of a separate app.

**E2a. Can the room ship as a page inside Paperclip? (Claude, merged as 0.1.150; the answer and briefs E2b to E2d are in `docs/agents/PLUGIN-PAGE.md`; the owner chose not to build them for now).** The first part of E2, research only. Read Paperclip's plugin system in the installed `paperclipai` 2026.1012.0 (`npm install -g paperclipai@2026.1012.0` if it is missing): the plugin routes, the manifest, how a plugin adds UI (a page, a slot, an iframe or a bundle), how that UI talks to the server and with what auth, what a plugin worker can read and do (the live event stream, the heartbeat run log, the files of a project folder, other routes), how a plugin is installed, enabled, updated and removed, and what a plugin may not do. Then write `docs/agents/PLUGIN-PAGE.md` in plain words with these sections: (1) what a plugin page is, naming each file and route you read; (2) whether the room could run there, part by part: the event stream (today the bridge holds Paperclip's socket, restores truncated chunks and masks keys), the file tree (today the bridge lists the project folder with `git ls-files -z`), the control client's `/api/control/*` routes (which could become direct Paperclip calls from the page, which need a worker), the `confirmed: true` rule for writes, and the CSS3D and WebGL scene inside Paperclip's page; (3) what has to stay outside Paperclip (lane runs, reviews and merges, which start git and the harness CLIs on this machine); (4) a recommendation: a plugin page, the separate app as today, or both, with what each costs in work and what it gains the owner; (5) if a plugin page is worth it, the first two or three briefs to get there, written like the briefs in this file, each with its `Files:` list. Quote no more than a line of Paperclip's code at a time; name files and functions instead. If the package ships an example plugin and you can start a throwaway Paperclip in your own VM in under 20 minutes, with no keys and nothing pointed at another Paperclip, you may load a hello-world page to confirm what you read; say exactly what you ran. Change no code. Files: `docs/agents/PLUGIN-PAGE.md` (new), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Roadmap: subagents and a public release

The goal is a public application that turns Paperclip into an overview room where you watch agents, and the subagents they start through their own harnesses, move between folders and touch files. The recordings so far hold no real subagent events, so the data comes first.

**S1. Record subagent runs (OpenCode, extends B5; done as S1a; the subagent recordings are scenario-e to scenario-i).** One scenario per harness, tiny tasks, cheapest model that works: Claude Code starting a subagent with its Task tool, OpenCode starting one with its `task` tool, Hermes using `delegate_task`. Also record Paperclip's own delegation: the Orchestrator agent creating an issue for another agent (the `activity.logged` frame with `issue.created` carries the creator, the assignee and `parentId`). Report which signal in the raw log identifies the parent, the child and the child's tool calls. AGY's own session log for C3a shows it starting a subagent (a "Delegation" routine that invoked one named DeepCoder), so add one headless `agy --print --output-format stream-json` run with a task that makes it delegate, and report whether the stream carries the child.

**S1a. Subagent signals from the Hermes and OpenCode CLIs (orchestrator, done on 2026-10-21).** Recorded by the orchestrator with one read-only task each that asked for one subagent, run in the lane worktrees, a few cents in all. Claude Code, agy and Paperclip's own delegation (`issue.created` with `parentId`) were not recorded. Hermes (`hermes chat -q` with the `delegation` toolset, the stdout Paperclip shows): `┊ 🔀 preparing delegate_task…` when the call starts, then `┊ 🔀 delegate  1x: GOAL...  14.4s` when every child has finished (the count of children, the first goal cut short, the duration). The child's own tool lines never reach the parent's stdout; the session export holds one `delegate_task` call with `tasks: [{ goal, ... }]` and the result `{ results: [{ task_index, status, summary }] }`. OpenCode (`opencode run --format json`): the tool is named `subagent`, not `task`. One `tool_use` line arrives when the child is done, with `input: { agent, description, prompt }`, `state.status`, `state.time.start` and `end` in ms, and `state.metadata.sessionID` naming the child's own session; the child's tool calls are not in the parent's stream. Today's parsers already read both lines as one `other` tool call timed from its duration (`delegate 1x: ...` and `subagent {...}`), and the unreadable counter counts neither. So a log names a child only after it has ended and never shows what it did: S2a, S3a and S2b are held until a harness that streams the child's calls is recorded (Claude Code's Task tool is the likely one).

**S2. Subagent schema and room (Claude; done by S2a to S2g). Add `agent.spawn` (parent, child, label) to the activity schema, let the state and the room add agents while they run, draw the spawn as a new shape splitting off its parent along a string, and give each child a terminal on the wall.

**S3. Subagent parsers (OpenCode; done by S3a, S2c and S5; each parser reads its harness's subagents).** Detect the spawn in each harness and attribute the nested tool calls to the child. Needs S1 and S2's schema.

**P1. One command install (OpenCode).** `npx machine-room`: one process that serves the built viewer and the bridge on one port, with flags and env for the Paperclip URL, company and project folder, and no Vite or proxy needed. Add a `bin` entry and a test that packs the package and starts it.

**P1a. The bridge serves the built viewer (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.69).** The first step of P1: one process on one port, no Vite and no proxy. `createBridge({ staticDir })` (no default, so tests and `npm run bridge` behave as now) serves the files of a built viewer (`dist/` after `npm run build`) for GET and HEAD requests that no API route answered. `/` gives `index.html`. A path that names a file under `staticDir` gives that file with a content type from its extension (html, js, mjs, css, json, jsonl, svg, png, jpg, webp, woff2, ico, txt; anything else `application/octet-stream`). Any other path that does not start with `/api/` and has no dot in its last part gives `index.html`, so a deep link still opens the app. A path that resolves outside `staticDir` (`..`, an encoded `%2e%2e`, a backslash, a drive letter, a null byte) gets 404, and so does a missing file with an extension. Keep the Host and Origin checks in front of it, and keep the JSON 404 for an unknown `/api/` path. Put the file serving in a new `server/static.js`. Add `bin/machine-room.mjs` and a `bin` entry `machine-room` in `package.json` (keep `"private": true`), with flags `--paperclip <url>`, `--company <id>`, `--root <folder>` and `--port <n>` (each falls back to the environment names the bridge already reads: `PAPERCLIP_URL`, `PAPERCLIP_COMPANY_ID`, `PROJECT_ROOT`, `PORT`), `--dist <folder>` (default: the `dist` folder beside `bin/`) and `--help`. When the dist folder has no `index.html` it prints `Build the viewer first with npm run build.` and exits 1. Otherwise it starts the bridge with `staticDir` and prints one line, `machine-room: open http://127.0.0.1:<port>/?live&bridge=http://127.0.0.1:<port>`, with the real port when `--port 0` is given. It stops cleanly on SIGINT and SIGTERM. Add `npm run serve` that runs it. Do not run `npm pack` or `npm publish` (a later step). Tests in a new `tests/serve.test.js`, all on port 0: a temp dist folder with an `index.html`, a js file and a css file; `/`, each file with its content type, a deep link, HEAD, an unknown `/api/x` (JSON 404), a POST to a static path (404 or 405), traversal attempts (`/../package.json`, `/%2e%2e/package.json`, `/..%5cpackage.json`, `/C:/Windows/win.ini`) that never return a file outside the folder, a bridge with no `staticDir` that still answers 404 for `/`, and the bin run as a child process with a dist folder that has no `index.html` (exit 1 and the sentence) and with one (prints the line, answers `/api/health` and `/`, ends when killed). Files: `server/index.js`, `server/static.js`, `bin/machine-room.mjs`, `package.json`, `tests/serve.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P2. First run and polish (AGY, merged as 0.1.16 and wired in 0.1.17).** A connect screen (Paperclip URL, company picker, demo link), clear empty and error states, remembered settings, a name, logo and favicon, and screenshots for the README.

**P3. Scale (Claude; done by P3a to P3d, then replaced by the cabinet sphere V21).** Collapse and level of detail for projects with thousands of folders, layout in a worker, reduced motion for the 3D scene. Required before daily use on large repositories. The collapse plan is P3a (Hermes, pure); Claude wires it into the layout and the folder tiles.

**P4. Release decisions (the owner).** Licence, repository name and the trademark check on the names Paperclip and POI (use neither logo nor name of the show), whether `sprites.js` (copied from the graded portfolio) may be published or should be rewritten, CI (test and build), and a short security note (the bridge binds 127.0.0.1 and allows only local pages).

## Roadmap: planner, orchestrator and budgets

Design and rules are in `docs/agents/CONTROL-PLANE.md`. Paperclip already has hiring, budgets, approvals, planning and worktrees, so these tasks use its APIs and do not rebuild them.

**C0. Run the dev agents inside Paperclip (Claude, with the owner).** Needs the owner's yes to switch on the instance flag `enableIsolatedWorkspaces`, and a git project workspace for the repo. Register OpenCode, AGY and Claude Code as Paperclip agents with isolated worktree workspaces, so tasks arrive as issues and reports come back as run results and comments. agy needs a launcher that reads its issue through the API, because its arguments hold a fixed prompt today. Try one tiny issue per agent. The owner picks the models first, because each run spends credit. Stage 1 is done: one issue to OpenCode Go with the free model `opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash-free` fixed a typo in a throwaway repo and reported back through the API (see `PAPERCLIP-API.md`). Stage 2, worktrees, waits for the owner's yes on the flag.

**C1. Paperclip API audit (Claude, done).** Written up in `docs/agents/PAPERCLIP-API.md`. Open from it: the answers to a hire and an issue create, and a full plan flow, all need a throwaway write and the owner's OK.

**C2. Control client (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.34).** Make the contract in `CONTROL-CLIENT.md` real. Bridge routes that pass dispatch (an issue with `assigneeAdapterOverrides`), comments, approvals, budgets and costs to Paperclip, local pages only. Then `src/control/client.js`, a browser object with every method of the contract that calls those routes. `subscribe` is fed by the bridge's event stream: when a Paperclip frame touches a topic (run status, issue, comment, approval, cost), send a change notice for it. Write one contract test file that runs the same checks against the mock and against the real client over a fake Paperclip, so the two cannot drift apart. Hire and update an agent wait for C3c. Shapes are in `PAPERCLIP-API.md`. Done when `/?live&bridge=...` passes the real client to the HUD and the drawer shows the live agents and costs.

**C3. Control surface (AGY, starts now against the mock).** The contract is `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md` and the mock is `src/control/mock.js`, so the panels do not wait for C2. In the HUD: a dispatch panel (task, assignee, size, budget, subagents allowed), budget meters, quota meters (session and week percent, reset time), an approvals queue and a task thread. These are merged as C3a (0.1.22). The agent builder form is C3d. Real data needs C2.

**C4. Routing policy, version 1 (Claude; done by C4a to C4f).** A pure module `src/policy/` with tests: task profile to tier, escalation, spend limits, quota-aware choice (skip a provider above a set percent of its window), and the policy file schema.

**C5. Planner and orchestrator agents (Claude; done by C5a to C5e; the orchestrator now runs in the room, K1).** Instruction bundles for both, hired through C2, first run on a tiny goal with the cheapest models and approvals on.

**C6. Outcome log and decision layer (Claude, OpenCode; done by C6a to C6e).** Log every run, build the success and cost tables, propose policy changes as diffs. Start logging as soon as C4 exists.

## Roadmap: working in MachineRoom day to day

The daily loop and its limits are in `docs/agents/CONTROL-PLANE.md` under "Product shape". These tasks fill the gaps between "I can watch" and "I work here". C2 and C3 above are part of it.

**W1. Supervisor (OpenCode; done by W1a and W1b).** `machine-room up` and `machine-room doctor`: attach to a running Paperclip or start one, start the bridge, check health, and warn loudly when the last database backup is missing or older than a day. Extends P1.

**W2. Backups and recovery (Claude, then OpenCode; done by W2a to W2c; retired with Paperclip on 2026-10-24).** Find Paperclip's backup route (there is a route file for instance database backups), add a "back up now" call and a visible backup age in the HUD, and write down how to restore.

**W3. Review and merge (OpenCode for the bridge, AGY for the panel; done by W3a to W3c).** Split: W3a reads the lane branches (OpenCode), W3b merges with a confirm step, W3c is the panel (AGY). Local git through the bridge: the branch of each issue, diff stat and patch, test result from the run report, merge with a confirmation, stop on a conflict, never push. Tests with a throwaway repo.

**W4. Projects (Claude; replaced by --root and the task and queue runs on any project, S8 and S9).** A project and company picker. The bridge serves one project folder today. Several projects means one room per project.

**W5. Talk to the planner (Claude, AGY; retired: Paperclip was removed on 2026-10-24, so there are no issue comments).** A comment thread panel on each issue, posting through `POST /api/issues/:id/comments`. Evaluate Paperclip's board chat stream (`POST /api/board/chat/stream`, experimental, off) after that.

**W6. Live status without polling (Claude, done by W6a).** Use the live-run routes (`/api/companies/:id/live-runs`, `/api/issues/:id/live-runs`) so the panels show which issue is running without reading the whole stream.

**W6a. Which task is running now (Claude, merged as 0.1.144).** The first part of W6. The Tasks panel cannot tell which task an agent is working on right now without reading the whole event stream. Paperclip 2026.1012.0 answers `GET /api/companies/:companyId/live-runs` (queued and running heartbeat runs, each with its agent, adapter, status, times and the `issueId` from the run's context) and `GET /api/issues/:issueId/live-runs`; read their handlers and the spec in the installed `paperclipai` (`@paperclipai/server/dist/routes/agents.js` and `openapi.js`) before you start, and check what `minCount` and `limit` do by default. Add `GET /api/control/live-runs` to the bridge, under the tasks feature with its own route in `server/surface.js`, answering `{ runs }` where each run is `{ runId, agentId, agentName, adapterType, status, issueId, startedAt, lastOutputAt }` (null for a field Paperclip leaves out), only queued and running runs, newest first, at most 50. Send a change notice `{ topic: 'liveRuns' }` from `changeEvents` when a `heartbeat.run.*` frame passes the bridge, so a page refreshes on a run's start and end instead of polling; add the topic to `ChangeEvent` in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`. Add `liveRuns()` to the client and the mock (the mock answers one running run on one of its issues and changes it when its own sample run starts and ends), with its method row and the contract test, and serve the route in `scripts/fake-paperclip.mjs` from its own runs. In `src/ui/panels/TasksPanel.jsx`, read `liveRuns()` when the panel opens and on each `liveRuns` notice, and mark each task that has a live run with `running AGENT` (or `queued AGENT`) in yellow beside its status; a failed read shows nothing and never blocks the list. Agent names go through `visibleText`. Tests: the route (mapping, only live runs, the 50 cap, the feature off, Paperclip failing), the notice for each `heartbeat.run.*` type, the mock and real client through the contract test, and the panel rendered with `renderToString` and the mock showing the mark. Check the mark in a browser against the fake Paperclip if you can; say what you looked at. Files: `server/control.js` or the module that holds the other task routes, `server/surface.js`, `server/index.js` (only `changeEvents` and the wiring), `scripts/fake-paperclip.mjs`, `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`, `src/ui/panels/TasksPanel.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, the tests named above, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`; name any other file in the report and why.

**W6b. The room names the task each agent runs (AGY, merged as 0.1.151).** After W6a. The Tasks panel now marks a task `running AGENT`, but the room itself never says which task an agent is on. Add a pure `agentTasks(liveRuns, issues, roster)` in a new `src/model/liveTasks.js` that returns a `Map` from room agent key to the identifier of the task its running run works on (`JC-19`): find the agent by the run's `agentId` among the roster that `toAgents` (`src/live/source.js`) builds, and the task by the run's `issueId` among `client.issues()`; count only runs with status `running`, take the newest when an agent has two, skip a run whose agent or task is unknown, pass the identifier through `visibleText` and cut it to 16 characters, and return an empty map for input that is not an array. In `src/Stage.jsx`, beside the cost board effect, read `client.agents()`, `client.issues()` and `client.liveRuns()` when there is a client and again on each `agents`, `issues` and `liveRuns` notice (`subscribeToNotices`, no timer), and hand the map to a new `room.setTasks(map)` in `src/engine/room.js`, which passes it to the agents; a failed read calls `room.setTasks(null)` and the room shows no task. In `src/engine/agents.js`, give the callout a third element `.mr-callout-task` under the title that shows the agent's task identifier while the callout is shown, and nothing when the agent has none; hide it with `.mr-callout-task:empty { display: none }` in `src/styles.css`, not through `style.display` (the CSS3D renderer sets the root's display every frame; see the review lesson on V7b), and set `textContent` only when it changed, as the callout does for its command. Style it in Space Mono, weight 400, white at 0.7, on the 8px grid, and add it to the DOM contract in `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Tests in a new `tests/live-tasks.test.js` for `agentTasks`: the mapping, running beats queued, the newest of two running runs, an unknown agent and an unknown task skipped, a name with a direction control shown through `visibleText`, the 16 character cut, and input that is not an array. Check by eye in replay mode, where Stage uses the mock client (its live run is OpenCode Go on JC-19): when OpenCode works, its callout reads JC-19 under its name, and the other agents' callouts show no task line; say what you saw at 1280 by 800. Files: `src/model/liveTasks.js` (new), `tests/live-tasks.test.js` (new), `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/engine/room.js`, `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/styles.css`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the DOM contract only), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Roadmap: the assistant and the cost board

The design is in `docs/agents/CONTROL-PLANE.md` under "The assistant and the cost board". The rule engine, the action list and the model reply parser exist and are tested.

**A1. Intent rules and action list (Claude, done as 0.1.20).** `src/assistant/actions.js`, `intent.js` and `llm.js` with `tests/assistant.test.js`. Pure code, no DOM. `interpret(text, context)` returns `{ kind: action | propose | ask | unknown | blocked, action?, say, options? }` where `context` is `{ agents: [{ key, name }], paths: [...] }`. The only way to run an action is `validateAction`.

**A2. Floating assistant terminal (AGY, merged as 0.1.28).** A React component `Assistant` for the HUD, in the style of the wall terminals. Props: `context` (as above), `onAction(action)`, and `ask(text)` (optional, async, returns the same result shape as `interpret`, used for the model fallback). It takes no key prop on purpose. Behaviour: opens with `/`, closes with Escape, draggable, remembers its position in `machineroom.settings`, history with the up arrow. Calls `interpret` on enter. For `action`, show the `say` line and call `onAction`. For `propose`, show a card with the `say` text and two buttons, and call `onAction` only on confirm. For `ask`, show the `options` as buttons that send the option's `text` back through `interpret`. For `blocked`, show the `say` line and open the masked key field if the result carries an `open_config` action with section `keys`. For `unknown`, call `ask` when given, otherwise show the help line. Never echo or store typed text that `containsSecret` flags. Use the mock for any data. Do not edit `src/engine` or `src/assistant`. Add a short test for any pure helper you write.

**A3. Dispatcher (Claude, built as 0.1.31).** `src/assistant/dispatch.js` maps each validated action to the room, the HUD and the control client: camera focus on an agent or folder, wall terminal focus, follow modes, panel tabs, issue and config views, replay controls, and the three confirmed writes through `client.createIssue`, `client.setAgentBudget` and the subagent setting.

**A4. Model fallback route (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.34; the viewer does not call it yet, because it needs a setting that turns it on).** `POST /api/assistant` in the bridge, local pages only. Body `{ prompt }` with a length limit of 2000 characters. It runs `opencode run -m <model> --format json` with a timeout of 8 seconds and returns the model text. The model id comes from an env variable or the settings file and defaults to `opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash-free`. When no model is set, answer `404` with a plain message. Never log the prompt. Test it with a fake runner.

**A5. Cost board in the room (Claude; done by A5a to A5c; the board now sits in the dock).** A CSS3D panel floating near the core, fed by `client.costs()`. Per-agent spend and tokens, company budget, quota windows, and "not reported" for a harness that gives no numbers. Click an agent row to fly there. Also a plain HUD version for narrow windows.

**A6. Key form and Paperclip secrets (AGY for the form, OpenCode for the bridge route; retired: Paperclip was removed on 2026-10-24, so there are no Paperclip secrets).** A masked field per agent that sends the value only to a bridge route, which writes it to Paperclip's secrets and never returns or logs it. Needs C2. Check `PAPERCLIP-API.md` for the secrets routes first.

## Roadmap: the next plan

The order and the milestones are in `docs/agents/PLAN.md`. These are the briefs it names that are not written elsewhere in this file.

**C3b. Change notices in the panels (AGY, merged as 0.1.33).** `client.subscribe(listener)` is in the contract (`CONTROL-CLIENT.md`, "Change notices") and in the mock. In each panel, subscribe on mount and fetch again for the topics it shows (tasks: `issues`; a thread: `comments` with its issue id; approvals: `approvals`, including the pending count on the tab; meters: `agents` and `costs`; dispatch: `agents`). Unsubscribe on unmount. Keep the Refresh buttons. The mock now sends a notice after each of its own writes, so the drawer should update without a click. Test the pure parts. Second part, so the assistant can open the drawer (A3): `Hud` takes an optional prop `controlTarget`, an object `{ tab, issueIdentifier?, nonce }` where `tab` is `dispatch`, `tasks`, `approvals` or `meters`. When `nonce` changes, the drawer opens on that tab, and if `issueIdentifier` is set and an issue with that identifier is in the list, its thread opens. Do not edit `src/control`, `src/engine` or `src/assistant`.

**C3c. Contract: hire and update an agent (Claude, built as 0.1.37).** Add `hireAgent(draft)` and `updateAgent(id, patch)` to the contract and the mock, from `PAPERCLIP-API.md` (`POST /api/companies/:id/agent-hires` and the agent patch routes). A hire can wait for an approval, so it returns `{ agent, approval }`. Draft fields: name, adapter, model, reasoning effort, turn and time limits, monthly budget, subagents allowed, instructions.

The tab has a New agent form and, below it, the list of existing agents; clicking one loads it into the same form for editing, with the harness read-only. Fields: name, harness (`ADAPTER_TYPES`), role (`AGENT_ROLES`), model (the `models()` list grouped by tier, free text allowed), reasoning effort (`EFFORTS`), turn limit, time limit in seconds, monthly budget (the person types dollars with up to two decimals, the client takes whole cents), subagents allowed with a depth (the range in `LIMITS.subagentDepth`), instructions, and who it reports to (a select of `client.agents()`). Editing calls `updateAgent` with only the changed fields, and the Save button stays off when nothing changed. By-eye checks are part of the task: clear `machineroom.settings` first, start `npm run dev -- --port 5181`, open `/?replay` at 1280x800 and 1024x768, hire an agent that needs approval and watch the Approvals badge go up, approve it in the Approvals tab and see the agent become ready in the list, enter a turn limit of 0 and read the sentence under the field, edit one agent and change a single field, then stop the server. Health fields and the unreadable counter (OpenCode for the bridge, AGY for the chip; done by U2a to U2d).** `/api/health` also returns `paperclipVersion`, `surface` (ok, or the routes that changed) and `unparsed` (chunks from a known adapter that no parser could read). **U2b. Health chip in the HUD (AGY, merged as 0.1.83).** The bridge's `GET /api/health` answers `{ ok, paperclip, paperclipVersion, surface }` (U2a; see Live mode in `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`). Show a small chip in the HUD, under the Replay and Live switch, when something is wrong, and nothing when all is well. Put the reading in a pure helper `src/ui/healthNotice.js` exporting `healthNotice(health)`, which returns null or `{ tone, title, lines }`. When `health` is `{ error: true }` (the fetch failed): tone `red`, title `The bridge is not answering`, no lines. When `paperclip` is false: tone `red`, title `Paperclip is not connected`. When `surface.ok` is false: tone `red`, title `Paperclip changed: N routes` (`1 route` for one), where N counts the missing and changed routes, and one line per missing route (`GET /api/companies/{companyId}/issues is gone`) and per changed route (`PATCH /api/agents/{agentId}: no budgetMonthlyCents, status`, answer and body fields together). When `surface.ok` is null and `surface.checkedAt` is set: tone `yellow`, title `Paperclip routes not checked`, and the error as the one line. Anything else is null, including `not checked yet` before the first check, a healthy answer and junk input. A non-empty `paperclipVersion` is added to a red or yellow title as ` (Paperclip VERSION)`. Add `src/ui/useBridgeHealth.js` exporting a hook `useBridgeHealth(bridge, { everyMs = 60000, fetchImpl = fetch } = {})` that returns null when `bridge` is empty, and otherwise fetches `${bridge}/api/health` at once and every `everyMs`, returning the parsed answer or `{ error: true }` on a failed fetch, a bad status or a body that is not JSON, and clears its timer on unmount or when `bridge` changes. `Hud` takes a new prop `health` (default null) and draws the chip from `healthNotice(health)`: the title on one line, a click opens a small list of the lines with a Close button, and `Escape` closes it. Red border and text for red, `#e5ff00` for yellow, Space Mono 11px, 8px spacing, never bold. Do not touch `src/Stage.jsx` or `src/App.jsx`: Claude calls the hook there with the bridge address and passes `health` to the HUD. Tests in a new `tests/health-notice.test.js`: each case above, the plural, the version in the title, junk input, and the HUD rendered with `renderToString` showing the chip for a changed surface and no chip for a healthy answer. Check by eye at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768 by passing a test `health` value for a moment, and say in the report that you did, or that you could not. Files: `src/ui/healthNotice.js`, `src/ui/useBridgeHealth.js`, `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, `tests/health-notice.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U2a. Paperclip version and route check in the health answer (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.64).** The bridge checks the routes it uses against the running Paperclip, with `compareSurface` from `server/surface.js` (U1), and says so in `GET /api/health`. When the bridge starts, each time its Paperclip socket opens after being closed, and every 15 minutes (`createBridge({ surfaceEveryMs })` makes that shorter for a test; the timer is cleared by `bridge.stop()`), it fetches `${paperclipUrl}/api/openapi.json` with a 10 second timeout and keeps the result. `/api/health` answers `{ ok, paperclip, paperclipVersion, surface }` where `paperclipVersion` is the spec's `info.version` or null, and `surface` is `{ ok: true, checkedAt }`, `{ ok: false, checkedAt, missing, changed }` (the arrays `compareSurface` returns, method and path and field names only) or `{ ok: null, checkedAt, error }` with a plain sentence when the spec could not be read. Before the first check finishes, `surface` is `{ ok: null, checkedAt: null, error: 'not checked yet' }`. Only one check runs at a time, and a failed fetch never throws out of the timer. Keep the old fields as they are, so the HUD's probe keeps working. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js` with a fake Paperclip: a matching spec, a spec that drops a route, a spec answer that is not JSON, Paperclip down, the check running again after the socket reopens, and no timer left after `stop()`. Add the new fields to the health section of `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md` if it lists them, and one line to the changelog. **P5. Recover cut and redacted tool lines (Hermes, merged as 0.1.38).** Every OpenCode Go run in `public/fixtures/scenario-*-run.jsonl` loses one to three tool calls. Paperclip's `***REDACTED***` masking breaks the stored JSON line (search the files for `REDACTED`), so the line no longer parses and the parser drops the whole call. First find out exactly what the broken lines look like and describe the shapes in your report. Then, in `src/model/parsers/opencode.js` (and `common.js` only if you must), when a line does not parse as JSON but is clearly a tool call, recover the tool name and the path or the command with a narrow pattern, and build the same call the parser builds for a good line. Never guess: if the pattern does not find both, drop the call as today. Also map the Hermes `grep` line to the `search` kind in `src/model/parsers/hermes.js`. Tests: a unit test for each broken shape you recover, and update the scenario tests in `tests/scenarios.test.js` that now see more calls (explain each changed line in your report). Files: `src/model/parsers/opencode.js`, `src/model/parsers/hermes.js`, `src/model/parsers/common.js`, `tests/parsers.test.js`, `tests/scenarios.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P6. Message text is not a file touch (Hermes, merged as 0.1.32).** `git commit -m "..."`, `curl -d '...'` and similar must not touch files that are named only inside the message. A quoted path that is an argument (`cat "src/math.js"`) still counts. The change goes in `touchesForCommand` in `src/model/commands.js`: before the command is split into segments, blank out the value of the message flags (`-m`, `--message`, `-d`, `--data`, `--data-raw`, `--data-binary`, with a space or `=` before the value) when the value is a double or single quoted string, and when the quote is never closed (the stored command labels are cut with a `~`) blank to the end of the text. Blanking first also stops a `;` or `&&` inside a message from splitting the command. Three tests currently pin the wrong behaviour and must change to the right one: `tests/commands.test.js` (the `git commit -m "rename docs/notes.md to docs/readme.md"` case) and the two rename commit lines in `tests/scenarios.test.js`. Add tests for each flag, for a cut message, for a message holding `;`, and for `cat "src/math.js"` still counting. Files: `src/model/commands.js`, `tests/commands.test.js`, `tests/scenarios.test.js`, nothing else.

**P7. A here-string body is not a file touch (Hermes, merged as 0.1.41).** In the recorded runs, PowerShell commands like `$comment = @' ## JC-17 complete ... src/strings.js ... '@` post a report whose text names files. The parser reads those names as touches, so the room shows reads that never happened (see `tests/scenarios.test.js`, the lines starting `shell | $ $comment = @'`, `$body = @"` for JC-19 and `$body = @"` for the rename). The change goes in `touchesForCommand` in `src/model/commands.js`, in the same place and style as `withoutMessageValues` from P6. Before the command is split into segments, blank out the body of a PowerShell here-string: from `@'` or `@"` up to the matching closing `'@` or `"@` (the closing mark may follow a newline or, because stored labels are flattened to one line, a space), and to the end of the text when the closing mark never comes (labels are cut with a `~`). Text after the closing mark still counts, so `@' ... '@ | Set-Content docs/report.md` still touches `docs/report.md` as a write. A single quoted `'@'` or an email address such as `me@site.com` is not a here-string. Three pinned lines in `tests/scenarios.test.js` currently expect the wrong touches and must change to the right ones: explain each changed line in your report (which touches went away and why that is correct). Add unit tests in `tests/commands.test.js` for: a closed single quote here-string, a closed double quote one, an unclosed one, a file named after the closing mark, an email address, and a command with no here-string that is unchanged. Files: `src/model/commands.js`, `tests/commands.test.js`, `tests/scenarios.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`, nothing else.

**W3a. Read the lane branches from the bridge (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.52).** The first, read-only part of W3: the owner should see what each lane did without opening a terminal. Add two bridge routes in a new `server/reviews.js`. `GET /api/control/reviews` answers `{ lanes: [...] }` with one entry per own key of `docs/agents/lanes.json` that has a `worktree`: `{ lane, worktree, branch, ahead, commits, dirty, files, added, deleted, lastRun }`. `branch` is `git branch --show-current` in that worktree, `commits` the lines of `git log --oneline main..HEAD` (at most 50) and `ahead` their count, `dirty` whether `git status --porcelain` prints anything, `files`, `added` and `deleted` the totals of `git diff --numstat main...HEAD` (a binary file counts as a file with 0 and 0), and `lastRun` the newest record for that lane from `runs.jsonl` (reuse `readRunRecords` and `runsFile` from `server/runs.js`), or null. A worktree that is missing or is not a git repository gives `{ lane, worktree, error }` with a plain sentence, and the other lanes still answer. `GET /api/control/reviews/:lane/diff` answers `{ lane, branch, files: [{ path, added, deleted }], patch, truncated }`, where `patch` is `git diff main...HEAD` cut at 400 KB (`truncated` true when cut; let `createBridge` take an option for the limit so a test can make it small). The lane must be an own key with a `worktree` (`Object.hasOwn`); decode it inside a try block and answer `404` with a plain sentence for a bad one. Rules: run git with an argument list, `shell: false`, `windowsHide`, a 10 second timeout and a large enough buffer; the folder is always the lane's `worktree` from `lanes.json`, never anything from the request; `main` is the base. No route in this task writes anything: no merge, checkout, fetch, reset or commit. Merging comes later as W3b, with a confirm step. Client: `reviews()` and `reviewDiff(lane)` in `src/control/client.js`; add both names to `CLIENT_METHODS` in `src/control/mock.js` with a matching mock pair (sample lanes with commits and a short sample patch, the same refusals). Add the two methods to the method table and `Review`, `ReviewDiff` to the shapes in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`. Tests in a new `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js` with a throwaway repository in a temp folder: `git init`, a commit on `main`, `git worktree add -b feat/x <folder>`, a commit there, and a `lanes.json` that points at it. Cover: the list with ahead, commits, totals and the joined run record; a dirty worktree; a missing worktree; a lane named `__proto__`, `constructor`, `..%2F..` and malformed percent encoding; the cut patch. Add the shared cases to `tests/control-contract.test.js` so both clients answer the same. **T8. Replay a lane run in the room (Hermes, merged as 0.1.50).** A run started with `npm run run-lane` never passes through Paperclip, so the room cannot show it. Turn a finished run into a recording that the replay loads like `public/fixtures/scenario-a-*`. In a new pure module `src/briefs/recording.js`: `framesFromRun({ harness, log, session, record })` returns frames shaped like the ones in `public/fixtures/scenario-a-run.jsonl`: one `heartbeat.run.queued`, one `heartbeat.run.status` with `running`, one `heartbeat.run.log` per line the agent's parser reads, and a last `heartbeat.run.status` with `succeeded` or `failed` (from the record's `exitCode` and `killed`). For OpenCode the chunk is the raw line of the `opencode run --format json` log and its time is the line's own `timestamp`. For Hermes the chunk is the `┊` line that `sessionToolLines` builds and its time is the tool result's `timestamp` from the session export (`hermes sessions export --session-id <id> --format jsonl -`; the id is `session_id` in the run's `.usage.json` next to its log). Frame ids count up from 1, times are ISO, frames are in time order, and the run, agent, issue and company ids are made-up fixed strings built from the lane and the start time (no randomness, no real Paperclip ids). `agentsFile(lane, lanes)` returns `{ projectRoot, agents: [{ id, key, name, adapterType, parser }] }` with the same agent id the frames use, and `treeFile(root, files)` returns `{ root, files }`, where the root is the base name of the lane's worktree. Check the exact shapes against `prepare` in `src/model/source.js` and the scenario files. A test must run `prepare` on what these functions return and check the activities: the tool starts, their file paths relative to the root, and the run end. Script `scripts/run-recording.mjs` with `npm run run-recording -- <n>`, where `n` is the position in `npm run run-lane -- --list` (1 is the newest). It reads the record, the log and, for Hermes, the usage file and the session export (same binary as the lane, no shell, a timeout), takes the file list from `git ls-files` in the lane's worktree at the record's last commit (the first word of its first `commits` entry, or the branch when there is none), and writes `public/recordings/<log name>-run.jsonl`, `-agents.json` and `-tree.json`. Add `public/recordings/` to `.gitignore`, because these files hold real commands. Print the three paths, the frame count and the tool call count. A refusal (no such record, the log is gone, the session cannot be exported) is a plain sentence and exit code 1. Also: `src/model/parsers/hermes.js` reads ` [error]` at the end of a tool line as a failed call, the way it reads ` [exit N]`, because `sessionToolLines` writes it. No agent runs and no paid calls. Test with fixtures: a few OpenCode lines (take them from the chunks in `public/fixtures/scenario-a-run.jsonl`), and a small hand-made Hermes session export `tests/fixtures/hermes-session.jsonl` with two tool calls, their results and timestamps, and no prompt text or personal paths. You may run the script once on the newest Hermes record to check it by hand; do not commit what it writes. Claude adds a `?recording=<name>` address to the replay afterwards, so do not touch `src/App.jsx` or `src/model/replay.js`. **T7. Run a brief from the dispatch panel (AGY, merged as 0.1.53).** Starts after T5 is merged: the bridge can then start Hermes or OpenCode on a brief, and both clients have `runs()` (answers `{ running, recent }`) and `startRun(lane, briefs)`, with a `runs` change notice. In `DispatchPanel`, when `client.startRun` is a function and the chosen brief has a lane, add a Run now button beside Copy prompt. It opens a confirm card inside the panel with the lane name, the model (from the `briefPrompt` result), the brief id, and the sentence "This starts the agent on this computer and spends money." Confirm calls `client.startRun(lane, [id])`; Cancel closes the card. While the call runs the button reads Starting and is disabled. Nothing in the panel starts a run without the confirm card. Under the brief section, when `client.runs` is a function, add a Runs list. Running entries first: lane, brief ids, how long ago it started, and its `lastLines` in Space Mono, dim. Then the recent records: lane, brief ids, the result (finished when `exitCode` is 0, stopped when `killed`, failed otherwise), the cost from `costUsd` ("cost unknown" when it is null), the minutes, and the start time. **P3b. Wire the collapse plan into the room (Claude, replaced by V3).** Use `planCollapse` from `src/model/collapse.js` (P3a) in the engine. The hot set is every folder an agent has touched in this session plus the folders on the way to each touched file. Collapsed folders draw as one tile with a count of folders and files and no file planes. When an agent touches a file inside a collapsed folder, the plan is made again and the layout warm starts (existing folders keep their place). Lay out only the tiles in the plan. Check by eye with a synthetic tree of 5,000 folders and with the recorded replay (which fits and must look the same as before). Files: `src/engine/layout.js`, `src/engine/clusters.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, tests for any new pure helper, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Room behaviour and Known limits), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P3a. Folder collapse plan (Hermes, merged as 0.1.42).** The first part of P3. A large repository has thousands of folders and the layout compares every pair, so the room must show at most a few hundred folder tiles and fold the rest. Write a pure function `planCollapse({ folders, fileCounts, hot, maxTiles })` in `src/model/collapse.js`. `folders` is a list of folder paths relative to the project root (`''` is the root, then `'src'`, `'src/model'`), in any order; add every missing ancestor before you start, so `['src/model']` alone is the tree `''`, `'src'`, `'src/model'`. `fileCounts` is a `Map` from folder path to the number of files directly in that folder (a missing entry means 0). `hot` is a list of folder paths that must stay open, because an agent has touched them; a hot path that is not in the tree is ignored. `maxTiles` defaults to 400. It returns `{ visible, collapsed, overBudget }`. `visible` is the sorted list of open folders. It always holds the root, every hot folder and every ancestor of a hot folder. `collapsed` is the sorted list of `{ path, folders, files }`, one for each folder that is not open but whose parent is open; `folders` counts the folders in its whole subtree including itself, and `files` counts the files in that subtree. A tile is an open folder or a collapsed one, and `visible.length + collapsed.length` must not pass `maxTiles` unless `overBudget` is true. `overBudget` is true only when the root, the hot folders and their ancestors, with their collapsed children, already need more tiles than `maxTiles`. Fill the open set in this order: the root, then each hot folder with its ancestors, then the rest breadth first (by depth, then by path in plain string order). Open a folder only when the tile count after opening it stays within the budget (it stops being a collapsed tile and each of its children becomes one). When the whole tree fits, every folder is open and `collapsed` is empty. The same input in any order gives the same output. Tests in `tests/collapse.test.js`: a small tree that fits comes back whole; a tree over the budget opens the shallow folders first and collapses the deeper ones with the right counts; a hot folder deep in the tree stays open with its whole chain; the output does not depend on the input order; a hot set too large for the budget gives `overBudget: true` with all of it open; a missing parent is added; and 10,000 folders finish in under 200 ms (time it with `performance.now()` and keep the bound loose so CI on a slow machine passes). Do not wire it into the room; Claude does that in the engine. Files: `src/model/collapse.js`, `tests/collapse.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`, nothing else.

**P3c. Large project pass (Claude, in the cloud, merged as 0.1.139).** The room has only run on small projects. Measure each stage on a large one and fix what is too slow, before daily use on large repositories. Add `scripts/scale-probe.mjs` (`npm run scale-probe`, with `--files N`, `--folders N` and `--seed N`; by default 40000 files in 4000 folders, up to 12 deep, names from a fixed seed) that builds the tree in memory and prints the time of each stage and the size of each payload: `readTree` in `server/index.js` with a fake `listDir`, the size in bytes of the `tree` event it would send, `makeTree` in `src/model/paths.js`, `planFolderView` with 3 working agents, `layoutCards` on the cards that plan draws, `state.js` applying 5000 activities over the tree, and `planCollapse`. Then fix each stage that takes more than 16 ms where it runs per activity or per frame, or more than 1 second where it runs once at load, and a `tree` event over 2 MB. A fix must not change what the room draws for the existing data: replay every `public/recordings/*-run.jsonl` and each fixture before and after, and compare the activities and the folder plans. No new dependency. Tests in a new `tests/scale.test.js`: the same seed gives the same tree, and each fixed stage stays under a loose bound (10 times the time you measured, so a slow CI passes). Files: `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `package.json` (the script line only), `tests/scale.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`, and the modules a fix needs in `src/model/**`, `src/engine/layout.js` and `server/index.js`; name each one in the report.
