## Roadmap: the decision model

Design and the model comparison are in `docs/agents/CONTROL-PLANE.md` under "The decision model".

**D1. Probe set, seam and comparison run (Claude, built as 0.1.27, waiting for the owner to run it).** How to run it and read it: `docs/agents/DECIDER-EVAL.md`. Built: `src/decider/` holds the request builder, the answer parser and `decide(question set, state)` with an injected `fetch`, a timeout and a fallback to the rules. `scripts/decide-eval.mjs` runs every listed model id on the probe sets and prints, per model and per question: accuracy, coverage at 5 percent accepted-case error, calibration error, median latency, cost and the share of answers that change on a repeat. Baselines in the same table: our rules (`interpret`) and a small language model with structured output. Probe sets in `tests/probes/`: the assistant phrases from `tests/assistant.test.js`, and about 40 coding tasks with tier, splits and consequence labels that the owner reviews. Tests use a fake Decisions server. Running it needs `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` in the environment of whoever runs it (cents of spend). The key is never written to a file or printed.

**D2. Decide route in the bridge (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.78).** `POST /api/decide`, local pages only, like every bridge route. Body `{ state, questions, model? }` with a 32 KB limit (answer 413 in plain words above it): `state` is a string or an object, `questions` an object with at least one question (answer 400 otherwise). Call `decide()` from `src/decider/decide.js`, which already does the 3 second timeout, the one retry and the key masking; do not write a second client. The key comes from `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` in the bridge's environment (`createBridge({ env })` already takes one for tests) and the model from the body, else `DECISION_MODEL`, else `typesafe/jev-1.14`; never hard code a model anywhere else. `createBridge({ decideUrl })` points `decide()` at a fake server in tests. Answers: a good call gives 200 `{ ok: true, model, answers, usage, ms, attempts }`; no key gives 404 `No key is set for the decision model.`; a `rate_limited` failure gives 429; any other failure gives 502 with the plain message `decide()` returns, never the raw upstream body. Log one line per call with only the model id, the question names, the result kind and the milliseconds: never the state, the answers, the probabilities or the key. Add `decide({ state, questions, model })` to `src/control/client.js`. Do not touch `src/control/mock.js`, `src/decider/**` or `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`: Claude adds the mock and the contract. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js` with a fake Decisions server and a fake key: a good call, the model from the body, from `DECISION_MODEL` and the default, no key, a body over 32 KB, no questions, a 429, a 500 after its retry, a timeout, the key never appearing in any answer or log line (capture the log), and the client method against the bridge. Never call the real Decisions API. Files: `server/index.js` or a new `server/decide.js`, `src/control/client.js`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**D3. Assistant fallback through the decider (Claude; retired: the app does not use the decider; the rules and the orchestrator cover it).** After D1 shows it matches the rules on the assistant probe set: choice over action types, agent keys and up to 20 candidate paths, through D2. It replaces the text model for A4, which stays as the second choice.

**D4. Routing policy uses the answers (Claude; retired: the routing policy, version 1, is C4; the decider is not used).** C4 takes `tier`, `harness`, `coordination`, `uncertainty`, `consequence`, `splits` and `effort`, applies the floors and the three bands from the design, and returns the dispatch settings (model, effort, limits, subagents allowed, approval needed). Unit tests with canned answers. No network in the tests.

**D5. Queue proposals (Claude, AGY; retired: Paperclip routines were removed on 2026-10-24).** `shape` turns a typed request into a routine proposal card in the assistant and the drawer. Needs the routines routes in `PAPERCLIP-API.md` (check them first), C2 and D2.

## Roadmap: the room, version 2

The owner's redesign, 2026-10-19. The room today reads as a test board seen from above: folder tiles with every file plane drawn, a sphere in the middle that stands for Paperclip, plain solids for agents, and a fixed terminal wall. Version 2 makes it a 3D space: a black void with a very faint grid, folders that look like folders and open only along the path an agent works on, a ring in the middle where agents park, a distinct complex shape for every agent with no main one at the center, terminals that show only when asked, and the lens look of the owner's portfolio site. The same rules stay: one meaning per colour, no randomness, the same recording always draws the same room.

**V1. Folder view plan (Hermes, merged as 0.1.54).** A pure function `planFolderView({ files, focus, touched, maxFolders = 3, maxItems = 10, backLayers = 3 })` in a new `src/model/folderView.js`. `files` lists the project files relative to the root, `focus` the files or folders agents touch right now, and `touched` everything touched earlier in the session. It returns the folders to draw, each `{ path, state, folders, back, hiddenFolders, items, hiddenItems }`. Rules: the root is always drawn; every folder on the way from the root to a focus path is `open`; any other drawn folder is `closed`. An open folder shows at most `maxFolders` child folders (`folders`): the ones on a focus path first, then ones touched earlier, then by name. The child folders it does not show are counted in `hiddenFolders` and drawn as `back` faint folders stacked behind it, at most `backLayers`. The folder that holds a focused file has state `files` instead and lists at most `maxItems` items (`{ path, kind }`, kind `file` or `dir`), of which at most `maxFolders` are folders; the focused file is always listed, then touched files, then by name, and `hiddenItems` counts the rest. Paths are cleaned like `src/model/paths.js` does. No randomness. Tests in `tests/folder-view.test.js`: a deep path, a folder with 100 child folders (3 shown, 3 back, 97 hidden), a folder with 40 files, two agents on two branches, a focus on a folder, and the root alone. Files: `src/model/folderView.js`, `tests/folder-view.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V2. The void (Claude, merged as 0.1.55).** Black space with a very faint grid that recedes into the distance and fog for depth, so the room reads as 3D and not as a board seen from above. The default camera looks across the room at a low angle, and folders, agents and the hub sit at different depths and heights. The look follows the owner's portfolio void (read once by Claude with the owner's leave, 2026-10-20; agents must not open the graded folder, and no code is copied from it): no lens shader, but a cubic 3D lattice in all three axes at about 3.5% opacity with brighter tick crosses at the grid points and a few faint coordinate labels, a far shell of glowing dust, a field of tiny folder and square sprites far out, a camera that drifts a little with the pointer (eased, off under reduced motion), drag to look around that springs back to the target, and eased flights between places. Field of view about 50 degrees. Files: `src/engine/room.js`, `src/engine/camera.js`, a new `src/engine/void.js`.

**V3. Folders as folders (Claude, merged as 0.1.55).** Done as cards, after the owner's sketch: one shared space, upright folder cards that face the camera, laid out around the hub (root above, folders left and right, deeper folders in columns further out and nearer the camera), with rows for files that can be clicked. A folder draws as a flat folder outline with a tab and its name, closed until an agent works inside it. It opens along the path the agent takes, using V1: an open folder shows up to 3 child folders and, behind it, up to 3 faint folder outlines for the ones it does not show, so a folder with 100 children still draws 6 shapes. The folder that holds the file being touched always opens to its files and lists up to 10 items, at most 3 of them folders. Folders close again a while after the work in them ends. This replaces P3b; `planCollapse` stays as the budget for very large trees. Files: `src/engine/clusters.js`, `src/engine/layout.js`, `src/engine/strings.js`, `src/engine/room.js`.

**V4. Agents and the hub ring (Claude, merged as 0.1.55).** Done: the ring has radius 300, agents park closer on it, and a working agent stands on the hub side of its card with its chip between them and a dashed trace to the file (yellow active, green finished, red failure). The delegation flight waits for S1. The sphere in the middle goes. In its place a ring path is the hub: an agent with no run parks on the ring at its own slot (from the roster order), leaves it to work, and returns when its run ends. Every agent gets a distinct complex wireframe shape built from its key with no randomness (for example torus knots with different windings, compound or stellated solids, nested shells), all about the same size, none at the center. The orchestrator is one of the agents, not the center: the agent marked as orchestrator gets its own shape like the others and parks on the ring; when it hands a task to another agent it flies to that agent (needs the delegation signal from S1; until then it moves like the others). The orchestrator is Paperclip's Orchestrator agent (role `ceo`, the owner's choice on 2026-10-20). Files: `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/engine/core.js` (removed or turned into the ring), `src/engine/room.js`, `src/engine/route.js`.

**V5. Terminals on demand (Claude for the engine, merged as 0.1.55; AGY for the button, next).** The engine part is done as chips: each agent has a small chip with its name and a status ring beside it, `T` opens the focused agent's chip to show the log (or every chip with no agent focused), and the room offers `toggleTerminal(key)`, `openTerminals(open)` and `openTerminalKeys()`. Left for AGY as **V5b**: a Log button on each agent row, a Show all button, and a `T` line in the help list. No fixed terminal wall. Each terminal is hidden until asked: a button on each agent in the agent list and the `T` key show it next to its agent, following it as it moves and facing the camera, and a Show all button shows every terminal. The DOM contract for terminals in ARCHITECTURE.md keeps its classes. Files: `src/engine/terminals.js`, `src/engine/room.js` (Claude); `src/ui/**` and `src/styles.css` for the buttons (AGY, after T7).

**V5b. Terminal buttons in the HUD (AGY, merged as 0.1.67).** `Stage.jsx` now passes three props to `Hud`: `openTerminals` (the keys of the agents whose chip is open), `onToggleTerminal(key)` and `onShowTerminals(open)`. In the agent list, give each agent a small Log button beside its row. A button cannot hold a button, so wrap the row button and the Log button in one element and keep the row's click, keyboard and `aria-pressed` behaviour as it is. The Log button calls `onToggleTerminal(agent.key)`, takes `aria-pressed` from `openTerminals.includes(agent.key)`, and is named for screen readers as Show the log of NAME or Hide the log of NAME. Beside Overview, add a Show all button that calls `onShowTerminals(true)`, and that reads Hide all and calls `onShowTerminals(false)` when every agent's chip is open. Add `['T', 'Show or hide the log of the selected agent, or of every agent']` to the shortcut list after F. Keep the row height and the 8px spacing; Space Mono for the button labels; a white border, yellow when pressed. If any logic grows (for example the Show all label from the open keys and the agent list), put it in a pure helper with a test in `tests/`. Check by eye with `/?replay` at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768: press a Log button and see that agent's chip open beside it in the room, press Show all and see every chip open, press T with an agent selected. Files: `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, a helper and its test if needed, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V6. Folders close after the work ends (Hermes, merged as 0.1.59).** A folder card in the room stays open while its path is among the 8 most recent touches (`state.recent` in `src/model/state.js`), however long ago they were. Keep the time of each touch: `state.latest` is the largest activity `time` applied so far (0 in a new state), and `state.recentAt` is a Map from each path in `state.recent` to the `time` of the activity that last touched it, kept in step with `state.recent` (a path that drops out of `recent` drops out of the map, and a repeat touch updates its time). Export `RECENT_KEEP_MS = 90000` and `freshRecent(state, keepMs = RECENT_KEEP_MS)`, which returns the paths of `state.recent` in the same order (newest last) whose last touch is at most `keepMs` before `state.latest`. Use activity times, not `state.now`: the players set `state.now` in their own clocks, and a seek applies every activity again from a new state, so activity times give the same answer either way. Do not touch `src/engine/**`; Claude wires `freshRecent` into the cards. Tests in `tests/state-focus.test.js`: an old touch drops out once a later activity moves `state.latest` past it, a fresh one stays, a repeat touch refreshes its time, the map holds exactly the paths of `recent`, `keepMs` 0 keeps only the touches at `state.latest`, and a new state starts with `latest` 0 and an empty map. Files: `src/model/state.js`, `tests/state-focus.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P8. Resolve a bare Hermes file name from the agent's last folder (Hermes, merged as 0.1.60).** Hermes read lines carry only a file name (`┊ 📖 read  strings.js  1.2s`). `resolveKnown(name, files)` in `src/model/paths.js` returns the one known file with that name, but when two files share it (`src/index.js` and `test/index.js`) it gives up and returns the bare name, which lands at the root. Add an optional third argument `near`, a folder path (`''` is the root). When several files match and `near` is given: pick the one whose folder is `near`; if none is, pick the match that shares the longest leading folder path with `near`, counted in whole folder names (`src/ui` shares one folder with `src/engine`, none with `srcx`); if that is still a tie, keep today's answer, the bare name. With one match or no `near`, nothing changes. In `src/model/parsers/hermes.js`, remember the folder of the last read, write or edit whose path is a known file or a path with a folder, and pass it as `near` on the next read, write or edit. Keep it per parser (one parser per agent run). Do not change the other parsers. Tests: in `tests/paths.test.js`, one match, two matches with `near` on one of them, a match by the longest shared folder, a tie that keeps the bare name, and no `near` keeps today's behaviour; in `tests/parsers.test.js`, a Hermes read of `src/a.js` then a bare `index.js` read resolves to `src/index.js` when `test/index.js` also exists, and a first bare read with no folder yet keeps the bare name. Files: `src/model/paths.js`, `src/model/parsers/hermes.js`, `tests/paths.test.js`, `tests/parsers.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**T11. An index of the recordings (Hermes, merged as 0.1.62).** Every lane run now writes a recording under `public/recordings/`, but the app can only open one by its exact name, because a static server lists no folder. Keep `public/recordings/index.json`: an array of entries, newest first, at most 50, each `{ name, lane, briefs, model, start, minutes, exitCode, frames, toolCalls }` taken from the run record and the counts `writeRecording` already has. Add a pure `recordingIndex(entries, entry, limit = 50)` to `src/briefs/recording.js` that returns the new array: the entry first, any older entry with the same `name` removed, anything that is not an object with a plain `name` (the `RECORDING_NAME` pattern in `src/startup.js`) dropped, cut to `limit`. In `writeRecording` (`scripts/recording-files.mjs`), after the three files are written, read the index (a missing or unreadable file counts as empty), drop entries whose `-run.jsonl` file is gone, add the new entry with `recordingIndex`, and write it to a temporary file in the same folder and rename it over `index.json`, so a reader never sees half a file. A failure to write the index must not fail the recording: return it as `indexError` (a plain sentence) and let `run-lane.mjs` print `index: not updated (<the sentence>)`. `npm run run-recording` updates the index too. Tests: `recordingIndex` in `tests/run-recording.test.js` (newest first, a repeat name replaced, the limit, junk entries and names like `../x` dropped) and `writeRecording` with a temp folder (the index is created, a second recording goes first, an entry whose run file is gone is dropped, an unreadable index is replaced). Files: `src/briefs/recording.js`, `scripts/recording-files.mjs`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `tests/run-recording.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**T12. Read the recordings index in the app (Hermes, merged as 0.1.63).** T11 keeps `public/recordings/index.json`. Give the app a safe way to read it, so a later HUD brief can offer a list of past runs. In `src/model/replay.js`, add a pure `parseRecordingIndex(raw)` that takes the parsed JSON and returns a clean array: only objects whose `name` matches `RECORDING_NAME` from `src/startup.js`, each as `{ name, lane, briefs, model, start, minutes, exitCode, frames, toolCalls }` with strings as strings (empty when missing or not a string), `briefs` as an array of strings, numbers as finite numbers or null, no other keys, no repeated names (the first one wins), at most 50, in the order given. Anything that is not an array gives `[]`. Add `loadRecordingIndex({ base = import.meta.env.BASE_URL, fetchImpl = fetch } = {})`, which fetches `${base}recordings/index.json` and returns `parseRecordingIndex` of it, `[]` when the file is missing (a 404), and throws a plain sentence (`The list of recordings could not be read.`) for another failed answer or a body that is not JSON. `import.meta.env` has no value under vitest's node environment, so read it in a way that does not throw there and let the tests pass `base`. Do not touch `src/App.jsx`, `src/ui/**` or `src/engine/**`. Tests in a new `tests/replay.test.js`: junk entries and names like `../x` dropped, a repeated name kept once, the cut at 50, wrong types cleaned, a non-array, a 404, a 500, a body that is not JSON, and `replayFiles` with and without a recording. Files: `src/model/replay.js`, `tests/replay.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P9. Lines that look like tool calls but no parser read (Hermes, merged as 0.1.65).** U2 wants a count of agent output the room could not read, but most output is plain text that holds no tool call and must not count. Count only lines shaped like a tool call for that parser that the parser still could not turn into one. Add `src/model/parsers/unreadable.js` exporting `unreadableLines(parser, text)`, where `parser` is `claude`, `hermes`, `opencode` or `agy` and `text` is one log chunk; it returns the lines (trimmed of the line break) that count, and `[]` for any other parser name. A line counts when: for `hermes`, it starts with optional spaces and `┊` but does not match the line pattern in `src/model/parsers/hermes.js` (export that pattern from there and use it, do not copy it); for `claude`, it contains `acpx.tool_call` but `parseJson` from `common.js` gives null or an object whose `type` is not `acpx.tool_call` or that has no `toolCallId`; for `opencode`, it matches `"type"\s*:\s*"tool_use"` but `parseJson` gives null or an object with no `part.tool`, unless the OpenCode parser's own recovery for a masked line would read it (export `recoverCall` from `opencode.js` and treat a line it returns a call for as read); for `agy`, it contains `step_update` but `parseJson` gives null or an object whose `event` is not `step_update`. Plain text, blank lines and well formed tool lines never count. Do not wire it into the bridge or the pipeline (OpenCode does that in a later task). Tests in a new `tests/unreadable.test.js`, built from real line shapes in the existing parser tests and fixtures: one well formed and one broken line per parser, plain text that never counts, a Hermes `┊` line with a changed shape, an OpenCode line cut in the middle of its JSON, an OpenCode masked line the recovery reads (does not count), an unknown parser name, and a chunk with several lines where only the broken ones come back. Files: `src/model/parsers/unreadable.js`, `src/model/parsers/hermes.js` and `src/model/parsers/opencode.js` (exports only), `tests/unreadable.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P10. Count unreadable lines across chunks (Hermes, merged as 0.1.66).** A tool line can be split over two log chunks, and `unreadableLines` (P9) on one chunk would count each half. In `src/model/parsers/unreadable.js`, add `createUnreadableCounter({ tailLimit = 65536 } = {})` that returns `{ push(parser, runId, chunk), end(runId), counts(), reset() }`. `push` puts the kept tail of that run in front of the chunk, counts the complete lines (each one followed by a line break) with `unreadableLines`, and keeps the text after the last line break as the new tail of that run. A tail longer than `tailLimit` characters is dropped without counting. `end(runId)` counts the kept tail of that run as one last line and forgets the run. `counts()` returns a fresh `{ total, byParser: { claude, hermes, opencode, agy } }` with every number present (0 when nothing counted). A parser name that `unreadableLines` does not know counts nothing and keeps no tail. `reset()` clears everything. Runs are independent, and at most 50 runs keep a tail: a new run beyond that drops the oldest tail without counting it. Tests in `tests/unreadable.test.js`: a broken line split over two chunks counts once, a well formed OpenCode line split over three chunks counts zero, `end` counting a broken tail and then forgetting the run, two runs interleaved, the tail limit, the cap of 50 runs, an unknown parser, `\r\n` line breaks, `counts()` giving a copy, and the sandbox fixture (`public/fixtures/sandbox-run.jsonl`, with `public/fixtures/agents.json` mapping each frame's `payload.agentId` to its `parser`, ending each run after its last chunk) giving a total of 0. Files: `src/model/parsers/unreadable.js`, `tests/unreadable.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**A5a. Cost board rows (Hermes, merged as 0.1.68).** The first step of A5: a pure join of the control client's `Costs` (see `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`) with the room's agents, for the meters panel and the room board to draw. Add `src/model/costs.js` exporting `costBoard(costs, roster)`, where `roster` is the room's agent list, an array of `{ id, key, name }` in roster order. It returns `{ summary, rows, quota }`. `summary` is `{ spendCents, budgetCents, percent, over }`: the numbers from `costs.summary` (0 when missing or not a finite number), `percent` is `utilizationPercent` when it is finite, else spend over budget times 100 rounded to one decimal when the budget is above 0, else null, and `over` is true when the budget is above 0 and the spend is above it. `rows` has one row per roster agent in roster order, matched to `costs.byAgent` by `agentId === id`, then each `byAgent` row with no roster agent, by `costCents` high to low, then by name. Each row is `{ key, agentId, name, costCents, inputTokens, outputTokens, reported, share }`: `key` is the roster key or null, `name` the roster name, else `agentName`, else `agentId`, and the numbers are finite and not negative, else null. `reported` is false when the agent has no `byAgent` row, or when the cost and both token counts are all 0 or null (a harness that reports nothing, such as agy through the `process` adapter); a row that is not reported has null numbers and a null share. `share` is the row's cost over the sum of the reported costs, rounded to 3 decimals, null when that sum is 0. `quota` has one row per window, `{ provider, label, usedPercent, resetsAt, error: null }` in the given order, and for a provider with `ok: false` one row `{ provider, label: null, usedPercent: null, resetsAt: null, error }` with its error or `Not available.`. Junk input (costs null or not an object, arrays missing or not arrays, entries that are not objects) gives empty parts and never throws, and a repeated `agentId` keeps the first. No DOM and no imports from `src/ui/**`. Tests in a new `tests/costs.test.js`: the mock client's costs (`createMockClient` from `src/control/mock.js`, its `costs()` and `agents()` turned into a roster) giving rows in roster order, an agent with no row, an all zero row, a `byAgent` row with no roster agent, shares that add up to about 1, a provider that failed, `percent` from `utilizationPercent` and from spend over budget, `over`, junk input, and a repeated `agentId`. Files: `src/model/costs.js`, `tests/costs.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**A5b. Cost board in the room (AGY, merged as 0.1.138).** After A5a. The room shows what each agent has spent without opening the drawer. In `src/Stage.jsx`, when there is a client, read `client.agents()` and `client.costs()` at start, every 60 seconds and on an `agents` or `costs` notice (`subscribeToNotices` from `src/ui/panels/notices.js`), turn the agents into a roster with `toAgents` from `src/live/source.js` so the keys match the room's, and pass `costBoard(costs, roster)` from `src/model/costs.js` to a new `room.setCosts(board)`; when a read fails, pass `null`. Add `src/engine/board.js` exporting a pure `boardLines(board)` and `createCostBoard(cssScene, { onPick })`, which returns `{ object, update(board), setVisible(visible), dispose() }`. `boardLines` answers `{ summary: { text, over }, rows: [{ key, name, cost, tokens, share, reported }], quota: [{ text, error }], empty }`: the summary reads `SPENT $1.23 OF $5.00 (24.6%)`, or `SPENT $1.23, NO BUDGET` when the budget is 0; a row's cost reads `$0.08`, its tokens `12.3k in / 4.5k out` (a count under 1000 as the number, a null count as `?`), and a row that is not reported reads `not reported` with no tokens and no share; a quota row reads `PROVIDER LABEL 42%`, or the provider and its error; `null` gives no rows and `empty` set to `Costs not available.`. The board is a CSS3D panel that floats to the right of the hub ring in the overview and faces the camera like the terminal chips: the title `COSTS`, the summary (red when `over`), the rows with the name through `visibleText` and a thin bar for the share (a row that is not reported in dim white), then the quota rows (an error in red). Clicking a row with a key calls `onPick(key)`, which Stage wires to `room.focus(key)`; a row with a null key, or a key the room does not have, does nothing. Hide it with `object.visible`, since the CSS3D renderer resets `display` every frame, and hide it when the window is narrower than 900 pixels, where the drawer's Meters tab covers costs. Classes `.mr-board`, `.mr-board-title`, `.mr-board-summary[.is-over]`, `.mr-board-row[.is-dim]`, `.mr-board-bar`, `.mr-board-quota[.is-error]`, `.mr-board-empty`; colours and fonts from the conventions, never bold. The room disposes the board in `dispose()`. Check by eye in the dev server, replay mode (it uses the mock client), at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768: the board beside the ring, its numbers match the drawer's Meters tab, a click on a row flies to that agent, a window 880 wide hides it, and it covers no HUD part. Say in the report what you looked at. Tests in a new `tests/cost-board.test.js`: `boardLines` on the mock's costs and agents (the summary, rows in roster order, a row not reported, over budget, no budget, a failed provider, `null`, junk input), and `createCostBoard` with a fake document and scene as in `tests/engine-dispose.test.js` (one object added, `update` writes the rows, a click calls `onPick` with the key, `dispose` removes it). Files: `src/engine/board.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, `tests/cost-board.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**A5c. Make room for the cost board (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.142).** After A5b and P3c. The board from A5b (`src/engine/board.js`) floats right of the hub at the terminal chips' scale, so at the overview it is about 110 by 150 pixels at 1280 by 800 and its text cannot be read; drawn at scale 2.4 it reads well but covers the right-hand folder cards and the agents' chips, because `layoutCards` and `fitView` do not know it is there. Move the board below the hub ring in the overview's view plane, centred under the ring, and draw it at scale 2.4. Export its box in the view plane from `board.js` and pass it to `layoutCards` through a new `obstacles` option, a list of `{ x, y, hw, hh }` boxes that box relaxation keeps every card clear of, the way it keeps them clear of the hub; with no `obstacles` the layout is exactly as before. Add the board's bounding sphere to the spheres the overview fit uses in `src/engine/room.js` while the board is shown, and leave it out while it is hidden (window narrower than 900 pixels). Tests: in `tests/layout.test.js`, no card overlaps a given obstacle box, and the same entries with no `obstacles` give the same positions as before; in `tests/cost-board.test.js`, the exported box matches the board's position and scale. Check by eye is left to the orchestrator: say so in the report. Files: `src/engine/board.js`, `src/engine/layout.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, `tests/layout.test.js`, `tests/cost-board.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V8. Strands keep up with longer strings (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.140).** `update` in `src/engine/strings.js` calls `strand.mesh.geometry.setFromPoints(pts)` on the strand's existing geometry. When a string grows longer than it was when the geometry was first filled, three.js keeps the first buffer, draws only its first points and prints `THREE.BufferGeometry: Buffer size too small for points data. Use .dispose() and create a new geometry.` on every update; one replay of the sandbox recording printed thousands of them. When the number of points changes, dispose of the strand's old geometry and give its mesh a new `BufferGeometry` filled with `setFromPoints`; when the number is the same, keep updating the existing one. Do the same check anywhere else in `strings.js` that refills a geometry. Tests in a new `tests/strings.test.js`, with a fake scene (`add` and `remove` only): build one link, update with two centres close together and then far apart, and check that the strand's position count equals `strandPoints` for the far pair, that the old geometry was disposed, and that `console.warn` was never called. Files: `src/engine/strings.js`, `tests/strings.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V9. Reduced motion across the room (AGY, merged as 0.1.158).** Part of P3. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the room stops only the camera's pointer drift (`camera.js`), the strand fade (`strings.js`) and the CSS transitions in `src/styles.css`; everything else still moves. Add a helper to `src/engine/sprites.js` that keeps the `MediaQueryList` for `(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` (null when `window` or `matchMedia` is missing) and answers `still()` from its `matches` on each call, so turning the setting on or off applies at once without a reload, and a pure `easeRate(delta, speed, still)` that returns 1 when `still` is true and `1 - Math.exp(-delta * speed)` otherwise. Use them so that while `still()` is true: the camera jumps to its goal instead of flying (`update` in `camera.js`, which keeps its drift check); folder cards jump to their place and size (`cards.js`); agents jump to their goal instead of travelling, and their shapes, the hub's dash ring (`hub.js`) and the far dust (`void.js`) stop turning; file name labels appear in place and fade without rising (`labels.js`); and a pulse that travels or grows (`room.js`, `hub.js`) only changes brightness where it ends. Colours, flashes, the trace, the callout and the `FAILED` band stay as they are, since they carry meaning. Replace the two checks in `camera.js` and `strings.js` with the helper. Keep the room deterministic. Tests in a new `tests/motion.test.js`: `easeRate` (still gives 1, not still gives the old formula, a delta of 0) and the helper with a stubbed `window.matchMedia` (matches, does not match, missing, and a change of `matches` after the helper was made). Check by eye with reduced motion on (Chrome DevTools, Rendering, Emulate CSS media feature `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, or the Windows animation setting): seek the replay, focus an agent with `1` to `9`, and say what moved and what did not at 1280 by 800. Files: `src/engine/sprites.js`, `src/engine/camera.js`, `src/engine/cards.js`, `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/engine/hub.js`, `src/engine/void.js`, `src/engine/labels.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, `src/engine/strings.js`, `tests/motion.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the Room behaviour lines that name reduced motion), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U2c. The unreadable counter in the health answer (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.72).** Uses `createUnreadableCounter` from `src/model/parsers/unreadable.js` (P9, P10). The bridge keeps one counter and feeds it in `accept()`, the one place every frame passes on its way to the browser, so it sees restored chunks and never the same frame twice. For a `heartbeat.run.log` frame with `payload.stream === 'stdout'` it calls `push(parser, payload.runId, payload.chunk)`, where `parser` is the one `mapAgent` gives the frame's `payload.agentId`. For a `heartbeat.run.status` frame whose status is `succeeded`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `timed_out` it calls `end(payload.runId)`. For that the bridge keeps an `agentId` to parser map, filled each time it fetches the agent list: at start, each time its Paperclip socket opens, and whenever `/api/agents` is asked. A frame from an agent the map does not know starts one fetch (at most one every 30 seconds) and counts nothing until the map knows the agent. A failed fetch keeps the old map and never throws. `/api/health` adds `unparsed: { total, byParser, since }`, where `since` is the ISO time the counter started. Keep every other field as it is. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js` with the fake Paperclip: a broken OpenCode tool line split over two frames counts once, a well formed one counts zero, a Hermes `preparing` line counts zero, a stderr frame counts nothing, an unknown agent counts nothing and then counts once the agent list knows it, a truncated frame the bridge restores counts as the full line, and a run that ends with a broken tail counts it. Files: `server/index.js`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**W2a. Paperclip's backup routes, read from its code (Hermes, merged as 0.1.70).** The first step of W2. Paperclip 2026.1012.0 is installed at `C:\Users\<name>\AppData\Roaming\npm\node_modules\paperclipai`, and its server code is in `node_modules\@paperclipai\server\dist` under that folder. Read it, starting with `routes\instance-database-backups.js`, `routes\health.js` and `routes\openapi.js` and the services they call, and write `docs/agents/BACKUPS.md` in plain words: each backup route (method, path, who may call it, the request body and the answer fields), where a backup file is written and how it is named, whether Paperclip backs up by itself and how often (and which setting controls that), how the time of the last backup can be read, and how to restore a backup, step by step, as far as the code shows. Give the file and line for each fact, and write `not found in the code` where the code does not say. Read only: do not start, stop or call Paperclip, do not change any file outside your worktree, and do not copy Paperclip's code into the repository (a route path or a field name is fine). Files: `docs/agents/BACKUPS.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**G3b. Bring the README up to date (Hermes, merged as 0.1.71).** `README.md` still describes the first room (a core, terminals on a wall, folders on a floor, yellow for finished work). Rewrite it from `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` as it stands now, for a person who has never seen the project: one short paragraph on what it is (coding agents working on one project, followed live from a local Paperclip or replayed from a recording); "What you see", a short list taken from "What it shows" (the hub ring, the agent shapes, the terminal chips, the folder cards, and the four colours with what each means); "Run it" with `npm install`, `npm run dev`, the connect screen and the addresses; "One port" with `npm run build` and then `npm run serve` and its flags; "Keys", checked one by one against `SHORTCUTS` in `src/ui/Hud.jsx`; "Recordings" (each lane run started with `npm run run-lane` writes one, opened with `/?recording=<name>`); "Checks" (`npm test`, `npm run build`, `npm run doctor`); and a last line pointing to `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Keep the three image lines as they are (G4 replaces the pictures). Plain words, short sentences, no em dashes, no emoji, and nothing the code does not back. Under 120 lines. Files: `README.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**W2b. Backup age and back up now through the bridge (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.73).** From `docs/agents/BACKUPS.md` (W2a). Add both Paperclip routes to `PAPERCLIP_SURFACE` in `server/surface.js`: `POST /api/instance/database-backups` (answer fields `backupFile`, `sizeBytes`, `prunedCount`, `finishedAt`) and `GET /api/health` (answer field `databaseBackup`), so `npm run doctor` checks them. In the bridge, `GET /api/backup` reads Paperclip's `GET /api/health` and answers `{ enabled, status, latestAt, ageHours, warnings, checkedAt }` from its `databaseBackup` block: `latestAt` and `ageHours` from `latestBackup.mtime` and `latestBackup.ageHours`, `warnings` as the list of warning codes only, and `{ enabled: false, status: null, latestAt: null, ageHours: null, warnings: [] }` with `checkedAt` when the block is missing. `POST /api/backup` needs the body `{ confirmed: true }` and answers 400 with a plain sentence without it; it calls `POST /api/instance/database-backups` with no body and answers `{ ok: true, name, sizeBytes, prunedCount, finishedAt }`, where `name` is the file name of `backupFile` without its folder (never send a full path to the page). A 409 from Paperclip answers 409 `A backup is already running.`, a 403 answers 403 `Paperclip refused the backup.`, and any other failure answers 502 `Paperclip could not make a backup.`; a failed `GET` answers 502 `Paperclip did not answer.`. Paperclip takes 10 to 60 seconds for a backup, so give the POST a 120 second timeout and the GET 10 seconds. Add `backupStatus()` and `backupNow()` to `src/control/client.js` (the second sends `{ confirmed: true }`). Do not touch `src/control/mock.js` or `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`: Claude adds the mock and the contract. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js` with the fake Paperclip: a healthy block, a missing block, a stale warning, a POST without `confirmed`, a good backup (the answer holds the file name and no folder), a 409, a 403, Paperclip down for each route, and the client methods against the bridge. Never call a real Paperclip in a test. Files: `server/surface.js`, `server/index.js`, `src/control/client.js`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `tests/doctor.test.js` if the new routes change its counts, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**T13. Lane run totals (Hermes, merged as 0.1.74).** The run records in `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs\runs.jsonl` (T3) hold each lane run's lane, model, minutes, exit code, `killed` and cost. Add `npm run run-lane -- --stats`, with an optional `--since YYYY-MM-DD`, that prints totals per lane and model. In `src/briefs/run.js`: `parseRunArgs` accepts `--stats` (no lane or brief needed, like `--list`) and `--since` with a date in that form (anything else throws a plain sentence); a pure `laneStats(records, { since } = {})` returns `{ rows, total }`, where each row is `{ lane, model, runs, finished, killed, failed, minutesTotal, minutesMedian, costTotal, costRuns }` for one lane and model (a missing model counts as `model unknown`), sorted by lane and then model. `finished` counts exit code 0 and not killed, `killed` counts killed runs, and `failed` is the rest. Minutes and costs that are not finite numbers are left out of the sums and the median, `costRuns` counts the runs that reported a cost, minutes are rounded to one decimal and costs to four. `total` has the same fields summed over every row (`lane` and `model` are `all`, and the median is over every run). With `since`, a record whose `startedAt` is before that day (UTC) or missing is left out. Junk records (not objects, no lane) are skipped. A pure `formatStats(stats)` returns the lines to print: one per row, `hermes | deepseek/... | 12 runs | 11 finished, 0 killed, 1 failed | 61.2 min, median 4.9 | 0.5120 USD over 12 runs`, then the total, or `No runs recorded.` when there are none. `scripts/run-lane.mjs` reads the file the way `--list` does and prints `formatStats`. Tests in `tests/run-lane.test.js`: parsing `--stats` and `--since` (good and bad dates), grouping by lane and model, the three outcome counts, the median with odd and even counts, a missing cost, a missing model, `since` leaving out older and undated records, junk records, the total, and the formatted lines. Files: `src/briefs/run.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `tests/run-lane.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**W1a. machine-room doctor (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.76).** The first step of W1. `bin/machine-room.mjs` gains a subcommand, `machine-room doctor` (run as `npm run serve -- doctor`), that starts neither the bridge nor the viewer. It checks a running Paperclip, prints one line per check that starts with `ok` or `FAIL`, and exits 0 when every check passes and 1 when any fails. It takes `--paperclip`, `--company` and `--root` with the same environment fallbacks as the serve command. The checks, in this order: Paperclip answers `GET /api/health` within 10 seconds (the ok line names its `version`; a failure says `Paperclip does not answer at URL.` and skips the checks that need it); the route check, `compareSurface` against `/api/openapi.json`, naming each missing route or field (use the code `scripts/doctor.mjs` already uses, moving any shared part into `server/surface.js` instead of copying it, and keep `npm run doctor` working); the company is set and `GET /api/companies` lists it; the project folder exists and is a folder; and the database backups, read from the health answer's `databaseBackup` (see `docs/agents/BACKUPS.md`): a missing block or `enabled: false` fails with `Automatic database backups are off.`, no `latestBackup` fails with `No database backup found.`, and a newest backup older than 24 hours fails with its age, for example `The newest database backup is 49.5 hours old.`; Paperclip's warning codes are listed on that line. Every failed backup line ends with `Make one from the room or with paperclipai db:backup.` Tests in a new `tests/machine-doctor.test.js` with a fake Paperclip HTTP server: everything good (exit 0), Paperclip down, a dropped route, an unknown company, no company given, a missing project folder, backups off, no backup, a backup 25 hours old, and the bin run as a child process for one good and one failing case. Never call a real Paperclip. Files: `bin/machine-room.mjs`, `server/surface.js`, `scripts/doctor.mjs`, a new `server/doctor.js` if the checks want their own module, `tests/machine-doctor.test.js`, `tests/doctor.test.js` if it needs to follow a move, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

In `src/model/replay.js`, add a pure `describeRecording(entry)` for one entry of `parseRecordingIndex` that returns `{ name, title, detail, when }`: `title` is the lane and the briefs joined by spaces (`hermes T13`; `run` when the lane is empty, the lane alone when there are no briefs); `detail` joins with `, ` the minutes rounded to whole minutes (`8 min`, `under 1 min` below one, left out when null), the tool calls (`42 tool calls`, `1 tool call`, left out when null) and the outcome (`finished` for exit code 0, `failed` for any other number, left out when null); `when` is the start as `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` in the machine's local time, or an empty string when the start is not a valid time. Add `filterRecordings(entries, { lane, text } = {})` that keeps the order and returns the entries whose lane equals `lane` when one is given, and whose name, lane, briefs or model contain `text` ignoring case when that is given and not blank. Tests in `tests/replay.test.js`: each part of the title and the detail, the plurals, `under 1 min`, a failed run, missing numbers, an invalid start, the local time (build the expected string from a `Date` in the test so it passes in any time zone), the lane filter, the text filter on each field, a blank text, both filters together, and junk input giving an empty list. Files: `src/model/replay.js`, `tests/replay.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U2d. Switch off only the feature whose routes changed (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.81).** U2 says only the failing feature switches off and the room keeps drawing. Give each route in `PAPERCLIP_SURFACE` (`server/surface.js`) a `feature`: `room` for `GET /api/companies`, the event socket, the run log route, `GET /api/companies/{companyId}` and `GET /api/companies/{companyId}/agents`; `backup` for `GET /api/health` and `POST /api/instance/database-backups`; `team` for the agent hires, the agent `PATCH`, pause and resume; `meters` for the budgets `PATCH` and the three costs routes; `tasks` for the issues and comments routes; `approvals` for the approval routes. A test fails when a route has no feature or an unknown one. `/api/health` adds `features`, an object with every feature name set to false when any of its routes is missing or changed in the last surface check, and true otherwise (true too while the check has not run or could not read the spec: unknown is not off). The bridge routes that call a feature that is off answer 503 with `Paperclip changed: FEATURE is off. Run npm run doctor to see which routes.` before they call Paperclip: the control routes for `tasks`, `approvals`, `meters` and `team` (find them in `server/control.js`) and `/api/backup` for `backup`. `room` off never stops the event stream, the agent list or the file tree; it only shows in `features`. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js` with the fake Paperclip's spec builder: a full spec (every feature true), a spec without an approvals route (approvals false, an approvals control route answers 503 with the sentence and Paperclip is not called, a tasks route still works), a changed costs field (meters false), no spec yet (all true), and `room` false with the event stream still sending frames. **T15. Find the native test worker crash (Hermes, merged as 0.1.79).** About one full test run in four dies with exit code -1073740791 (0xC0000409, a stack buffer overrun inside Node) and no failed test, on `main` too (OpenCode saw it in U2c). Run the full suite at least 12 times, each with its output in its own log under `%TEMP%` (`node node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs run --reporter=verbose > %TEMP%\mr-crash-N.log 2>&1`, then the exit code), and for each crashed run note the test files that started but did not finish. Then try, one at a time: the suspect files alone, 20 runs; `--pool=forks` for the whole suite, 12 runs; and, only if forks stops the crash, the smallest change in `vite.config.js` that keeps the faster threads pool for every other file (read `node_modules/vitest/package.json` for the version and use the option that version supports). Keep a config change only when 12 full runs in a row pass and a run is at most half again as slow as now; otherwise change nothing and report what you found. Report a table: the setup, runs, crashes, the files running at each crash, and the seconds per run. Delete the logs when you are done. Files: `vite.config.js` (only if the change earns its place), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**T15b. Run the tests again when Node itself crashes (Hermes, merged as 0.1.82).** T15 found that about half of the full runs on this machine die inside `node.exe` 24.15.0 with 0xC0000409 while the bridge tests run, that no pool setting stops it without making the suite 3.6 times slower, and that bash shows it as exit 127. Running the tests again (try N of 3).`, and `{ action: 'done', code: 1, message }` after the third crash, with `The test worker crashed inside Node three times. See Known limits in docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md.`. Add `scripts/test.mjs`, which runs `node node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs run` with any extra arguments passed through and the output shown live, follows `nextTestStep`, prints its messages, and exits with the final code; an environment variable `MACHINEROOM_TEST_COMMAND` (a JSON array of the command and its arguments) replaces the vitest command for tests. Files: `src/briefs/run.js`, `scripts/test.mjs`, `package.json`, `tests/run-lane.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

One root for the tests' temp folders (Hermes, merged as 0.1.85).** Ten test files make temp folders with `mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), PREFIX))` under a dozen prefixes, and a run that Node kills (T15) never cleans them up: T15 found 288 left in `%TEMP%`. **W1b. machine-room up (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.84).** One command to open the room on a running Paperclip. `machine-room up` (run as `npm run serve -- up`) takes the serve flags, runs the doctor checks from `server/doctor.js` first and prints their lines, then starts the bridge and the built viewer as `serve` does and prints the address, whether the checks passed or not. When a check failed it prints one more line, `Some checks failed. The room opens anyway; features whose routes changed stay off.`, before the address. When Paperclip does not answer at all, it still serves, so the room can show its replay, and says `Paperclip is not running. Start it yourself with paperclipai run; machine-room never starts it.` It never starts, stops or changes Paperclip: starting Paperclip can start agents on their schedules, and that is the owner's call. A missing build stops it as `serve` does. Tests in `tests/machine-doctor.test.js` or a new file, with a fake Paperclip and a temp dist folder: all checks pass (the lines, then the address, and `/` answers), a failed backup check (the extra line, and it still serves), Paperclip down (the not running line, and it still serves), no build (exit 1), and the bin run as a child process that is killed at the end. Never call a real Paperclip. Files: `bin/machine-room.mjs`, `server/doctor.js` if it needs an export, the test file, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**W2c. Backup age and Back up now in the meters panel (AGY, merged as 0.1.98).** The last step of W2. The control client has `backupStatus()` and `backupNow()` (W2b; shapes `BackupStatus` and `BackupResult` in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`), and the mock client, which the room uses in replay, answers both: its newest backup is 49.5 hours old, so its status is `warning` with `database_backup_stale`. Add a Backups section at the bottom of `src/ui/panels/MetersPanel.jsx`. Put the reading in a pure helper `src/ui/panels/backup.js` exporting `backupNotice(status)`, which returns null or `{ tone, title, lines }`. `enabled` false gives tone `yellow`, title `Automatic backups are off` and the line `Turn them on in Paperclip's instance settings.` No `latestAt` gives tone `red` and title `No backup yet`. Otherwise the title is `Newest backup N hours ago`, with `1 hour ago` for one, `less than an hour ago` under one hour, and days from 48 hours on (`2 days ago`, rounded down). The tone is `red` when a warning is `database_backup_stale`, `database_backup_missing` or `database_backup_last_failure`, `yellow` for `database_backup_check_failed`, and `ok` (white) with no warning. One line per warning in words: stale `Older than Paperclip's limit.`, missing `Paperclip found no backup file.`, last failure `The last backup failed.`, check failed `Paperclip could not check its backups.`; an unknown code is shown as it is. Junk input gives null. Also export `formatBytes(n)`: `778 kB` for 796653 and `1.2 MB` for 1258291 (1024 bytes to a kB, 1024 kB to an MB, MB with one decimal), and `size unknown` for null. The section shows the title, the lines and a `Back up now` button. A click opens a confirm card inside the section, styled like the confirm card in `DispatchPanel.jsx`, saying `Back up Paperclip's database now? Paperclip writes a file to its backup folder; agents keep working.` with Back up and Cancel. Back up calls `client.backupNow()` once (the button is disabled and reads `Backing up` while it runs), then shows `Backed up as NAME, SIZE.` and reads `backupStatus()` again. A rejection shows its message in red: the bridge sends `Paperclip changed: backup is off. Run npm run doctor to see which routes.` when the backup routes changed, and the mock sends `A backup is already running.` The section reads `backupStatus()` when the panel loads, and a failed read shows `Could not read the backup status.` in the section without breaking the meters above it. Never call `backupNow()` without the confirm. Do not touch `src/control/**`, `server/**`, `src/Stage.jsx` or `src/App.jsx`. Tests in a new `tests/backup-notice.test.js`: each `backupNotice` case, the hours and days wording, an unknown warning code, junk input, `formatBytes`, and `MetersPanel` rendered with `renderToString` and the mock client showing the Backups heading. Check by eye in the dev server, replay mode, with the control drawer open on Meters (`C`): the stale title, the confirm card, a backup that succeeds and the title after it. **P1c. Pack, unpack and start the room (Hermes, merged as 0.1.87).** P1 asks for a test that packs the package and starts it. The pack check (P1b) only compares file lists. The packed server imports no npm package (it uses Node's own `WebSocket`), so an unpacked pack must run with no `node_modules`. Add to `scripts/pack-check.mjs` an export `packTo(root, destination, budgetMs)` that runs `npm pack --json --pack-destination DESTINATION` in `root` (the same way `runPack` runs `npm pack --dry-run --json`, with the same timeout handling) and returns `{ status, error, signal, file }`, where `file` is the full path of the `.tgz` it wrote, read from the JSON. Add `scripts/untar.mjs` exporting `untar(gzipBuffer, destination)`: gunzip with `node:zlib`, then read the tar in 512-byte blocks (name at offset 0, 100 bytes; size at offset 124, 12 bytes of octal; type at offset 156; the `prefix` field at offset 345, 155 bytes, joined in front of the name when set), write each regular file (type `0` or an empty byte) under `destination`, make its folders, skip other types, stop at the first all-zero block, and throw `Unsafe path in the pack: NAME` for an absolute name or one with a `..` part. Do not shell out to `tar`: Git Bash's GNU tar reads `C:` as a host name. A new `tests/pack-start.test.js`: when `dist/index.html` is missing, skip with a note to run `npm run build` first; otherwise pack into a folder from `makeTempDir` (`tests/helpers/tempRoot.js`), untar it, start `node <unpacked>/package/bin/machine-room.mjs --port 0 --paperclip http://127.0.0.1:1 --company pack-test --root <an empty temp folder>` as a child process with `PAPERCLIP_URL`, `PAPERCLIP_COMPANY_ID`, `PROJECT_ROOT` and `PORT` removed from its environment, read the address from its `machine-room: open` line, and check that `/` answers 200 with the same `<title>` as `dist/index.html`, that `/api/health` answers JSON with `paperclip: false`, and that the unpacked folder has no `node_modules`. Kill the child in a `finally` and wait for it to exit. Skip, like the pack check does, when `npm pack` runs past its budget. Tests for `untar` in `tests/untar.test.js` with tar buffers built in the test: a file in a folder, a long name split with `prefix`, a folder entry skipped, and both unsafe names. Run the full suite twice and say how long `tests/pack-start.test.js` took. Files: `scripts/pack-check.mjs`, `scripts/untar.mjs`, `tests/pack-start.test.js`, `tests/untar.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**W3b. Merge a lane branch with a confirm step (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.88).** The second part of W3, after **W3a** (merged as 0.1.52: `server/reviews.js`, `reviews()` and `reviewDiff(lane)`). Add `head` to each lane entry of `GET /api/control/reviews`: the full sha from `git rev-parse HEAD` in that worktree. Add `POST /api/control/reviews/:lane/merge` with the body `{ confirmed: true, head, message }`. Check in this order, answer each refusal with a plain sentence, and write nothing on a refusal: the lane is an own key with a `worktree` (the same check and 404 as the diff route); `confirmed` is exactly true, otherwise 400 `Confirm the merge first.`; `head` is 40 hex characters and `message` one line of 1 to 200 characters, otherwise 400; no other merge is running, otherwise 409 `A merge is already running.`; the main checkout (`projectRoot`) is on `main`, otherwise 409 `The main checkout is not on main.`; `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` in the main checkout prints nothing, otherwise 409 `The main checkout has uncommitted changes.`; the worktree's `git rev-parse HEAD` equals `head`, otherwise 409 `The lane moved since you read it. Read it again.`; the worktree is not dirty, otherwise 409 `The lane has uncommitted changes.`; `git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD main` fails in the main checkout, otherwise 409 `Nothing to merge.` Then run `git merge --no-ff --no-edit -m MESSAGE HEAD` in the main checkout, where HEAD is the checked sha (never the branch name), with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GIT_EDITOR=true` added to its environment. On success answer `{ ok: true, lane, branch, head, commit }`, where `commit` is the new sha of `main`. When the merge stops on a conflict, read the conflicted paths with `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U`, run `git merge --abort`, check that `main` is back at its old sha with a clean status, and answer 409 `{ error: 'The merge stopped on a conflict. Nothing changed.', files }`. On any other git failure, run `git merge --abort` when a merge is in progress and answer 500 with a plain sentence. Never push, fetch, pull, reset, checkout, stash or delete a branch. Release the merge lock in a `finally`. `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md` merges with git's union driver (see `.gitattributes`), so two changelog lines added on both sides do not conflict. Client: `mergeReview(lane, { head, message })` in `src/control/client.js`, which posts `confirmed: true`, so it is called only after the user confirmed. Add it to `CLIENT_METHODS` in `src/control/mock.js` with a mock that merges a sample lane once and then answers `Nothing to merge.`, refuses a stale `head` with the moved sentence and refuses a missing confirm, and add `head` to the mock's sample lanes. Add the method row, `head` in `Review` and a `MergeResult` shape to `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`. Tests in `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js` with the throwaway repository (folders from `makeTempDir` in `tests/helpers/tempRoot.js`, `user.name` and `user.email` set in that repo only): a clean merge (main gains a merge commit with the message and both parents, and the answer's `commit` matches), each refusal above with the repo unchanged (every ref from `git for-each-ref` and `git status --porcelain` are the same before and after), a conflict that is aborted and leaves main as it was, a changelog line added on both sides that merges cleanly through the union driver (give the throwaway repo the same `.gitattributes` line), and two merges sent at once where the second gets the lock sentence. Shared cases in `tests/control-contract.test.js` so both clients answer alike. The panel that calls this is W3c (AGY); do not add it. Files: `server/reviews.js`, `server/index.js` if the route needs wiring, `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js`, `tests/control-contract.test.js`, `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**T17. The lane runner refuses a brief it cannot run (Hermes, merged as 0.1.89).** On 2026-10-20 a second brief with the id W3a sent OpenCode the old W3a, merged as 0.1.52; OpenCode found the branch already there and stopped. Catch that before an agent starts. Add to `src/briefs/run.js` a pure export `briefProblems({ briefs, ids, laneKey, branchExists, force })`, where `briefs` is the `parseBriefs` list, `ids` the ids asked for, and `branchExists(name)` says whether a local branch exists. It returns a list of sentences, empty when the run can go: an id that is not there gives `There is no brief named ID.`; an id that appears more than once (compare ids without case) gives `Brief ID appears N times in TASKS.md. Give each brief its own id.`; a brief whose `done` is true gives `Brief ID is marked OWNER_TEXT. Pick another brief, or pass --force to run it again.` with the brief's `ownerText` as it is written; a brief whose `owners` list is not empty and does not hold the lane gives `Brief ID is for OWNERS, not LANE. Pass --force to run it anyway.` (owners joined with `and`); and a brief whose `branchFor(brief)` already exists gives `The branch BRANCH already exists. Rename or delete it first, or pick another brief.` `force` drops only the done and owner sentences. `parseRunArgs` takes `--force`. In `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, replace the lookup that throws `There is no brief named` with `briefProblems`, where `branchExists` runs `git rev-parse --verify --quiet refs/heads/NAME` in the main checkout with an argument list (no shell, `windowsHide`): print each sentence and exit 1 before the lock, the log or the prompt are made, and do the same for `--dry-run`. Tests in `tests/run-lane.test.js`: each sentence, `--force` dropping only its two, several problems at once in a fixed order (missing, repeated, done, owner, branch), the id compared without case, `parseRunArgs` with `--force`, and the bin run as a child process with `--dry-run` on a merged brief exiting 1 with the sentence (use a brief from the real `docs/agents/TASKS.md` that is marked merged, read at test time). Files: `src/briefs/run.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `tests/run-lane.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

The review checker's temp test looks only at its own folder (Hermes, merged as 0.1.148).** `tests/review-lane.test.js` ends with a test that the system temp folder gains no `machineroom-review-*` folder during the file, to catch a `reviewLane` or `main` call that forgets `tempRoot`. **G1a. History audit before anything is published (Hermes, merged as 0.1.93).** The first part of G1. Before the owner pushes (G6), find what must not leave the machine. Add a pure `src/briefs/audit.js` exporting `findLeaks(text, { profileName })`, which returns `[{ kind, line, sample }]` for: `key` (a token that starts `sk-` or `sk-or-v1-` followed by 20 or more word characters, a `ghp_`, `gho_` or `github_pat_` token, `AKIA` followed by 16 capitals or digits, and a line that sets `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY` to a value that is not empty and not a placeholder such as `your-key`, `xxx` or `<...>`); `email` (any address except `noreply@anthropic.com` and addresses at `users.noreply.github.com`); `userpath` (`C:\Users\NAME` with either slash, any case); `profile` (the profile name as a whole word, any case); and `graded` (any path into the graded coursework folder). A key's `sample` never holds the key: only its first 4 characters, `…` and its length (`sk-o… (73 characters)`). Other kinds show the line cut to 80 characters around the match. Add `scripts/history-audit.mjs` (`npm run history-audit`, added to `package.json`). It reads `git log -p --no-color --no-ext-diff --no-textconv main --tags` as a stream, line by line (spawn with an argument list; the output is large), which is what G6 would push: `main` and the tags, not the `feat/` branches. It also reads the tracked files (`git ls-files`, skipping files over 2 MB). It prints the findings grouped by kind with the commit, file and line, then the count per kind; `--json` prints the same as JSON; it exits 1 when it found anything and never writes a file. The profile name comes from `os.userInfo().username`. Tests in a new `tests/history-audit.test.js`: each kind, the allowed addresses, placeholders that are not keys, a key's sample, and a throwaway repo from `makeTempDir` where a fake key is added and then removed (the audit still finds it). Build every fake key in the tests from pieces (`'sk-' + 'a'.repeat(40)`) so no key-shaped string sits in any file. Run it once on the real repository and give only the count per kind in your report, never a sample of a key. Files: `src/briefs/audit.js`, `scripts/history-audit.mjs`, `package.json`, `tests/history-audit.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U3a. Try a lane's build beside the stable room (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.91).** The first part of U3. Add `scripts/candidate.mjs`, run as `npm run candidate -- LANE` (add the script to `package.json`). It reads the lane's `worktree` from `docs/agents/lanes.json` (an own key, `Object.hasOwn`) and refuses when the lane has no worktree (`The lane LANE has no worktree.`) or the worktree has uncommitted changes (`The lane has uncommitted changes. Commit them first.`). It checks the port before anything else is done and refuses with `Port PORT is in use. Pass --port.` Then it builds that worktree's viewer by running its `npm run build` (an argument list through `npm-cli.js` beside node, as `runPack` in `scripts/pack-check.mjs` does, with a 5 minute timeout; on failure it prints the last 20 lines and exits 1), starts that worktree's own `bin/machine-room.mjs` with `--dist WORKTREE/dist` and `--port 5175` (or the `--port` given), passes `--paperclip`, `--company` and `--root` through, and prints `Candidate from LANE (BRANCH at SHA7) on http://127.0.0.1:PORT/?live&bridge=http://127.0.0.1:PORT. The stable room keeps its own port.` It never starts Paperclip, never writes to the main checkout and never switches a branch. Ctrl+C stops the child and waits for it. Keep the parts that decide in pure exports (`parseCandidateArgs(argv)`, `candidatePlan({ lanes, lane, port })`) with their own tests. For tests, `MACHINEROOM_BUILD_COMMAND` (a JSON array) replaces the build command, as `MACHINEROOM_TEST_COMMAND` does in `scripts/test.mjs`. Tests in a new `tests/candidate.test.js` with a throwaway repo and worktree from `makeTempDir`: the plan for a known lane, an unknown lane, `__proto__`, a dirty worktree, a taken port (listen on it first), a failed build, and a run against a worktree whose `bin/machine-room.mjs` is a small fake that prints its arguments and the open line, killed at the end. Add a short runbook, `docs/agents/CANDIDATE.md`: try the candidate next to the stable room, then merge (the Reviews panel or by hand), rebuild and restart the stable room. Files: `scripts/candidate.mjs`, `package.json`, `tests/candidate.test.js`, `docs/agents/CANDIDATE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V7a. The room's panels in the owner's concept style (AGY, merged as 0.1.101).** The owner keeps reference stills in `C:\GitHub\MachineRoom\Concepts\`. Open them read-only to look; never copy them into your worktree or the repo, and do not use `POIelements(NOTTOBEACTUALLYUSED).PNG` as a source. They show a surveillance interface: black space, and over it small black panels with a thin light border and slightly rounded corners; uppercase text; a header strip set as an inverted label (dark text on a white block) over a box; a status line with a white tag and a coloured value; an outlined progress bar with a solid fill and the percentage at its right end; red callout tags with two small sub-tags under them, joined to their target by a thin red line; a full-width solid red band with white uppercase text over red horizontal scanlines when something fails; a small numbered index list with a box-drawing rule on its left; and a command line as a black bar with an outlined `EXEC` button. The owner's portfolio look is the room itself (see V2): keep the void, the lattice and the dust. Restyle, in `src/styles.css` and the components in `src/ui/**`, every HUD surface and the CSS3D surfaces the engine builds (folder cards and terminal chips, styled through the DOM contract in `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`; keep every class name): panels and drawers become bordered boxes with an inverted header strip; panel and card titles are uppercase with 0.04em letter spacing (Jost for titles, Space Mono for data, weights 400 and 500 only, never bold); agent rows and terminal chip titles take the tag look (a name block, then small sub-tags for the lane and the status); budget meters and the backup age use the outlined bar with the percentage at the right; an error state (a failed chip, a refusal, the health chip when red) uses the red band with white uppercase text; a failed file row and a `.is-flash-red` card get faint red horizontal scanlines behind their text (a repeating gradient, at most 25% strength, nothing moving under `prefers-reduced-motion`); the help overlay becomes the numbered index list; the assistant's input becomes the black command bar with an outlined `EXEC` button that sends like Enter; the waiting overlay shows a blinking underscore after its title (no blink under reduced motion). Thin corner ticks on panels are welcome, in CSS only. Colours keep their one meaning each: white activity, `#e5ff00` active, `#ff2a2a` failure, `#2aff80` finished, on black, with greys only for borders and dim text. No logo or name of the show, no images from the stills, no new fonts or packages. Do not change behaviour: the same keys, buttons and text, apart from letter case. Check by eye in the dev server at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768, in replay and with `?live&simulate&speed=3`: the HUD, the control drawer on each tab, the help overlay, the assistant (`/`), an open terminal chip (`T`), a folder card with file rows, and a failed row (the recording has failed tool calls; seek to one). Say in the report what you looked at. Tests: the suite passes, and a new `tests/hud-style.test.js` renders the HUD and the help overlay with `renderToString` and checks the new structure (the header strip element, the index list, the `EXEC` button). **W3c. Review and merge panel (AGY, merged as 0.1.109).** The last part of W3. The control client has `reviews()`, `reviewDiff(lane)` and `mergeReview(lane, { head, message })` (W3a and W3b; shapes `Review`, `ReviewDiff` and `MergeResult` in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`), and the mock answers all three. **V7b. Concept details in the room itself (AGY, merged as 0.1.120).** After V7a, in `src/engine/**`, which AGY owns from 0.1.90. First, strands: each curved string between folders (`strings.js`; `curvePoint` stays pure and tested) gains a fine strand of small points along its curve, like the network strands in the stills (`C:\GitHub\MachineRoom\Concepts\`, read-only): white at low opacity, about one point per 24 units of curve, scattered a little off the curve by a seeded offset, drawn as one `THREE.Points` per string; the strand on the path a working agent travels turns yellow and fades back over 1.5 seconds after the work moves on. The box follows the agent, faces the camera and stays clear of its terminal chip. **C6a. Outcome log (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.94).** The first part of C6. The lane runner records each run in `runs.jsonl` under `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs\` (lane, briefs, model, minutes, exitCode, costUsd, branch, commits), but not what happened after: merged or sent back, the grade, the review fixes. Add `scripts/outcome.mjs`, run as `npm run outcome -- BRANCH merged|rejected [--grade A-] [--fixes N] [--note TEXT]` (add it to `package.json`), which appends one line to `outcomes.jsonl` beside `runs.jsonl`: `{ branch, outcome, grade, fixes, note, version, recordedAt }`, with `version` from `package.json`. It refuses with a plain sentence, writing nothing, when no run record has that branch (`No lane run made the branch BRANCH.`), the outcome is not `merged` or `rejected`, the grade is not one of `A+ A A- B+ B B- C D F`, fixes is not a whole number from 0 to 50, or the note is over 200 characters or holds a line break. A later outcome for the same branch is allowed and the newest wins. Add a pure `outcomeTable(runs, outcomes)` in a new `server/outcomes.js`, which returns one row per lane and model, `{ lane, model, runs, merged, rejected, pending, mergeRate, averageFixes, costUsd, costPerMerge }`, where `pending` counts runs whose branch has no outcome, `mergeRate` is merged over merged plus rejected (null when both are 0), `averageFixes` is over the merged runs (null with none), and `costPerMerge` is the cost of all the rows' runs over merged (null with none); a run with no branch counts as pending. Rows are sorted by lane, then model. Add `GET /api/control/runs/outcomes` to `server/runs.js`, answering `{ rows }`, and `outcomes()` to `src/control/client.js` and `src/control/mock.js` (`CLIENT_METHODS` and a mock built from sample runs), with the method row and an `OutcomeRow` shape in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`. Read both files the way `server/runs.js` reads `runs.jsonl`, skipping lines that are not JSON. Tests in a new `tests/outcomes.test.js`: each refusal with nothing written, the newest outcome winning, `outcomeTable` with merged, rejected, pending and branchless runs, the route against temp files (point the runs folder at a `makeTempDir` folder the way the existing runs tests do), and the shared case in `tests/control-contract.test.js`. **C4a. Routing policy, version 1 (Hermes, merged as 0.1.96).** The first part of C4 (see `Routing policy, version 1: rules` in `docs/agents/CONTROL-PLANE.md`). Add a policy file `docs/agents/policy.json` and a pure `src/policy/route.js`. The file has `tiers` (`small`, `medium`, `large`), each an ordered list of choices `{ lane, model, reasoning, provider }`; `limits` with `runUsd`, `issueUsd`, `agentMonthlyUsd`, `companyMonthlyUsd`, `maxTurnsPerRun` and `timeoutSec`; and `quotaSkipPercent`. Fill it from today's lanes in `lanes.json`: small is Hermes on `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017` at xhigh, then Hermes on `minimax/minimax-m3.1-flash:free` at high; medium is OpenCode on its model at max, then Hermes on DeepSeek; large is AGY on Gemini 4 Argon (Medium), then `owner` (a choice with lane `owner` means ask the owner). Limits: 0.25, 1, 10 and 30 dollars, 200 turns, 3600 seconds; `quotaSkipPercent` 90. Exports: `readPolicy(json)` checks the schema and throws a plain sentence naming the first bad field (`policy.limits.runUsd must be a number above 0.`); `tierFor(profile)` maps `{ files, hasTests, clarity, reversible, touchesSecrets, touchesDeploy }` to a tier: `large` when clarity is `unclear`, it touches secrets or deployment, or it is not reversible; `small` when it is 2 files or fewer with tests and none of those; `medium` otherwise; `escalate(tier, failures)` moves up one tier per failure and stops at `large`; `chooseRoute({ policy, profile, failures, quotas, spent })` returns `{ tier, choice, reason }`, taking the first choice of the tier whose provider is not over its quota (a provider is over when any window in `quotas`, the `quota` list of the control client's `Costs` in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`, has `usedPercent` above `quotaSkipPercent`; a window with `usedPercent: null` or a provider with `ok: false` does not count), and `choice: null` with a reason when every choice is skipped or `spent` (`{ runUsd, issueUsd, agentMonthlyUsd, companyMonthlyUsd }`, each optional) is at or above its limit (`The issue has spent $1.00 of its $1.00 limit.`). No randomness: the same input gives the same answer. Nothing calls this yet. Tests in a new `tests/policy.test.js`: each tier rule, escalation stopping at large, a skipped provider, a null window not skipping, every limit, a bad policy file for each field, and the real `docs/agents/policy.json` passing `readPolicy`. **U19. The proxy route masks keys too (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.160).** From the UI audit (N6) and the October audit (N2): the bridge masks keys in log chunks, messages and errors (`createSplitMasker` in `src/briefs/audit.js`, since 0.1.152), but a page on the proxy route (`/?live` through Vite, no bridge) reads Paperclip's socket itself and shows a key an agent prints as it is. In `openStream` in `src/live/source.js`, pass each parsed frame through one `createSplitMasker` per stream, the way `release` and `accept` in `server/index.js` drive it: `push(frame, now())`, hand the frames it returns to `onFrame` in order, then set one timer for `nextDue()` that calls `due(now())` and releases those too. So a frame reaches `onFrame` only after it is masked, and a frame held for a key that may continue in the next chunk waits at most 1 second. `close()` clears that timer and drops the held frames, since nothing reads them after a close. When the socket closes and reconnects, call `flush()` before the new socket opens, so held frames come before the new socket's frames. Add `now = Date.now` to `openStream`'s options beside `setTimer` and `clearTimer`, and keep the reconnect and backoff as they are. `src/Stage.jsx` calls `openStream` and stays unchanged. Tests in `tests/live.test.js` with its fake WebSocket and fake timers: a key whole in one chunk reaches `onFrame` as `[key]`; a key split across two chunks of one run and stream reaches it masked in both frames; a held frame is released by the timer after 1 second with no second chunk; a status frame behind a held chunk keeps its place; a frame with no key passes through unchanged and at once; `close()` leaves no timer. Build the test keys from pieces, never one literal. Update the masking line in Known limits in `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, the sentence in `README.md` that says the proxy route masks nothing, and the N6 row of `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-UI.md`. Files: `src/live/source.js`, `tests/live.test.js`, `README.md`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-UI.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U5d. Three small fixes the audit left open (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.116).** From `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md` (N7, N8 and N9). In `server/index.js`, `readJsonBody` accepts a body only when the media type before any `;` is exactly `application/json` (case and spaces ignored), so `text/plain; x=application/json` gets the 415 `Send JSON.` that other types get today; and after its 413 it sends `Connection: close` and destroys the request, so the bridge stops reading a large body at once. In `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, move `takeLaneLock` and `releaseLaneLock` into a new `scripts/lane-lock.mjs` that the runner imports, and replace a stale lock by deleting it and taking it again with the `wx` flag; when that second try finds the file there, another runner took it first, and it refuses with the sentence it gives today for a running lane. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js` (`application/json; charset=utf-8` passes, `text/plain; x=application/json` answers 415, a 100 KB body answers 413 and the connection closes without the rest being sent) and a new `tests/lane-lock.test.js` (a free lock is taken, a live holder is refused, a stale lock is taken, a stale lock that another runner takes between the read and the second try is refused, and release removes only its own lock). Files: `server/index.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `scripts/lane-lock.mjs`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `tests/lane-lock.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.
