Give `reviewLane` and the merged-result step a `tempRoot` option (default `tmpdir()`, so `npm run review-lane` keeps its place), and make every test in `tests/review-lane.test.js` that reaches the merged result pass a `makeTempDir` folder from `tests/helpers/tempRoot.js`. Do not change how the script makes, checks or removes the `node_modules` link. Tests: a run with `tempRoot` makes its worktree inside that folder and leaves it empty afterwards, and the system temp folder gains no `machineroom-review-*` folder during the file. Files: `scripts/review-lane.mjs`, `tests/review-lane.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Roadmap: Hermes lane

Hermes runs on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash (H1, set by the owner), works in its own worktree `C:\GitHub\MachineRoom-hermes`, and takes cheap tasks with named files and clear tests. One task at a time, one branch per task, never on `main`, never pushed. The prompt for each task says: the branch, the files it may touch, the test command, the commit prefix, and the report to give. Known limits to plan around: it may not see a long brief at once, so briefs stay short; its reads show only file names; and it tends to bundle commands, so the prompt asks for `npm test` after every change and a stop on a red test.

Order: **P6** (parser, small, merged), **G5** (CI file, merged), **P5** (parser, harder, merged), **P7** (here-string bodies, merged), **G3** (README draft, merged), **G7** (pin Node, merged), **P3a** (folder collapse plan, merged). **T2** (lane runner, merged). **T3** (run records, merged). **T4** (OpenCode cost, smaller toolset, merged). **T6** (tool lines from Hermes runs, merged as 0.1.48). **T8** (replay a lane run in the room, merged as 0.1.50). **T9** (the OpenCode report and the model in run lists, merged as 0.1.51; run on MiniMax M3.1 Flash Free at high: 9 minutes, no cost, clean). **V1** (folder view plan, merged as 0.1.54). **T10** (record every lane run, merged as 0.1.56). **V6** (folders close after the work ends, merged as 0.1.59; 3 minutes, 4 cents). **P8** (resolve a bare file name from the agent's last folder, merged as 0.1.60; 4 minutes, 3 cents). **T11** (an index of the recordings, merged as 0.1.62; 5 minutes, 4 cents). **T12** (read the recordings index in the app, merged as 0.1.63; 4 minutes, 4 cents). **P9** (lines that look like tool calls but no parser read, merged as 0.1.65; 7 minutes, 6 cents). **P10** (count unreadable lines across chunks, merged as 0.1.66; 6 minutes, 7 cents). **A5a** (cost board rows, merged as 0.1.68; 5 minutes, 4 cents). **W2a** (Paperclip's backup routes, read from its code, merged as 0.1.70; 14 minutes, 23 cents). **G3b** (bring the README up to date, merged as 0.1.71; 5 minutes, 4 cents). **T13** (lane run totals, merged as 0.1.74; 8 minutes, 9 cents). **T14** (labels and a filter for the recordings list, merged as 0.1.75; 6 minutes, 3 cents). **T15** (find the native test worker crash, merged as 0.1.79; 28 minutes, 8 cents). **T15b** (run the tests again when Node itself crashes, merged as 0.1.82; 13 minutes, 8 cents). **T16** (one root for the tests' temp folders, merged as 0.1.85; 14 minutes, 7 cents). **P1c** (pack, unpack and start the room, merged as 0.1.87; 14 minutes, 6 cents). **T17** (the lane runner refuses a brief it cannot run, merged as 0.1.89; 13 minutes, 14 cents). **G1a** (history audit before anything is published, merged as 0.1.93; 20 minutes, 6 cents). **C4a** (routing policy, version 1, merged as 0.1.96; 9 minutes, 2 cents). **G3c** (README for the room as it is now, merged as 0.1.100; 15 minutes, 9 cents). From the October audit: **U5b** (key masker, merged as 0.1.103; 8 minutes, 3 cents). **C4d** (task profile from a brief, merged as 0.1.105; 10 minutes, 8 cents). **C5a** (planner prompt and plan reader, merged as 0.1.107; 6 minutes). **C6c** (policy proposals, merged as 0.1.111; 12 minutes, 4 cents). **G1b** (placeholders the history audit should not count, merged as 0.1.114; 10 minutes). **S2a** and **S3a** wait for S1. Each is described elsewhere in this file or in `PLAN.md`. Claude reviews and merges each branch. Hermes does not touch `server/**` (OpenCode), `src/ui/**` (AGY) or `src/engine/**`, `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/App.jsx`, `src/control/**`, `src/assistant/**`, `src/decider/**` (Claude). That split is history: `docs/agents/lanes.json` gives `src/ui/**` and `src/engine/**` to OpenCode now.

## Found in review, 2026-10-19

**U4. Reject a foreign Host header in the bridge (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.39).** The bridge rejects a foreign `Origin`, but a page that rebinds its own name to 127.0.0.1 sends a same-origin GET with no `Origin` and can read `/api/control/*`, `/api/tree` and the event stream. Answer `403` when the `Host` header is not `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` with any port. Tests for each. Add it to the protected files list.

**C3e. HUD polish (AGY, merged as 0.1.40).** The assistant window opens top left over the terminal wall. Move its default position to above the transport bar on the right, keep the saved position working, and check the result by eye at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768. Also check by eye every change you make to a panel and say in the report that you did. Part of C3b was skipped.

**M1. Exit test with a real Paperclip (Claude and the owner, done as 0.1.97).** M1 passed on 2026-10-20 (0.1.97) with the owner's yes: `machine-room up` against the real Paperclip 2026.1012.0 (every route check ok, the backup 68 hours old), a task JC-24 created from the drawer for Hermes in the sandbox (low priority, 15 turns, 300 seconds), the run drawn in the room (Hermes left the hub, the sandbox card opened, `package.json` read once, Hermes green at the end), and its correct reply read in the thread, 58 seconds in all. Found: Hermes in Paperclip has no model set and runs on its own default, `xiaomi/mimo-v2.6-pro` at xhigh (the owner's choice whether to set one); the Hermes adapter reports no spend, so the meters show 0 cents for it; the dispatch form offers every model to every agent (M1a); posting the reply is three shell calls through `PAPERCLIP_API_URL`, even when the task says to run no command; with five agents parked, the hub is crowded in the overview; and JC-16 (agy, in progress) and JC-1 (Orchestrator, to do) are old open tasks in Paperclip. The brief was: Run Paperclip, the bridge and the viewer at `/?live&bridge=...`, create a task for Hermes from the drawer, watch it work in the room, and read its comment in the thread. It writes to Paperclip and spends a few cents on Hermes, so it needs the owner's yes. The sandbox project is used, not this repository. Models for this test and for any later live test that spends money (the owner's choice, 2026-10-19): Hermes and OpenCode on MiniMax M3.1 Flash Free at high reasoning (in OpenCode `opencode-go/minimax-m3.1-flash-free`; in Hermes `minimax/minimax-m3.1-flash:free` with `--provider openrouter`; it is on OpenRouter's public model list on 2026-10-19 but missing from Hermes' cached list, so pass the id directly), with `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017` as the fallback when the free model is refused or rate limited (in OpenCode `opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash`, which needs Global regions in the OpenCode Go privacy settings); Claude Code on `claude-haiku-5-5` at medium effort; agy on Gemini 3.8 Flash at medium, and only that model. Paperclip does not fall back by itself, so a switch to the fallback is made by hand and said in the report. Setting these models on the Paperclip agents is part of the owner's yes for the test. Before the run, read each agent's model in Paperclip and tell the owner which model each will use.

## Briefs from the October audit

Written 2026-10-20 with `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md`. They aim at the routing policy in the dispatch form (C4b, C4c, C4d), a planner that splits a goal into briefs (C5a, C5b, C5c), the outcome log feeding policy changes as reviewed diffs (C6c, C6d), outcome records for AGY's hand-run tasks (C6e), subagents in the room once S1 has recorded them (S2a, S3a, S2b), and three audit findings left open (U5a, U5b, U5c). Order: C4d and C4b before C4c (which also needs M1a); C5a, then C5b, then C5c; C6c before C6d; S1 before S2a, S2a before S3a and S2b; U5b before U5c. C6e and U5a stand alone.

**C4b. Route suggestion through the bridge and the control client (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.108).** The routing policy (C4a) is pure and nothing calls it. Add `POST /api/control/route` in a new `server/policy.js`, wired in `server/index.js` beside the other control routes. The body is `{ profile, failures }`. It reads `docs/agents/policy.json` from the briefs folder the way `server/runs.js` reads `lanes.json` and checks it with `readPolicy` from `src/policy/route.js` (do not change that file). It answers `{ tier, choice, reason }` from `chooseRoute`, with `quotas` from Paperclip's `GET /api/companies/:id/costs/quota-windows` (the `quota` list of `mapCosts` in `server/control.js`) and `spent` as `{ companyMonthlyUsd }` from `costs/summary` (`spendCents` over 100). When the meters feature is off or Paperclip does not answer either call, route without that part and add ` Quota and spend were not read.` to the reason. Refusals, each 400 unless named: the file is missing (404 `The policy file is missing.`), the file is bad (the `readPolicy` sentence), `profile` is not an object (`Describe the task first.`), `failures` is present and not a whole number from 0 to 5 (`Failures must be a whole number from 0 to 5.`). The route only reads, so it needs no `confirmed`. **C4d. A task profile read from a brief (Hermes, merged as 0.1.105).** The policy routes a profile, but nobody writes one. Add a pure `profileFromBrief(brief)` in a new `src/policy/profile.js` that reads a brief from `parseBriefs` and answers `{ files, hasTests, newTest, clarity, reversible, touchesSecrets, touchesDeploy, touchesData, kind }`. `files` counts the backticked paths after `Files:` in the brief's text, leaving out `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md` and paths under `tests/`. `hasTests` is true when the text names a test file or says `Tests in`. `newTest` is true when it says `a new` right before a `tests/` path. `clarity` is `unclear` when the brief has no `Files:` list or names no test, `clear` otherwise. `reversible` is false when the text says `needs the owner's yes`, `writes to Paperclip` or `spends`. `touchesSecrets` is true for any of `server/index.js`, `src/assistant/intent.js`, `src/assistant/actions.js` or `.env.example`; `touchesDeploy` for `package.json`, `.gitignore` or a path under `.github/`; `touchesData` for a path under `public/fixtures/` or `public/recordings/`; `kind` is `design` when the owner is AGY and the text names `by eye`, else `change`. Then extend `tierFor` in `src/policy/route.js`: `newTest: true` is never small, and `kind: 'design'` or `touchesData: true` is large, as CONTROL-PLANE.md asks; every other rule stays. No randomness. (2) The free lane: the Run brief button in `src/ui/panels/DispatchPanel.jsx` uses `ownerToLane`, so an OpenCode brief always goes to `opencode`, and the bridge refuses it while that lane runs, though `opencode2` is free. Add a pure `runLaneFor(brief, running, lanes)` to `src/ui/panels/briefs.js` that takes the brief's owner lane and, when that lane is running and another lane of the same harness (the same name with or without a trailing number, such as `opencode` and `opencode2`) is free, gives that lane instead; with both busy it keeps the owner lane, so the bridge's own refusal shows. DispatchPanel reads the running lanes from the runs list it already loads, and the confirm card names the lane it will use. Tests: `tests/run-lane.test.js` (a retired lane refused, and run with `--force`), `tests/bridge.test.js` (`POST /api/control/runs` refuses a retired lane with 400), and `tests/panels-briefs.test.js` for `runLaneFor` (owner lane free, owner lane busy and the other free, both busy, a lane with no twin, a brief with no owner). **U18. One runner replaces a stale lock, and nothing starts by its bare name (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.159).** From `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md` (N10, N11, and what U5d left of N9). (1) N9: `takeLaneLock` in `scripts/lane-lock.mjs` reads a dead holder, deletes the lock and takes it again with `wx`. Two runners that both read the same dead holder can still both start, because the second one's delete removes the lock the first one has just taken. Before it deletes a stale lock, a runner takes `LANE.lock.takeover` with `wx`. Under it, the runner reads the lock again and deletes and retakes it only when it still names the same dead process; otherwise it refuses with the sentence it gives today for a running lane. It removes the takeover file in every case. A runner that finds the takeover file refuses with the running-lane sentence while the takeover's holder is alive; when that holder is dead, it deletes the takeover file and refuses with `Another start of the LANE lane stopped half way. Start it again.` (2) N10: `buildCommand` in `scripts/candidate.mjs` falls back to `npm run build` through a shell when neither `npm_execpath` nor `npm-cli.js` beside Node exists, and cmd.exe then looks in the lane worktree first. Throw instead, before anything starts, with `npm was not found. Run this as npm run candidate.` (3) N11: `resolveOpencodeBinary` in `server/assistant.js` returns the bare name `opencode` when the shim is not found, and Windows then runs an `opencode.exe` from the bridge's folder first. After `OPENCODE_BIN` and the shim search, use `programPath('opencode', { platform, env })` from `server/programPath.js`, and return null when nothing is found. `runOpencode` rejects at once on null without starting anything, so the route gives its existing 502. Tests: `tests/lane-lock.test.js` (two takers of one stale lock, with a hook between the read and the delete: exactly one wins; a takeover file held by a live process is refused; a stale takeover file is removed and the start refused with the new sentence; the takeover file is gone afterwards), `tests/candidate.test.js` (the refusal, with an `execPath` whose folder has no `npm-cli.js` and no `npm_execpath`), and `tests/assistant-route.test.js` (an absolute path from PATH through a `check`, null when nothing is found on Windows or Linux, `OPENCODE_BIN` first; change the two expectations of `'opencode'`). Mark N9, N10 and N11 fixed in the audit table. Files: `scripts/lane-lock.mjs`, `scripts/candidate.mjs`, `server/assistant.js`, `tests/lane-lock.test.js`, `tests/candidate.test.js`, `tests/assistant-route.test.js`, `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**C5a. Planner prompt and plan reader (Hermes, merged as 0.1.107).** The first part of C5: a planner model splits a goal into briefs, and our code checks them before anyone sees them. Add a pure `src/briefs/plan.js`. `planPrompt({ goal, lanes, rules, ids })` builds the planner's prompt: the goal; the house rules from `parseRules`; for each lane its key, name, role and forbidden paths; the ids already used (`ids`, from `parseBriefs`); and the answer format, one JSON object `{ "briefs": [{ "id", "title", "owner", "size", "files", "tests", "text", "dependsOn" }] }`, with the style rules (one paragraph, plain words, no em dashes, exact sentences in backticks, tests by name, at most 8 briefs). `parsePlan(text, { lanes, ids })` reads the first JSON object in the text and answers `{ briefs, problems }`, where `briefs` holds only the briefs with no problem and `problems` holds these sentences: `The plan is not JSON.`, `The plan has no briefs.`, `The plan has more than 8 briefs.`, `Brief N has no id like C7a.` (N counts from 1), `Brief ID uses an id that is taken.`, `Brief ID appears twice.`, `Brief ID names an owner that is not a lane: OWNER.`, `Brief ID has no size of small, medium or large.`, `Brief ID lists no files.`, `Brief ID lets OWNER touch PATH, which lanes.json forbids.`, `Brief ID names no test.`, `Brief ID has an em dash.`, `Brief ID depends on OTHER, which is not in the plan.` and `The plan has a loop in its dependencies.` Read lanes and ids with `Object.hasOwn`. `pathAllowed(path, forbidden)` answers false for a path under a `dir/**` entry or equal to a plain entry. `briefMarkdown(brief, lanes)` writes `**ID. Title (Name).** text` and adds ` Files: ` and the backticked files when the text has no `Files:`. Tests in a new `tests/plan.test.js`: each sentence, a good plan, `pathAllowed` on the real `lanes.json`, a plan of two briefs where one is bad (the good one is kept), and `parseBriefs(briefMarkdown(brief, lanes))` giving back the id, title and owner. Files: `src/briefs/plan.js`, `tests/plan.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**C5b. Ask the planner through Paperclip (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.113).** After C5a. The planner is a Paperclip agent with the role `pm`, hired by the owner with the agent builder. Add `POST /api/control/plans` in a new `server/plans.js`, wired in `server/index.js`. The body is `{ goal, confirmed }`. Refusals, each 400 unless named: not confirmed (`Confirm the plan request first.`), a goal that is not text of 1 to 2000 characters (`Write the goal in 1 to 2000 characters.`), no agent with the role `pm` (404 `Hire a planner first: an agent with the role pm.`), more than one (`More than one agent has the role pm. Keep one.`), a waiting or terminated planner (`That agent cannot take tasks yet.`). It builds the prompt with `planPrompt` from `TASKS.md`, `HOUSE-RULES.md` and `lanes.json`, creates a Paperclip issue for the planner (title `Plan: ` and the first 60 characters of the goal, the prompt as the description, priority medium, no overrides) and answers 201 `{ issueId, identifier }`. `GET /api/control/plans/:issueId` reads the thread and answers `{ status, briefs, problems }`: `waiting` with empty lists while no comment from the planner holds a `{`, else `ready` with `parsePlan` run on the newest such comment. Both routes use the tasks feature and the time limit of the other control routes. Add `src/briefs/plan.js` to `files` in `package.json`. A real try writes to Paperclip and spends the planner's budget, so it needs the owner's yes and is not part of this brief. Tests in a new `tests/plans.test.js` against the fake Paperclip of `tests/control-contract.test.js` (each refusal with nothing created, the issue body, waiting, ready, a comment that is not JSON), a shared contract case, and `npm run pack-check` passing. **C5c. Plan tab in the control drawer (OpenCode, moved from AGY, merged as 0.1.133).** After C5b, C4b and C4d. **C6c. Policy changes proposed from the outcome table (Hermes, merged as 0.1.111).** Step 3 of the decision layer in CONTROL-PLANE.md: the outcome log suggests a policy change as a diff for the owner to approve. Add a pure `src/policy/proposals.js`. `proposePolicy({ policy, rows, minDecided = 10, margin = 0.15 })` takes a policy from `readPolicy` and the rows of `outcomeTable` (`OutcomeRow` in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`), finds the row of each choice by lane and model, and in each tier looks at each choice and the one after it: when both have at least `minDecided` decided runs (merged plus rejected), the later one's merge rate is at least `margin` above the earlier one's, and its cost per merge is not higher (a null cost counts as highest), it swaps the two, at most one swap per tier. It never adds, removes or edits a choice, never moves a choice whose lane is `owner`, and never touches `limits` or `quotaSkipPercent`. It answers `{ policy, changes, notes }`, with merge rates as whole percents and costs to the cent: a change reads `Move LANE on MODEL ahead of LANE on MODEL in the TIER tier: 92% merged over 12 decided runs against 70% over 15, $0.03 a merge against $0.05.` and a note reads `The TIER tier keeps its order: LANE on MODEL has N decided runs, fewer than 10.` The answer passes `readPolicy`. `policyText(policy)` is `JSON.stringify(policy, null, 2)` and a newline, and `policyDiff(beforeText, afterText)` answers a unified diff with the headers `--- a/docs/agents/policy.json` and `+++ b/docs/agents/policy.json` and three lines of context, so `git apply` takes it, or an empty string when the texts match. No randomness. Tests in a new `tests/policy-proposals.test.js`: a swap, each reason for no swap (too few runs, a small margin, a higher cost, the owner), one swap per tier, limits left alone, `policyText` of the real `docs/agents/policy.json` equal to the file (so diffs stay small), and the diff checked with `git apply --check` in a throwaway repo. Files: `src/policy/proposals.js`, `tests/policy-proposals.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**C6d. Write a policy proposal as a patch to review (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.115).** After C6c. Add `scripts/policy-propose.mjs`, run as `npm run policy-propose -- [--since YYYY-MM-DD] [--min N]` (add it to `package.json`). It reads `runs.jsonl` and `outcomes.jsonl` with the readers in `server/runs.js`, keeps the runs since the date with `filterRunsSince` (a bad date prints `SINCE_PROBLEM` and exits 1), builds `outcomeTable`, reads `docs/agents/policy.json` with `readPolicy` (a bad file prints its sentence and exits 1) and calls `proposePolicy` with `--min` as `minDecided` (a whole number from 1 to 1000, else `--min needs a whole number from 1 to 1000.`). It prints each change and each note. With a change it writes the `policyDiff` to `policy-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.patch` in the runs folder (`%TEMP%\machineroom-runs\`) and prints `Patch: PATH. Read it, then run git apply PATH in the main checkout.`; with none it prints `No change to propose.` and writes nothing. It never writes `docs/agents/policy.json`: a policy change stays a reviewed diff. Tests in a new `tests/policy-propose.test.js`, with the runs folder pointed at a `makeTempDir` folder the way `tests/outcomes.test.js` does: records that justify a swap (the patch is written, named in the output and passes `git apply --check` against a copy of the policy file), records that do not (`No change to propose.` and no file), a bad `--since`, a bad `--min`, and the real policy file left unchanged. Files: `scripts/policy-propose.mjs`, `package.json`, `tests/policy-propose.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**C6e. Record AGY's hand-run tasks (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.106).** AGY is started by hand, so its runs leave no record in `runs.jsonl`, and `npm run outcome -- BRANCH merged` refuses its branches with `No lane run made the branch BRANCH.`. Add `scripts/hand-run.mjs`, run as `npm run hand-run -- LANE ID --minutes N [--model TEXT] [--branch NAME] [--exit CODE]` (add it to `package.json`). It reads `lanes.json` and `TASKS.md` and refuses, writing nothing: a lane that is not in `lanes.json` (read with `Object.hasOwn`; `There is no lane named LANE.`), a lane with a command line (`The LANE lane runs through npm run run-lane, which records it already.`), a brief that `briefProblems` refuses for that lane, called with `branchExists: () => false` because AGY's branch exists by then (print its sentences), minutes that are not a number from 1 to 600 (`--minutes needs a number from 1 to 600.`), a branch of `main` (`The worktree is on main. Pass --branch.`) and a branch that a run record has already (`A run record for BRANCH exists already.`). The branch is `--branch` or the lane worktree's current branch, read with `gitPath()` from `server/programPath.js`. It appends `runRecord(...)` from `src/briefs/run.js` with the brief, the model from `--model` or `modelFor(lane, ID)`, no reasoning, a start `minutes` before now, `--exit` (default 0), no cost (AGY reports none), no log and the commits of `refs/heads/main..HEAD` in the worktree, plus `source: 'hand'`, and prints `Recorded LANE ID on BRANCH. Record the review with npm run outcome -- BRANCH merged or rejected.` Also make `scripts/agy-prompt.mjs` print one last line, `When AGY is done: npm run hand-run -- agy ID --minutes N`. Tests in a new `tests/hand-run.test.js`, with the runs folder pointed at a `makeTempDir` folder and a throwaway git repo with a worktree: each refusal, a record written and then accepted by `scripts/outcome.mjs`, and the record counted by `outcomeTable`. Files: `scripts/hand-run.mjs`, `scripts/agy-prompt.mjs`, `package.json`, `tests/hand-run.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S2a. Subagents in the activity schema and the room state (Hermes, replaced by S2c).** Start only after S1 is merged: its report says which signal marks a child in each harness and its recordings are in `public/fixtures/`. Add two activities. `agent.spawn` has `agent` (the parent key), `child` (the child key), `name` and `label`; the child key is the parent key, `~` and a number that counts from 1 within the parent's run, so a recording always gives the same keys. `agent.end` has `agent` (the child key) and `ok`. A child's tool calls are ordinary activities with `agent` set to the child key and the parent's `runId`. In `src/model/state.js`, `agent.spawn` adds the child to the roster with the fields `createState` gives every agent plus `parent` and `depth` (the parent's depth plus one; a top agent has depth 0), status `working`; a spawn deeper than 3 adds nothing, and that child's later activities go to its deepest allowed parent. `agent.end` sets the child to `done` or `error`. The parent's `run.end` ends every child of that run still working, with the parent's result. A seek rebuilds the children from the activities, as it rebuilds everything else. `player.snapshot()` gives each agent its `parent` (null for a top agent) and `depth`. List both activities in your report so the orchestrator can add them to the schema table in `ARCHITECTURE.md`. Tests in a new `tests/state-subagents.test.js`: a spawn, two children with their keys, a child's file touch, the depth limit, the parent's end closing a child, a seek to the middle, and the snapshot fields. Files: `src/model/state.js`, `src/model/player.js`, `tests/state-subagents.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S3a. Subagent spawns read from each harness (Hermes, replaced by S2c).** After S1 and S2a. In the parsers in `src/model/parsers/`, emit `agent.spawn` and `agent.end` (S2a) from the signal S1's report names for each harness: the Claude Code Task tool, OpenCode's `task` tool, Hermes' `delegate_task`, and agy if S1 found its child in the stream. Give a child's tool calls the child key when the harness says which call belongs to the child; when it does not, emit only the spawn and the end, keep the calls on the parent and say so in your report. Do not guess a signal S1 did not record. `createPipeline` passes the parser the parent key, so the child keys come out as S2a asks. The unread-line counter in `src/model/parsers/unreadable.js` must not count a spawn line. Tests: in `tests/parsers.test.js` one spawn and one end per harness from short lines taken from S1's recordings; in `tests/scenarios.test.js` each S1 recording replayed, with the number of children and the first file each child touches written as numbers and paths; list every changed scenario line and why. Files: `src/model/parsers/*.js`, `src/model/pipeline.js`, `tests/parsers.test.js`, `tests/scenarios.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S2b. Subagents drawn in the room (AGY, replaced by S2d).** After S2a. Keep the room deterministic and calm under `prefers-reduced-motion`. When the state gains a child, the room draws it as the next spare shape from `src/engine/shapes.js` at 0.6 of its parent's size, appearing at the parent and then travelling the strings to the folders it touches like any agent. A thin line in the parent's colour joins the child to its parent while both work. The child's terminal chip title reads `PARENT > NAME` and its status ring follows the same colours. When the child ends it moves back to its parent and fades out over 1.5 seconds (at once under reduced motion). A child of a child is drawn the same way at 0.6 of its own parent. In the HUD agent list, a child sits under its parent, indented by 16px, with a `subagent` tag; Log and the follow keys work for it as for any agent; the event log names it `PARENT > NAME`. Keep the DOM contract classes, add any new ones and list them in your report for `ARCHITECTURE.md`. Check by eye in replay with each S1 recording, at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768: a child splitting off, its line, its chip, follow mode on it, its return, and reduced motion on; orbit to check that the line hides behind cards. Measure the frame time with a recording at 4x before and after, and give both. Tests: the engine tests pass, and a new `tests/subagent-shapes.test.js` covers a pure `childShapeFor(childKey, parentShape)` (the same key gives the same shape and scale) and the HUD row order. Files: `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/engine/shapes.js`, `src/engine/terminals.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, `tests/subagent-shapes.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U5a. Name the pages the bridge allows, and confirm a lane run (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.104).** From the audit (N1 and N3 in `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md`): the bridge lets a page on any local port use every route, and `POST /api/control/runs` starts a paid run without `confirmed`. In `server/index.js`, `isAllowed(origin)` allows only `http://localhost:PORT` and `http://127.0.0.1:PORT` where PORT is the bridge's own port (the real one after `listen`, also with `--port 0`), the Vite port 5173, and each origin in `MACHINEROOM_ORIGINS`, a comma list of `http://localhost:N` or `http://127.0.0.1:N`; the bridge prints `MACHINEROOM_ORIGINS: skipped TEXT.` once for each other entry and ignores it. Keep the Host check as it is. Any other Origin gets 403 as today. In `server/runs.js`, a run needs `confirmed: true` (400 `Confirm the run first.`), and `startRun` in `src/control/client.js` sends it, as `mergeReview` does; the mock refuses without it. Add `MACHINEROOM_ORIGINS` with a comment line to `.env.example`. This changes a protected check: the orchestrator asks for the second look before merging and updates the known limit in `ARCHITECTURE.md`. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js`: the own port with both host names, 5173, a port from the list, port 3000 refused, a bad list entry skipped with its line, and the run refused without `confirmed`; the contract suite's run cases still pass. Files: `server/index.js`, `server/runs.js`, `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, `.env.example`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `tests/control-contract.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U5b. A key masker for text that leaves the bridge (Hermes, merged as 0.1.103).** From the audit (N2): the event stream passes raw agent output to the page, so a key an agent prints reaches every allowed page. The assistant's `redact` is too broad for this (it hides any 32 character token, so git shas and run ids would go too). Add `maskKeys(text)` to `src/briefs/audit.js`, beside `findLeaks`, using the same key shapes `findLeaks` reports as `key`: `sk-` tokens, Google keys, GitHub tokens, AWS key ids, and the value of a key setting that is not a placeholder. It answers `{ text, count }`, with each match replaced by `[key]` and the setting's name kept. Pure, and masking twice gives the same text. Tests in `tests/history-audit.test.js`: each shape masked and counted, two keys on one line, a 40 character git sha, a UUID and a placeholder setting left alone, and masking twice. Files: `src/briefs/audit.js`, `tests/history-audit.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U5c. Mask keys in the frames the bridge keeps and sends (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.110).** After U5b. In `accept()` in `server/index.js`, after `countUnreadable` has read the frame, run `maskKeys` on `payload.chunk`, `payload.message` and `payload.error` when they are text, before the frame goes into the history or to any client; a restored long chunk passes the same way. `/api/health` gains `masked: { total, since }`. The bridge never logs the text it masked. Add `src/briefs/audit.js` to `files` in `package.json` so the pack keeps it. The proxy route has no bridge and stays as it is; say so in the report. Tests in `tests/bridge.test.js`: a chunk with an `sk-or-v1-` key reaches `/events` with `[key]`, `/events?history=1` and a `Last-Event-ID` replay give the masked frame, the health count rises, a tool line in the same chunk still parses in the viewer's pipeline, and `npm run pack-check` passes. Files: `server/index.js`, `package.json`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Briefs from the UI audit

**U6. The review diff names the head it shows (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.123).** From the UI audit (N1 in `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-UI.md`): the Reviews panel reads a lane's diff and its head in two separate requests, so a lane that commits between them shows the owner one diff while the confirm card merges another head. In `laneDiff` in `server/reviews.js`, read `git rev-parse HEAD` in the lane worktree first, then diff against that sha instead of the moving branch (`refs/heads/main...SHA`, still with `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`), and answer it as `head` beside `lane`, `branch`, `files`, `patch` and `truncated`. The mock's `reviewDiff` answers a `head` too, and `ReviewDiff` in `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md` names it. Tests in `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js`: the answer's `head` equals the lane's head, a commit made after the head was read is not in the patch, and the contract suite checks `head` on both clients. Files: `server/reviews.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js`, `tests/control-contract.test.js`, `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U6a. Merge the head whose diff the owner read (OpenCode, moved from AGY, merged as 0.1.131).** After U6. In `src/ui/panels/ReviewsPanel.jsx`, when the lane's diff is open, the confirm card shows and sends `diff.head` (its first 7 characters in the card text), not the head from the list. When the list's head for that lane differs from `diff.head`, the Merge button in the row opens no card and the row shows `The branch moved since you read the diff. Open it again.` in the error style. With no diff open, the card keeps the list's head as today. Add a pure `mergeHead(row, diff)` to `src/ui/panels/reviews.js` that answers `{ head }` or `{ error }` with that sentence. Tests in `tests/reviews-panel.test.js`: `mergeHead` with no diff, a matching diff, a moved branch and a diff of another lane; the rendered card shows the diff's short head. Check by eye with `npm run dev -- --port 5181` and the mock client, at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768: open a diff, open the card, read the short head; then change the mock's head and check the sentence. Files: `src/ui/panels/ReviewsPanel.jsx`, `src/ui/panels/reviews.js`, `tests/reviews-panel.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U7. One broken panel must not blank the room (OpenCode, moved from AGY, merged as 0.1.129).** From the UI audit (N2): the app has no error boundary, so an answer of an unexpected shape that makes a drawer panel throw (a comment `body` that is an object, an issue list that is not a list) unmounts the whole page, room included. Add `src/ui/panels/PanelBoundary.jsx`, a class component with `static getDerivedStateFromError(error)` that answers `{ error }`, and wrap the panel of each tab in `ControlDrawer.jsx` with it, keyed by the tab so a new tab starts clean. Its fallback is one line in the error style: `This panel stopped: MESSAGE. Pick another tab, then come back.`, where MESSAGE is the error's message cut to 120 characters, or `no message`. Export a pure `panelStoppedText(error)` for the sentence. Tests in a new `tests/panel-boundary.test.js`: `panelStoppedText` with an error, a long message and `null`; `getDerivedStateFromError` answers the error; the drawer still renders each tab with the mock client. Check by eye at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768: make one panel throw on purpose in a local edit you do not commit, see the line, switch tabs and back, and see the room still moving behind the drawer. Files: `src/ui/panels/PanelBoundary.jsx`, `src/ui/panels/ControlDrawer.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, `tests/panel-boundary.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U8. The control client opens its change stream again (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.124).** From the UI audit (N3): `createClient` in `src/control/client.js` opens `/events` once for the panels' change notices. When the bridge answers that request with anything but a 200 event stream, the browser closes it for good, `source` stays set, and no panel hears a change again until a reload. Add an `error` listener: when `readyState` is 2, drop the source and, while any listener is subscribed, open a new one after 1 second, doubling up to 15 seconds and back to 1 second after an `open` event; `subscribe`'s unsubscribe of the last listener cancels a waiting reopen. Take `setTimer` and `clearTimer` as options like `openStream` does. Tests in a new `tests/control-client-stream.test.js` with a fake EventSource: the delays 1, 2, 4, 8, 15 and 15 seconds, the reset after `open`, no reopen with no listener, and the cancelled reopen. Files: `src/control/client.js`, `tests/control-client-stream.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U9. Cut long agent names in the room (OpenCode, moved from AGY, merged as 0.1.130).** From the UI audit (N4): the name label under each agent is a canvas exactly as wide as the name. An agent named with 2,000 characters in Paperclip asks for a canvas over 32,767 pixels wide, which the browser refuses, and a long name already runs over its neighbours. Add a pure `nameLabel(name)` to `src/engine/agents.js` that answers the name in capitals cut to 32 characters with `~` as the last one, and an empty text for a name that is not text; use it for the label. The terminal chip title cuts its text with CSS ellipsis at the chip width. Tests in a new `tests/agent-label.test.js`: a short name, a name of exactly 32 characters, a long one, and `null`. Check by eye at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768 with a local edit to `public/fixtures/agents.json` that you do not commit, giving one agent a 60 character name: the label and the chip title stay short and readable. Files: `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/styles.css`, `tests/agent-label.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U10. Show hidden direction and control characters in names (Hermes, merged as 0.1.122).** From the UI audit (N5): file names, task titles and diff lines reach the page as text, which is safe from markup, but a right-to-left override (U+202E) or another bidirectional control can make a name or a code line read differently from what it is, and the Reviews panel is where the owner decides on a merge. Add a pure `visibleText(text)` to `src/model/paths.js` that replaces U+200E, U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069 and the C0 controls except tab with `[U+XXXX]` (four upper-case hex digits), and returns other text unchanged. Tests in `tests/paths.test.js`: each listed character, a tab kept, plain and non-Latin text unchanged, and a mixed line. Files: `src/model/paths.js`, `tests/paths.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U10a. Use visibleText in the cards and the Reviews panel (OpenCode, moved from AGY, merged as 0.1.132).** After U10. Pass file names through `visibleText` in the card rows and names (`src/engine/cards.js`), and the diff file paths and every patch line in `src/ui/panels/ReviewsPanel.jsx`. Do not change the text sent with a merge. Tests in `tests/reviews-panel.test.js`: a patch line holding U+202E renders with `[U+202E]`. Check by eye at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768 with the mock client and a patch line you add to the mock in a local edit you do not commit. Files: `src/engine/cards.js`, `src/ui/panels/ReviewsPanel.jsx`, `tests/reviews-panel.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U11. Two small fixes the UI audit left open (Hermes, merged as 0.1.145).** From `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-UI.md` (N7 and N10). (1) N7: `src/App.jsx` reads `?simulate&speed=` as `Number(params.get('speed')) || 1`, so a negative speed plays every frame at once and a tiny one such as `1e-300` makes a gap over the browser's timer limit, which also fires at once. Add a pure exported `simulatedSpeed(value)` to `src/live/simulated.js` that returns 1 for anything that is not a finite number above 0 and otherwise clamps it to 0.25 through 64, and use it in `src/App.jsx` in place of the current expression. (2) N10: `src/ui/Assistant.jsx` keeps every log entry for as long as the page is open. Add an exported `MAX_ENTRIES = 200` and a pure exported `appendEntries(list, ...items)` that returns the list with the items added, keeping only the newest `MAX_ENTRIES`, and use it in every `setEntries((prev) => [...prev, ...])` call; the calls that map `prev` to replace an entry stay as they are. Tests: `simulatedSpeed` in `tests/simulated.test.js` for `'3'`, `'0'`, `'-2'`, `'abc'`, `'1e-300'`, `'1e9'`, `''` and `null`; `appendEntries` in `tests/assistant-ui.test.js` (order kept, the oldest dropped past 200, the list passed in not changed). Files: `src/live/simulated.js`, `src/App.jsx`, `src/ui/Assistant.jsx`, `tests/simulated.test.js`, `tests/assistant-ui.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U12. The card plan key without a walk over every file (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.146).** From the UI audit (N9). `update` in `src/engine/cards.js` calls `recompute(state)` on every frame, and `recompute` calls `viewInput(state, pinned)`, which walks every file in `state.files` to build the list of existing files, while the key only reads the list's length; the list is needed only when the key changed. On a 40000 file project that is a walk and a new array of 40000 paths per frame. Keep a count instead: `createState` in `src/model/state.js` sets `state.existingFiles` to the number of files it makes with `exists` true, and `apply` adds one where a file's `exists` turns from false to true (check that no other place sets `exists` or adds to `state.files` with `exists` true, and name what you checked). Add a pure exported `viewKey(state, pinned)` to `cards.js` that builds the same key as today from `state.existingFiles`, the focus and the touched list without walking `state.files`, and call `viewInput` only when that key changed. Keep `viewInput` and its signature (`tests/soak.test.js` uses it). The plan must still change when a file is created, when an agent's focus changes, when a pin is added or removed and when a recent path is added or ages out. Tests in `tests/cards.test.js` for `viewKey`: the same key for the same state, a new key after a write creates a file, after a focus change and after a pin, and the same key as the old form for a fixture state; and for `existingFiles` after `createState` with created files and after a write to one of them. `npm run scale-probe -- --replay` must print the same digest before and after your change. Report the time of 1000 calls of the old key and the new key on a 40000 file state. Files: `src/engine/cards.js`, `src/model/state.js`, `tests/cards.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U13. The bridge reads the file tree without blocking (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.149).** From the security audit (N14 in `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md`). `readTree` in `server/index.js` runs `git ls-files` through `execFileSync` with a 20 second limit, and the bridge calls it on every file change (300 ms after the last one, from `checkTreeChange`), at start (`startWatcher`) and on each `GET /api/tree`. While git runs, the event stream, the history replay and every route wait; on a large project during a run that is most of the time. Make `readTree` return a promise: run git through `execFile` with the same arguments and options (`gitPath()`, `-z`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, the buffer and time limits, `windowsHide`), and fall back to `walkFiles` on any error, as today. In the bridge keep at most one tree read in flight: a file change during a read marks the tree dirty, and one more read runs after the current one ends, however many changes came in. `GET /api/tree` awaits a read (it may share the one in flight). After each await, check `stopped` so a stopped bridge writes nothing. Check every reader of `lastFiles` and say in the report what each one sees before the first read ends. Let `createBridge` take an optional `readTree` in its options for tests, defaulting to the real one. Update `scripts/scale-probe.mjs` so its `readTree` stage awaits the read (its numbers may change; its `--replay` digest must not), and `tests/tree.test.js` and `tests/scale.test.js` to await. Tests in a new `tests/tree-read.test.js`: with a `readTree` that resolves only when the test lets it, `GET /api/health` answers while a read is pending; five file changes during one pending read lead to exactly one more read; a bridge stopped during a read writes no `tree` event; and `GET /api/tree` answers the tree from the read. Point every test bridge at the dead Paperclip port 3999. Files: `server/index.js`, `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `tests/tree.test.js`, `tests/scale.test.js`, `tests/tree-read.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U14. Mask a key that Paperclip splits across two chunks (Claude, merged as 0.1.152).** From the UI audit (N6 in `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-UI.md`) and the owner's rule that a key never reaches a page. `maskFrame` in `server/index.js` runs `maskKeys` (`src/briefs/audit.js`) on each frame's `chunk`, `message` and `error` by itself, and `maskKeys` works line by line. So when a run prints a key and Paperclip cuts the log between two `heartbeat.run.log` chunks (`sk-abc` in one, the rest in the next; or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY=` in one and its value in the next), neither half matches, and the whole key reaches the page, the 500 frame history and `/events?history=1`. Make the bridge mask across the cut. One way, yours to change if you find a better one: keep per run and stream the frame whose last line ends in text that could still grow into a key (a prefix of one of the key patterns, or a key setting name with its `=` or `:` and no value yet), hold it and every later frame (frames stay in order, as `dispatch` and `drain` already keep them while a cut chunk is restored), and when the run's next chunk on that stream arrives, mask the held line's tail joined to the new chunk's head, put `[key]` in the frame where the key starts and drop the rest of the key from the next one. Release a held frame unchanged after at most 1 second, at the run's end, when the next chunk shows the tail was not a key, or once the tail is longer than any key can be. Keep: the number of frames, their ids, `seq`, order and every field but the chunk text; the text outside a masked key exactly as it was; the `masked` count in `/api/health`; the restore of chunks over 8192 characters (mask after the restore); and no hold at all for a chunk whose tail cannot start a key. Put the logic in a pure, tested module (a new `server/mask.js`, or `src/briefs/audit.js` if it fits there) and keep `server/index.js` to the wiring. Tests: the pure module (a key split at every position of an `sk-` key, a GitHub token and a key setting value; a key split across three chunks; two runs interleaved; stdout and stderr kept apart; a tail that turns out not to be a key released unchanged; the 1 second release with a fake clock; the run end releases); the bridge with a fake upstream socket as `tests/bridge.test.js` does, Paperclip on the dead port 3999, showing the page and `/events?history=1` get `[key]` and never the raw key; and every recording in `public/recordings/*-run.jsonl` and `public/fixtures/*-run.jsonl` passed through the module with chunks cut at seeded random points gives the same joined text as `maskKeys` on the whole text, and the same activities from `buildActivities` as the frames before masking. Report the share of chunks held and the longest hold over those recordings. The proxy route (no bridge) still masks nothing; keep that known limit and update the line about chunks in Known limits in `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Files: `server/index.js`, `server/mask.js` (new) or `src/briefs/audit.js`, `tests/mask-split.test.js` (new), `tests/bridge.test.js` or a new bridge test file, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (that Known limits line), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`; name any other file in the report and why.

**U15. A quoted `>` is not a write, and the longer path wins (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.154).** From the security audit (N13 in `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md`), in `touchesForCommand` in `src/model/commands.js`. (1) `WRITE_OPERATOR` is searched in the whole segment, so a `>` inside quotes (`grep "<div>" index.html`, `rg 'a>b' src/x.js`) turns a read into a write, and the room flashes a file as written that nobody wrote. Look for the write operator outside quoted text, keeping indexes the same (blank the quoted spans in a copy). The quoted text that is itself a command must still count: `powershell -Command "Set-Content notes.md x"`, `pwsh -c "..."`, `bash -c "echo hi > out.txt"`, `sh -c '...'` and `cmd /c "..."` still write. (2) A known file that is the end of another known path is touched too: with `src/a.js` and `lib/src/a.js` both in the tree, `cat lib/src/a.js` touches both. When two known paths match in one segment and one match lies inside the other, keep only the longer one; a command that names both on their own still touches both, and an absolute path (`C:/proj/src/a.js`, where no longer known path contains the match) still touches `src/a.js`. Do the same for the folders a lister names. Tests in `tests/commands.test.js` for each case above, plus the existing tests unchanged. Run `npm run scale-probe -- --replay` before and after: for every recording whose digest changes, list each activity that changed with its command and why (a one-off script you do not commit is fine), and change nothing else. Files: `src/model/commands.js`, `tests/commands.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U16. The control client gives up on a request that never answers (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.154).** From the security audit (N15). `request` in `src/control/client.js` calls `fetchImpl` with no time limit, so a bridge that takes a request and never answers leaves a panel on its loading state until the page reloads. Give every request a time limit through an `AbortController` whose abort is scheduled with the injected `setTimer` and cleared with `clearTimer` (so tests drive it with no real time), passed to `fetchImpl` as `signal` and kept until the body is read: 30 seconds by default, and for each method whose bridge route can take longer, a limit a little above that route's own limit (read them in `server/`, for example the backup's 120 seconds, the merge's git calls, the assistant and the decision model, and name each one in the report). A request that runs out rejects with `The bridge did not answer within N seconds.`, which the panels show as they show other errors. The change stream keeps its own reconnect and gets no limit. Tests in a new `tests/client-timeout.test.js`: a fetch that never resolves rejects with that message at the default limit; a long route gets its longer limit; an answer in time clears the timer; `fetchImpl` gets the `signal` and it is aborted at the limit; and the contract test in `tests/control-contract.test.js` still passes for both clients. Files: `src/control/client.js`, `tests/client-timeout.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md` (one line on the limits), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q1. Security review of what changed since the audits (Claude, merged as 0.1.157).** The October audit (`docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10.md`) and the UI audit (`docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-UI.md`, merged as 0.1.121) read the code as it was then. Since then about 4800 lines went into `server/`, `src/` and `scripts/` (`git diff --stat` from the 0.1.121 merge to 0.1.152). Read that change with the same eyes and the same severity scale, report only. Look first at: `server/plans.js` (`POST /api/control/plans/:issueId/accept` writes planner briefs into `TASKS.md`; what a planner reply can put there, and whether `briefMarkdown` in `src/briefs/plan.js` keeps it to one line and out of other briefs), the live-runs route and its notice (`server/control.js`), the folder-shaped tree and `packTree` (`server/index.js`), the tree read that no longer blocks and its one-read-in-flight state, `createSplitMasker` in `src/briefs/audit.js` and its wiring (can a frame be lost, sent twice, sent out of order, or leak a key when Paperclip reconnects, when a run never ends, or when `stop()` comes while frames are held), the policy route (`server/policy.js`) and the `Route` row and Plan tab in the drawer (`src/ui/panels/`), the cost board (`src/engine/board.js`) and the task line in the callout (text from Paperclip shown in the room), and `scripts/build-fixtures.mjs`, `scripts/hand-run.mjs` and `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`. For each finding: the file and line, what an attacker or a bad answer from Paperclip or a model can do, the severity, and a fix in one or two sentences. Do not fix anything: OpenCode works in `src/model/commands.js` and `src/control/client.js`, and AGY in `src/engine/**`, at the same time. End with the findings worth a brief, written like the briefs in this file, each with its `Files:` list, and with what you checked and found sound. Files: `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-DELTA.md` (new), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P11. Name an OpenCode subagent call by its task (Hermes, merged as 0.1.125).** From S1a: OpenCode's `subagent` tool reaches the room as `subagent {"agent":"general","description":"Count exporte~`, the JSON of its input cut short. In `src/model/parsers/opencode.js`, label a `tool_use` whose `part.tool` is `subagent` as `subagent: DESCRIPTION`, from `part.state.input.description` when it is text, cut as other labels are cut, and as `subagent` when there is none. Keep the tool kind `other`, no touches, and the timing it has today; the `prompt` names files and must not become a file touch. Tests in `tests/parsers.test.js` with a line shaped like the recorded one, `{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":8000,"sessionID":"ses_a","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"subagent","id":"call_1","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"agent":"general","description":"Count exported functions","prompt":"Read src/model/paths.js and src/model/commands.js"},"metadata":{"sessionID":"ses_b"},"time":{"start":1000,"end":8000}}}}`: one start labelled `subagent: Count exported functions` with no touches and one end with ok true; status `error` gives ok false; no description gives `subagent`. ## Briefs from the delta review

**Q1a. The fixture builder refuses every key the audit knows (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.161).** From the delta review (D1 in `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-DELTA.md`): `SECRET` in `scripts/build-fixtures.mjs` misses OpenRouter, Anthropic and OpenAI project keys, `gho_`, `github_pat_`, AWS keys and key settings, so `npm run fixtures` can write a key into `public/fixtures/`, which is committed and shipped in `dist/`. Before writing, run `findLeaks` from `src/briefs/audit.js` on each frame line and refuse with `possible key in frame N; fixture not written` and exit code 2 when it finds a key kind; keep `SECRET` for the bearer, JWT, Slack and e-mail cases. Never print the key. Export the check as a pure `fixtureLeak(lines)` that answers the 1-based frame number or 0. Tests in a new `tests/build-fixtures.test.js`: each of the seven shapes built from pieces (as `tests/review-lane.test.js` builds `'sk-' + 'q'.repeat(40)`: OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI project, `gho_`, `github_pat_`, `AKIA` and an `OPENROUTER_API_KEY=` setting) is refused, a clean frame passes, and the message names the frame and not the key. Files: `scripts/build-fixtures.mjs`, `tests/build-fixtures.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q1b. The planner's briefs are written as the owner read them (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.162).** From the delta review (D2, D3 and D5): a planner title can end the brief head early and change the owner, the merged mark and the `Files:` list that `parseBriefs` reads from `TASKS.md`; test paths skip the `forbidden` check; the Plan tab never shows a brief's text; and the accept route writes whatever the newest planner comment holds at the click. In `src/briefs/plan.js`, `parsePlan` refuses a title with `*`, `(`, `)`, a line break or `Files:` with `Brief ID has a title the brief list cannot read.`, and checks every test path with `pathAllowed`. `briefMarkdown` answers an empty text when `parseBriefs` of its line does not give back the same id, title and owner. In `server/plans.js`, `GET /api/control/plans/:issueId` adds `planId` (the id of the planner comment it read), and the accept body needs that `planId`; when the newest planner comment has another id, answer 409 `The plan changed since you read it. Read it again.`. In `src/ui/panels/PlanPanel.jsx`, each brief shows its text in Space Mono at white 0.7 below its meta line, passed through `visibleText`, and the accept call sends `planId`. The client's `acceptPlan(issueId, ids, planId)` and the mock follow; `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md` names the field. Tests in `tests/plan.test.js` (the title from D2, a test path in `server/**` for a lane that forbids it, the round trip through `parseBriefs`), `tests/plans.test.js` (409 on a newer comment, 201 with the matching `planId`), `tests/plan-panel.test.js` (each brief's text is shown) and the contract suite. The check by eye with the mock at 1280 by 800 is left to the orchestrator. Files: `src/briefs/plan.js`, `server/plans.js`, `src/ui/panels/PlanPanel.jsx`, `src/ui/panels/plan.js`, `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`, `tests/plan.test.js`, `tests/plans.test.js`, `tests/plan-panel.test.js`, `tests/control-contract.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q1c. The split masker starts a new line after a gap (Claude, merged as 0.1.165).** For the cloud session. From the delta review (D4): `createSplitMasker` joins a chunk to the open line it kept even when frames were missed, so a key at the start of the next chunk follows a letter or digit and is not masked, which 0.1.151 masked. In `src/briefs/audit.js`, keep the last `seq` of each run; treat a chunk as the start of a line when its `seq` is not that number plus one, when it has `truncated: true`, or after `reset()`, which drops every kept line and releases what it holds. In `server/index.js`, call `masker.reset()` when the Paperclip socket opens again, after the frames it held went out. Keep the number, ids, `seq` and order of frames, and the joined text equal to `maskKeys` of the whole text when nothing was missed. Tests in `tests/mask-split.test.js`: a gap in `seq`, a chunk still marked `truncated`, and `reset()`, each with a key built from pieces at the start of the chunk after `progress 40`; every recording case still passes. A bridge test in `tests/bridge.test.js`: the fake upstream closes and reopens the socket between the two chunks, and the page never gets the raw key. Files: `src/briefs/audit.js`, `server/index.js`, `tests/mask-split.test.js`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q1d. Two small fixes from the delta review (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.164).** From D6 and D8 (D7 is Q1e). The cloud session changes the masker wiring in `server/index.js` and adds a test at the end of `tests/bridge.test.js` at the same time (Q1c): touch only `publishTree` and the `/events` handling in `server/index.js`, and put the new bridge tests beside the existing tree event tests, not at the end of the file. Check that an older page, which opens `/events` with no parameter, still gets its file list: if it does not ask for `tree`, give it `?tree=1` in `src/live/bridge.js` and say in the report which older pages lose live tree events. In `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `readTree` runs on a fresh folder from `mkdtempSync` that is removed afterwards. Tests: in `tests/bridge.test.js`, a stream without the parameter gets no `tree` event after a file change and a stream with it gets the change; in `tests/scale.test.js`, the probe's tree stage leaves no folder behind. Files: `server/index.js`, `src/live/bridge.js`, `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `tests/scale.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q1e. The cost board and the task line keep the newest answer (AGY, merged as 0.1.166).** From the delta review (D7): `updateCosts` and `updateTasks` in `src/Stage.jsx` run on a 60 second timer and on batched notices, and each answer replaces the last with no order check, so an older read that answers after a newer one puts the older state on the cost board or a callout's task line until the next notice. Add a pure `latestOnly()` to `src/ui/panels/notices.js` that numbers each read and tells whether an answer is still the newest one started, as `routeReqRef` in `DispatchPanel.jsx` does, and use it in both effects in `src/Stage.jsx` for the answer and for the error path. No visual change; say in the report that you checked the cost board still fills in the replay with the mock. Files: `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/ui/panels/notices.js`, `tests/panels-notices.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q1f. One plan accept at a time, and a planner's tests stay in tests/ (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.169).** From the delta review (`docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-DELTA.md`). (1) What Q1b left of D5: `parsePlan` in `src/briefs/plan.js` checks a test path with `pathAllowed` but accepts one outside `tests/`, so `tests: ["src/a.js"]` puts a source file in the brief's `Files:` list as a test. Refuse it with `Brief ID names a test outside tests/: PATH.` (2) The race the review found sound but narrow: two accept calls whose Paperclip answers finish in the same event loop turn both read the old `TASKS.md`, and the later write drops the earlier call's briefs. In `createPlansApi` in `server/plans.js`, run accept calls one after another: each call waits for the one before it to finish, whether it failed or not, before it reads the plan and `TASKS.md`. A call that fails must not stop the next one. Mark D5 fixed in the delta review's table. Files: `src/briefs/plan.js`, `server/plans.js`, `tests/plan.test.js`, `tests/plans.test.js`, `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-DELTA.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Briefs for big repositories (2026-10-22)

The owner's target is the app finished by Saturday 2026-10-24. The scale probe at 200,000 files in 20,000 folders (`npm run scale-probe -- --files 200000 --folders 20000`) marks four rows over budget; at 40,000 files every row is within it. P12, P13 and V10 run at the same time.

**P12. Shell names looked up from an index, at any repo size (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.172).** At 200,000 files the probe row `pipeline, Hermes read or shell line` is 14 ms mean and 33 ms worst against 16 ms, and it grows with the repo: `touchesForCommand` in `src/model/commands.js` runs `tokenSpans` for every known file on every command segment, and for every folder when the segment lists, and `resolveKnown` in `src/model/paths.js` (Hermes reads) runs `files.includes` and `files.filter` on every call. Add a pure `src/model/fileIndex.js` with `indexFor(list)`: an index kept in a `WeakMap` keyed on the array, which takes in the entries appended to the same array since its last call (`learn` in `src/model/pipeline.js` pushes into `ctx.files` and `ctx.dirs`) and builds itself again when the array got shorter; it answers whether a path is in the list and which paths have a given base name. In `touchesForCommand`, find the files and folders a segment names by looking up the segment's substrings that start and end where today's pattern allows a match (`(?<![\w.-])` before, `(?![\w-])` after a file, the folder tail after a folder) and are no longer than the longest path in the list, instead of walking the list; keep the signature, the order of the touches, the write and list rules and the nesting rule (`withoutNested`, `firstSpans`). In `resolveKnown`, use the index for the exact match and the base name matches, and keep the rest. Callers do not change. The output must be the same as today for every input: in a new `tests/file-index.test.js`, keep a copy of today's `touchesForCommand` as the reference and compare the two on every shell command in `public/fixtures/*.jsonl` and `public/recordings/*-run.jsonl` (say how you found the commands), and on 2000 seeded commands over a `makeScaleTree` tree that name files, folders, a name with spaces in quotes, a path inside a longer path, a file and its folder, and a name not in the list; test `indexFor` on an array that grows by push and on one that is replaced. `tests/commands.test.js` and `tests/paths.test.js` pass unchanged. Report the probe's two pipeline rows at 40,000 and 200,000 files before and after; the worst at 200,000 must be under 16 ms. Files: `src/model/fileIndex.js`, `src/model/commands.js`, `src/model/paths.js`, `tests/file-index.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P13. A smaller first file list (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.171).** For the second OpenCode lane. At 200,000 files the probe marks `GET /api/tree, folder shape` and `tree event, first to a page` OVER, 3.1 MB each against 1 MB. A page loads the list with `GET /api/tree` (`src/live/bridge.js:13`, `src/live/source.js:69`), and the stream then sends the whole list again as the page's first `tree` event on the first file change (`treeEvent(current, null, shape)` in `publishTree`, `server/index.js`), so a big project sends it twice. (1) `GET /api/tree` answers with `Content-Encoding: gzip` and `Vary: Origin, Accept-Encoding` when the request's `Accept-Encoding` names `gzip`, compressed with the async `zlib.gzip`, never `gzipSync`, so the bridge keeps serving the stream while it compresses; any other request gets the plain body as today. The browser unpacks it, so the page needs no change for this part. (2) The tree answer carries `version`, a whole number the bridge raises each time its file list changes. The page opens the stream with `tree=folders&since=VERSION`, and the bridge keeps the file lists of its last 4 versions, so a client that names one of them gets as its first `tree` event only the files added since, in the `{ root, files }` shape the later events use (no event when nothing was added); a client with a missing or unknown `since` gets the whole list as today, so an older page still works. Both `src/live/bridge.js` and the proxy route in `src/live/source.js` send `since`. (3) In `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, add a row for the gzip size of the folder answer and one for the first event with `since` set to the version the page read, and keep the old rows; AGY moves the probe's tree and activity makers into `src/model/scaleTree.js` at the same time (V10), so add your rows without moving code. Report the new rows at 40,000 and 200,000 files. Tests: in `tests/bridge.test.js`, a gzip answer that unpacks to the same JSON, a request without `gzip` that gets plain JSON, `version` rising after a file appears, a stream with a known `since` that gets only the new file, and an unknown `since` that gets the whole list; in `tests/bridge-client.test.js`, the page sending `since`; `tests/tree-format.test.js` and `tests/scale.test.js` where the shapes or rows change. Test bridges point at the dead port 3999, and no test calls a Paperclip route that writes. Files: `server/index.js`, `src/live/bridge.js`, `src/live/source.js`, `src/live/tree.js`, `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `tests/bridge.test.js`, `tests/bridge-client.test.js`, `tests/tree-format.test.js`, `tests/scale.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V10. The room at 200,000 files in the browser (AGY, merged as 0.1.173).** The scale probe times the room's code in Node, so nobody has measured the frame time in the browser with a big tree. (1) Move `random`, `uniqueName`, `makeScaleTree`, `scaleActivities`, their word lists and the probe's three agents from `scripts/scale-probe.mjs` into a new pure `src/model/scaleTree.js` with no Node imports, and have `scripts/scale-probe.mjs` import them and export them again, so `tests/scale.test.js` passes unchanged and the probe prints the same rows; OpenCode 2 adds rows to the probe at the same time (P13), so change only the moved code and its import. (2) `/?replay&scale=N`, with N a whole number from 1000 to 500000, replays a seeded room: the tree from `makeScaleTree({ files: N, folders: N / 10 })` through `makeTree`, the three agents, and `scaleActivities` of that tree as the activities, in the shape `loadReplay` gives; any other N opens the normal replay. Read N with a pure `scaleSize(params)` in `src/startup.js`. (3) `&fps` adds one line to the HUD's top left panel, `frame MEDIAN ms, p95 P95 ms` over the last 120 frames, in Space Mono at 11px, white at 0.7, updated twice a second; without `fps` nothing is drawn or measured. (4) Measure at 1280 by 800, at 40,000 and 200,000 files, playing at 4x for 60 seconds with the tab in front (a hidden tab throttles frames): the time from opening the page to the first frame, and the median and p95 frame time. If the p95 at 200,000 is over 33 ms, profile it in the browser, name the function that costs most, fix it in `src/engine/` or `src/Stage.jsx`, and give the numbers before and after. Tests: a new `tests/scale-tree.test.js` (the same seed gives the same tree and activities; `scaleSize` reads `1000` and `500000` and refuses `999`, `500001`, `abc` and an empty value) and the existing engine tests. Files: `src/model/scaleTree.js`, `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `src/startup.js`, `src/App.jsx`, `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, `src/engine/` for a measured fix only, `tests/scale-tree.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P14. No two folder cards overlap, even at 200,000 files (OpenCode, rejected, see P14b).** `layoutCards` in `src/engine/layout.js` clamps every card's height to `CARD_V` (-1400 to 1600), so a column with more cards than that span holds cannot spread, and `relaxCards` ends with cards on top of each other. Counted over the chain the room runs (the one in `replayDigest` in `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`: apply each activity, `viewInput`, `planFolderView`, `cardSize`, then `layoutCards` warm started from the last places whenever the input changes): over every fixture and every `public/recordings/*-run.jsonl`, 11 of 3027 layouts end with an overlap (11 pairs, the worst 92 units); in the seeded replay at 200,000 files in 20,000 folders (`makeScaleTree` and the first 800 activities of `scaleActivities` from `src/model/scaleTree.js`), 308 of 399 layouts do, with up to 158 cards. (1) Export a pure `cardOverlaps(entries, placed, obstacles = [])` from `src/engine/layout.js` that lists every pair of cards whose boxes (each card's width and height from `cardSize`, centred on its place) cross by more than 1 unit on both axes, and every card that crosses the hub box (`CARD_HUB`) or an obstacle by more than 1 unit. (2) When `relaxCards` ends with an overlap, move cards until `cardOverlaps` is empty: for example, let a crowded column continue in a further column on the same side, further from the hub, or widen the vertical range for that layout only; keep the root above the hub, top level folders left and right of it, and each folder on its parent's side and further out than its parent; keep it deterministic. A layout that ends with no overlap today must come out the same, place for place: the new step acts only after the relaxation leaves an overlap. Say in your report how many layouts in the two chains above changed place although they had no overlap before, measured with a script you do not commit; it must be 0. (3) Time: report the median, p95 and worst `layoutCards` time over the 200,000 file chain before and after (today about 10, 16 and 29 ms on this machine); the p95 must stay under 16 ms. (4) Add a row `layoutCards, overlapping layouts` to `scripts/scale-probe.mjs` that counts the layouts of its seeded chain that `cardOverlaps` finds anything in, with a budget of 0. Tests: in a new `tests/layout-overlap.test.js`, `cardOverlaps` on two crossing cards, two touching cards (not an overlap), a card on the hub and a card on an obstacle; the chain above over every fixture, and over every recording when `public/recordings/` exists (it is not in git, so CI skips it), with no overlap in any layout; the seeded chain at 200,000 files over its first 300 activities with no overlap; and a column of 40 tall cards under one parent, which today cannot fit, with no overlap. `tests/layout.test.js` passes unchanged. Files: `src/engine/layout.js`, `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `tests/layout-overlap.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P14b. Repair only the cards that overlap, and keep the room still (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.176).** For the second OpenCode lane. P14 (branch `feat/p14-no-two-folder`, not merged) reached no overlap, but by packing every card into columns by depth whenever one overlap was left, and by warm starting the next layout from the places before the repair (a hidden `WARM` symbol on each place). The room then jumps between two arrangements. The measure is the chain the room runs (`replayDigest` in `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`: apply each activity, `viewInput`, `planFolderView`, `cardSize`, `layoutCards` warm started whenever the input changes), with the move of a layout being the mean distance in x and y that the cards it shares with the layout before it moved. Over every fixture and every `public/recordings/*-run.jsonl` (3094 layouts), main leaves 11 overlapping layouts, 85 layouts with a move over 150 units and a worst move of 404, and P14 leaves 0, 93 and 2468; in the seeded chain at 200,000 files in 20,000 folders (`makeScaleTree` and the first 800 activities of `scaleActivities`, 399 layouts), main leaves 308, 52 and 335, and P14 leaves 0, 253 and 7605, with the layout 5560 units tall against 3796. Start by merging `refs/heads/feat/p14-no-two-folder` into your branch: keep its `cardOverlaps`, its probe row and its tests, and replace its repair. (1) When `relaxCards` leaves an overlap, move only the cards that take part in one, to the nearest free place on their own side that is further from the hub than their parent (below the last card of their column, or in a column further out), and keep every other card where the relaxation put it; a moved card that then hits another moves on the same way. Return the repaired places as the places, so the next warm start begins from what the room shows, and remove the `WARM` symbol. Keep it deterministic. (2) Add `layoutChain(activities, tree, agents)` to `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, returning `{ layouts, overlapping, moves, worstMove, tallest }` for the chain above (`moves` counts the layouts with a move over 150 units; `tallest` is the greatest height from the lowest card edge to the highest), and print it as rows for the seeded chain; the overlap row of P14 reads from it. Targets, all four at once: no overlapping layout in either chain; over the fixtures and recordings at most 90 moves and a worst move of 600; in the 200,000 file chain at most 80 moves and a worst move of 1000; and a `layoutCards` p95 over the 200,000 file chain at most 2 ms above main's, measured in the same run. If a target cannot be met without giving up another, stop, keep the best version, and give all the numbers in your report. This worktree has no `public/recordings/` (it is not in git); read them from `C:\GitHub\MachineRoom\public\recordings` for your numbers, without writing anything there. Tests: in `tests/layout-overlap.test.js`, the fixture chain with no overlap and at most the fixture share of the move target, the recordings when the folder exists, the seeded chain at 200,000 files over its first 300 activities with no overlap and no move over 1000, a repair that moves only the two cards of the one overlapping pair, and the column of 40 tall cards with no overlap; `tests/layout.test.js` passes unchanged. ## Briefs for Saturday (2026-10-24)

Three OpenCode lanes and AGY run at the same time; their files do not overlap. On main (0.1.176) the scale probe at 200,000 files has two rows over budget left: the plain size of the first file list, which only a page older than P13 gets, and `planCollapse`, which the room does not use. Nobody has run the bridge on a real checkout of that size, and the frame time in the browser was not measured again after V10's review.

**P15. The bridge on a real 200,000 file checkout (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.180).** The scale probe feeds `readTree` a fake `listDir`, so the real `git ls-files`, the file watcher and the tree events have never run on a big project. (1) Add `scripts/scale-live.mjs` (`npm run scale-live -- [--files 200000] [--folders 20000] [--seed N] [--keep]`). It builds a git checkout with the names from `makeScaleTree` (`src/model/scaleTree.js`), one short line per file, under `%TEMP%\machineroom-scale\FILES-SEED` and nowhere else; it refuses to start with less than 2 GB free on that drive; it writes in batches with a bounded number of open files; it starts git by `gitPath()` (`server/programPath.js`) and commits with an author given by `-c user.name=scale -c user.email=scale@example.invalid` on the command line, never the owner's. A checkout of the same size and seed is reused. The folder is removed at the end unless `--keep`. (2) It starts the bridge (`createBridge`) in a child process, so the bridge's CPU time and memory are its own (read them in the child with `process.cpuUsage()` and `process.memoryUsage().rss` and send them over IPC), with `paperclipUrl` `http://127.0.0.1:3999` (a dead port), any company id, the checkout as `projectRoot` and port 0. (3) It prints rows in the scale probe's format, with these budgets: build time (info); first tree read, from the bridge's start until `GET /api/tree` answers with every file (5000 ms); `GET /api/tree` in the folder shape with gzip, time and bytes (1000 ms); the bridge's RSS after the first read (500 MB); one new file, from its write until a page on `/events?tree=folders&since=V` gets the tree event that names it (2000 ms); a burst of 2000 new files written into an untracked folder `out/` over 10 seconds: the tree reads started during it (info), the bridge's CPU time over the burst and 3 seconds after as a share of one core (50 percent), and the time from the last write to the tree event that names the last file (3000 ms); and the same burst into `dist/`, which the checkout's `.gitignore` lists: tree events that name a `dist/` file (0) and the CPU share (50 percent). Run it at 200,000 and at 40,000 files and give every row of both in your report. If a row is over its budget, make the smallest change in `server/index.js` that brings it under, and give the row before and after; if it cannot be done without breaking another row or a test, stop and report the numbers. Keep `scripts/scale-live.mjs` out of the pack, as `scripts/fake-paperclip.mjs` is (`npm run pack-check` passes). Tests: a new `tests/scale-live.test.js` runs the script's exported function at 2000 files with bursts of 200, in a folder from `tests/helpers/tempRoot.js`, and checks that every row is there, that the new file arrives and that no ignored file is ever sent. Files: `scripts/scale-live.mjs`, `tests/scale-live.test.js`, `package.json`, `server/index.js` (only if a budget fails), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.
