## The room, version 3 (the owner's redesign, 2026-10-23)

The owner watched the room at 200,000 files and asked for these changes. (1) The folder cards sit in one plane in front of the camera; they should fill the 3D space as a sphere around the hub. (2) Agents travel so fast that following them hurts; they should stay parked on the hub ring, with the orchestrator on top, and the camera should follow the card being touched instead. (3) While agents work, the cards they touch are outlined, the rest dim, the tree they work in glows, and each agent's pointer to its file is a yellow strip with corner brackets, as in the reference stills. (4) Folders are joined by straight lines that meet in the centre, not by hanging curves. (5) The tree is always there: every folder down to a depth of 1 to 3 (a slider) is drawn all the time, like the explorer in an editor, and only a folder an agent reaches below that depth appears, with a short fade in. (6) A Settings button holds the depth slider and the screen effects: fish eye, chromatic aberration, vignette, grain and fine dust. (7) Agents below the orchestrator show their subagents when their harness reports them.

What the stills show, for every brief here (the stills stay in `Concepts/` and out of git): white rectangular boxes with thin borders and dark fills, joined by straight thin white lines that meet at shared points; angular polylines in yellow and red, two or three straight segments each, that run to small tagged labels; and small square bracket corners, four L shapes, around a target. Colours stay as they are: white for activity, `#e5ff00` for active, `#ff2a2a` for failure, `#2aff80` for finished.

Order: V12, V13 and S2c now (pure and model code, no file that V11 touches). After V11 is merged: V16 and V14 on OpenCode and V17 on AGY. Then V15 and S2d. G4b (screenshots) waits until V17 is merged, and G3f (the README for version 3) comes last. S2c and S2d replace S2a, S3a and S2b.

**V12. The folder sphere layout (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.184).** A pure layout that puts folder cards on shells of a sphere around the hub, to replace `layoutCards` (V16 wires it in; do not touch `room.js` or `cards.js`). Add `src/engine/sphere.js` with `layoutSphere(entries, { previous } = {})`: `entries` are `{ path, width, height }` with `''` the root, and it returns a `Map` of path to `{ x, y, z }`. The root card sits below the hub centre at `(0, HUB_Y - ROOT_DROP, 0)` (the orchestrator will sit above it). Export `SPHERE_CENTER` (the hub centre, `HUB_CENTER` from `src/engine/hub.js`), where V16 makes the straight lines from the top-level folders meet. A folder at depth d sits on the shell of radius `SHELL_RADIUS + (d - 1) * SHELL_STEP` around `SPHERE_CENTER` (start from 1400 and 700, and tune them so the overview at the current camera distance holds depth 2). Top-level folders spread over the whole sphere by a Fibonacci spiral in name order, leaving a cap of 25 degrees free at each pole so no card sits right above or below the hub. A deeper folder lies in a cone around its parent's direction, its children spread by the golden angle in name order, the cone wide enough for the count. No two cards overlap in 3D: the centres of any two cards are at least half the sum of their diagonals plus 40 apart, and no card comes within `HUB_RADIUS + 200` of the hub centre. Separate crowded siblings by moving them along their shell, and only within their own parent's group. Keep the room still: the result is deterministic; with `previous`, adding or removing a card moves only that card's siblings and their subtrees, and every other card keeps its place exactly; a card that changes size moves only if it now overlaps. Export the constants. Add to `scripts/scale-probe.mjs` the rows `layoutSphere (cold)` and `layoutSphere (warm)` for the 200,000 file tree with every folder down to depth 2 (budget 16 ms), and a `layoutSphere, overlapping layouts` row over the same chain as `layoutChain` (0). Tests in a new `tests/sphere.test.js`: the same input gives the same places; no overlap and no card near the hub, for a fixture tree, for 300 folders at depth 1 and 2, and for one parent with 80 children; adding a sibling moves nothing outside that parent's group; shells by depth; the root below the centre. Files: `src/engine/sphere.js`, `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `tests/sphere.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V13. The folder plan for an always-present tree (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.182).** For the second OpenCode lane. Today `planFolderView` (`src/model/folderView.js`) draws only the folders on the agents' paths, so cards pop in and out. Add an option `alwaysDepth` (a whole number from 0 to 3; 0 keeps today's plan exactly, so the room does not change until V16 passes a value): every folder down to that depth is in the plan all the time, closed, and the paths agents touch now and recently add their folders below that depth as today. A card lists its items only while it holds a file an agent touches now or touched recently, as today; every other card in the plan is closed. Cap the always-present folders at `maxAlways` (default 300): when a depth would go over it, a parent keeps its children with the most files, in name order on ties, and the rest go into its faint back folders (`backLayers`) as today's open folders do; give each view a `pinned: true` when it is in the plan because of `alwaysDepth`, so V16 can fade only the others. Keep the plan deterministic and keep its cost: at 200,000 files with `alwaysDepth` 2, `planFolderView` for one new file stays under 16 ms (add the row to `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`). Tests in `tests/folder-view.test.js` (add to it): depth 0 matches today's plans on every fixture (compare the `--replay` plans digest before and after); depth 1, 2 and 3 on a fixture tree; the cap with a parent of 500 children; a deep touch adds only its path; a recent touch going stale removes only the folders below `alwaysDepth`. Files: `src/model/folderView.js`, `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `tests/folder-view.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S2c. Subagents in the state, from the signals the harnesses give (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.183).** For the third OpenCode lane. Replaces S2a and S3a. S1a recorded what each harness reports: OpenCode's `subagent` tool and Hermes' `delegate_task` each arrive as one line when the child has finished, with its goal or description and, for OpenCode, `state.time.start` and `end` and the child's `sessionID`; the child's own tool calls never reach the parent's log. Claude Code's Task tool and agy were not recorded, so do not read them. (1) Add two activities. `agent.spawn` has `agent` (the parent key), `child` (the parent key, `~` and a number counting from 1 within the parent's run), `name` (a short label from the description or goal, at most 40 characters) and `label`; `agent.end` has `agent` (the child key) and `ok`. (2) In `src/model/state.js`, `agent.spawn` adds the child to the roster with the fields every agent has plus `parent` and `depth` (the parent's depth plus one), status `working`; a spawn deeper than 3 adds nothing; `agent.end` sets `done` or `error`; the parent's `run.end` ends its children still working with the parent's result; a seek rebuilds them; `player.snapshot()` gives each agent `parent` (null at the top) and `depth`. (3) In the OpenCode and Hermes parsers, emit `agent.spawn` and then `agent.end` for each child line, both at the line's time (the child is shown for as long as the room shows a finished agent, since the harness reports it only at its end), and keep emitting today's `other` tool call too, so the activity log is unchanged. The unread-line counter must not count these lines. Tests: a new `tests/state-subagents.test.js` (spawn, two children and their keys, the depth limit, the parent's end closing a child, a seek, the snapshot fields) and in `tests/parsers.test.js` one child each from the lines S1a quotes. Replay every fixture and local recording and list each one whose activities changed and why. Files: `src/model/state.js`, `src/model/player.js`, `src/model/parsers/opencode.js`, `src/model/parsers/hermes.js`, `src/model/pipeline.js`, `tests/state-subagents.test.js`, `tests/parsers.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V16. Wire the sphere, straight lines and the fade in (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.187).** In `src/engine/room.js` and `src/engine/cards.js`, lay the cards out with `layoutSphere` instead of `layoutCards`, and pass `alwaysDepth` to `planFolderView`. Add `room.setOptions({ alwaysDepth })` (default 2; V17 adds the slider in Settings) that rebuilds the plan. In `src/engine/strings.js`, make every link a straight line: each top-level folder to `SPHERE_CENTER`, the root to `SPHERE_CENTER`, and every other folder to its parent, card centre to card centre, with the strand points spaced along the straight line as now (keep `strandPoints` and its seed). A card that enters the plan without `pinned` and every list of items that opens fades in over 0.4 seconds, and fades out over 0.4 seconds when it leaves (at once under reduced motion); pinned cards never fade. Remove `layoutCards`, `repairCards`, `cardOverlaps` and their tests once nothing uses them, and point the probe's chain rows at `layoutSphere`. Update the overview fit to the sphere (`fitView` over the cards). Measure the frame time with `?replay&scale=40000&fps` and `scale=200000` by having the orchestrator check it in a browser (say so in your report; you cannot see the screen), and give the probe rows before and after. Tests: the engine tests pass, and the probe's chain rows report 0 overlapping layouts. **V14. Agents parked on the hub, the orchestrator on top, a yellow strip to the file (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.186).** For the second OpenCode lane, beside V16. Agents no longer travel along the strings. (1) In `src/engine/agents.js` and `src/engine/hub.js`, every agent stays at its slot on the ring whether it works or not, and the orchestrator (`isOrchestrator` in `src/engine/shapes.js`) has no slot: it floats above the hub centre at `HUB_Y + ORCHESTRATOR_LIFT` (start from 360). A working agent shows it by its status ring, its chip and a slow pulse of its shape, not by moving. (2) Replace the dashed trace with the pointer from the stills: from the agent to the row of the file it touches, a strip of two or three straight segments (the first rises from the agent, the last runs level into the card's side), drawn as a narrow band two lines wide, yellow while it works, green for 5 seconds after a good run and red on a failure; and around the touched card, four yellow L shaped corner brackets just outside its border (CSS on the card, a new class `.mr-card.is-targeted` with `.mr-card-bracket` children, listed in your report for the DOM contract). Under reduced motion the strip appears at once. (3) The chip stays beside its parked agent, joined by its thin line, and the callout stays as it is. Check the room in replay through the orchestrator (you cannot see the screen; say so). Tests: a pure `stripPoints(from, to)` that gives the segments, deterministic, with a test in a new `tests/agent-strip.test.js`, and the engine tests pass. **V17. Settings: the depth slider and the screen effects (AGY, merged as 0.1.190).** A Settings button in the HUD (top right, beside the help) opens a small panel in the concept style with: the always-shown folder depth, a slider from 1 to 3 (default 2), calling `room.setOptions({ alwaysDepth })` from `src/Stage.jsx` once V16 is merged (until then keep the value in the settings and leave the call out); and each screen effect with an on switch and a strength slider: fish eye, chromatic aberration, vignette, grain and fine dust. Save the settings in `machineroom.settings` through `src/storage.js`, beside the saved connection, and read them at start. The folder cards are CSS3D elements, not WebGL, so a WebGL post pass would bend the lines and shapes but not the cards. So: vignette and grain as a CSS overlay above both layers (grain still under reduced motion); fine dust as a slow WebGL point field near the camera in `src/engine/void.js` (still under reduced motion); chromatic aberration and fish eye as one SVG filter on the element that holds both layers (per-channel `feOffset` for the aberration, `feDisplacementMap` with a fixed barrel map for the fish eye), so both layers bend together. Fish eye and aberration are off by default, vignette, grain and dust on at low strength. Measure with `?replay&scale=40000&fps` in a front window: the fps line with every effect off, with the defaults, and with each effect alone at full strength, pasted as text. An effect that adds more than 4 ms to the p95 says so in its label (`costly`). Check by eye at 1280 by 800 and 1024 by 768, and for each claim give the console check that proves it (a computed style, the filter attribute). Tests: a pure `effectSettings(saved)` that fills defaults and clamps values, with a test in a new `tests/settings.test.js`. The camera follows the touched card, and the rest dims (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.189).** Follow mode (`F`) follows the card that holds the file being touched, not the agent: wide (the card and its parent) and close up (the card alone). To stay calm, it holds each card at least 4 seconds, does not move at all when the next card is already inside the middle two thirds of the frame, and eases over 1.2 seconds (jumps under reduced motion). While any agent works, every card not on a working agent's path dims to 0.25 opacity, its line and strand too; the cards on the path from the root to each touched file, and the lines between them, glow brighter (full white, a soft outer glow on the card border); the touched card carries V14's brackets. When no agent works, everything returns to normal over 1 second. `0` and clicks end follow mode as today. Move V14's corner brackets into `cards.js`: today `agents.js` finds the card element by `data-path` and adds `.is-targeted` and the four `.mr-card-bracket` children itself; let `cards.js` build the brackets with the card, add `is-targeted` in its own class name, and take the targeted paths from the room. Check through the orchestrator in replay (say so). ## After the redesign (2026-10-23)

The room, version 3 is merged through V17 (0.1.190). Its reviews left three things: the cost board can sit over a card, because the sphere layout has no obstacles; the overview fits every drawn card, so a deep touched path pulls the camera far out and the cards are small; and code the old room needed is still there. V18, P17 and G3f run on the three OpenCode lanes while AGY takes the screenshots (G4b). Hermes stays retired.

**V18. The cost board and the sphere's overview (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.194).** (1) Give `layoutSphere` (`src/engine/sphere.js`) an `obstacles` option, a list of `{ x, y, z, radius }`: no card centre comes closer to an obstacle centre than the card's half diagonal plus the radius plus 40, and the root card stays where it is. On a warm start a card that now meets an obstacle moves the way a resized card does (only it and its siblings), and every other card keeps its place. In `src/engine/cards.js` and `src/engine/room.js`, pass the cost board's bounding sphere (from `boardBox` in `src/engine/board.js` and the board's place) while the board is shown, and none while it is hidden (below 900 pixels wide or with no control client). (2) The overview fit in `room.js` uses the hub, the board while shown, the root and the cards in the plan with `pinned: true`, not every drawn card, so a deep touched path no longer pulls the camera out; a deep card can be outside the overview, and follow mode (`F`) still reaches it. ## Fixes from the V3 audit (2026-10-23)

Q3 (`AUDIT-2026-10-V3.md`) found 16 things in the room, version 3. V16 (a stale default in `ARCHITECTURE.md`) was fixed at the merge. V3 (an agent's focus stays on its last file during a shell call with no touches) stays as it is, by the owner's choice on 2026-10-23: clearing it would make the strip and the follow camera jump away and back between file touches. The rest go to four OpenCode lanes, split by file so no two lanes touch the same file. Each brief: one test per finding that fails before the fix and passes after (the audit's Node checks show how to set each case up), the `--replay` digests identical before and after unless the brief says otherwise, and in the report a line per finding with what you changed.

**Q3a. The cards: depth mesh, rows after a seek, idle frames (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.213).** V1 (high): a card's depth mesh is made at the origin and moved only when the card's position changes, so a card made at its home, the root first of all, leaves its mesh at the hub centre; place the mesh at the card's position when the card is made and whenever its position is set. V2: after a seek, `paintRows` keeps row entries whose `file` points at the old state's object, so a row keeps `w 1` and `is-written` that the new state does not have; make `cards.sync` clear each row entry's file and cached values, or read `state.files.get(path)` on every update. V10: with the replay paused and nothing changing, every frame still walks every card and row; skip the row loop when the newest activity, the working mask, the flashes, the fades and the dim level are all unchanged since the last frame, and say in the report how you know nothing is skipped that should run (a test that a flash, a fade and a new activity each still repaint). Files: `src/engine/cards.js`, `tests/cards.test.js` (or a new test file), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q3b. The agents and their chips: failed strip, child chips, idle writes (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.212).** V5: when a working agent's last tool call failed, the strip's stripes stay yellow while the callout is red; pick red before the working yellow when the last call failed (the callout already knows it). V8: a subagent's chip is hidden but never removed when its view goes, so a long session keeps one hidden chip per subagent and visits it every frame; add `remove(key)` to `src/engine/terminals.js` (it removes the element, the CSS3D object and the map entry) and call it from `removeView` in `agents.js`. V9: a chip made after the room starts (every subagent's) has no click handler; let `createTerminals` take an `onPick` option that every chip gets, including later ones, and in `src/engine/room.js` change only the `createTerminals(...)` call to pass the handler the room already attaches (and remove the loop that attaches it, if there is one); touch no other line of `room.js`, which Q3c changes. V11: every frame `setEnds` rewrites each agent's link line and the callout line even when nothing moved; write them only when an end moved. Files: `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/engine/terminals.js`, `src/engine/room.js` (only the `createTerminals` call and its click loop), tests under `tests/`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q3c. Follow mode and the room after a seek and a click; the HUD keys behind a dialog (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.215).** V4: a seek leaves `following`, `followCard` and `pendingFollow` as they were, so the camera holds a card from before the seek; in `room.sync` clear `pendingFollow` and `followCard`, and when following, aim at the card touched in the new state. V7: `ARCHITECTURE.md` says clicking ends follow mode, but a click on a card head, a card row (`focusPath`) or empty space leaves it on; end follow mode on each of those clicks, and keep it on for a click on the HUD. V15: `focusPath` schedules a label 250 ms later and a seek does not clear it, so the old label appears over the new room; clear the pending timers in `sync` as `dispose` does. V13: with the Settings dialog open, `L`, `C` and `?` still reach the HUD handler in `src/ui/Hud.jsx`; return early there for a key whose target is inside `[role="dialog"]`, as `Stage.jsx` does for the room keys. Do not change the `createTerminals(...)` call in `room.js` or the code that attaches chip clicks; Q3b changes those. **Q3d. The strands' texture and writes; the bridge's watcher after a burst (OpenCode 5, merged as 0.1.216).** V6: every `strings.build` makes a new 128 by 128 glow `CanvasTexture` and `dispose` never frees it, so each relink leaves one texture behind; make one texture in `createStrings`, reuse it for every strand, and dispose it once in `dispose` (a test that counts the texture's dispose calls over two builds and a dispose). ## The cabinet sphere and packets (the owner's sketch, 2026-10-23)

The owner drew the room they want. It is still a sphere, but packed: the top-level folders are the first layer around the hub, neat and close, allowed to overlap; a folder's subfolders sit stacked behind it like folders in a cabinet; and only the folders on a working path step out, one layer further per level, with their siblings fanned beside them in grey, until the deepest card lists its files. The dots on the lines should line up and travel like network packets, fast, from the agent outward. V21 and V22 wait for Q3a to Q3d, which touch the same files.

**V21. The cabinet sphere (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.218).** Rework `layoutSphere` in `src/engine/sphere.js` and the card sizes in `src/engine/cards.js`. (1) Neat cards: smaller, near uniform sizes, `CARD_SCALES` about `{ closed: 1, open: 1.2, files: 1.8 }` (choose and say). (2) The first layer: top-level folders on one shell whose radius comes from their count, so they sit close: about the radius at which their cards cover half of the shell's area, never less than 1100 and never more than 2600 (say the formula), spread by the Fibonacci spiral with the pole caps free as now. Neighbours may overlap, but no top-level card's centre comes closer to another's than 0.6 of a card width. (3) The cabinet: a folder's children that are not on a path (the always-present ones and recent closed ones) sit stacked directly behind it, each one a step further out along the parent's direction (about 50 units) and shifted a little up and to the side, like the faint back cards, overlapping; show at most 4 in a stack and let the parent's `back` count the rest. (4) The path: a folder on a working agent's focus chain, or pinned by a click, steps out from its parent's stack to the next layer, about 650 units further out along its parent's direction, and the parent's other children in the plan fan around it in a small cone (overlap allowed; the dimming already greys them). This repeats for each level, so the chain runs outward; the deepest card on the chain lists its files. `layoutSphere` takes the open paths as a new option (for example `open: Set`), and `cards.js` passes the chain it already works out for the dashes (compute it before the layout if it is computed after today). (5) The root stays below the hub. A warm start keeps every card that did not change role in place; a folder that leaves a path glides back into its parent's stack. (6) In `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, keep the sphere rows (cold and warm ms, tallest, overview distance) and replace the overlap row with a `covered` row: layouts in which a top-level card hides more than 70 percent of another top-level card (should be 0). Give the rows before and after for the fixtures, 40,000 and 200,000 files. Tests in `tests/sphere.test.js`: the first shell's radius for 4, 20 and 80 top-level folders; the 0.6 width spacing on the first shell; a stack behind its parent (each child further out than the last, at most 4 placed); a path child one layer out along the parent's direction with its siblings within the cone; the root unmoved; the same input gives the same layout; a warm start where a folder leaves the path and returns to the stack. Expect the `--replay` layout digests to change (say so), and the activity and plan digests to stay the same. Check the look through the orchestrator (you cannot see the screen; say so). Files: `src/engine/sphere.js`, `src/engine/cards.js`, `src/engine/room.js` (only if the open paths must come from it), `scripts/scale-probe.mjs`, `tests/sphere.test.js`, `tests/cards.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V22. Packets on the lines (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.217).** (1) The strand points in `src/engine/strings.js` are scattered up to 5 units off their line (`strandPoints` adds `(rand() - 0.5) * 10` on each axis), so they do not line up; put every point on the line, keeping the seed for the spacing only, and on a working agent's path let the strand's points travel along the line toward the touched card, fast (about 2000 units a second, wrapping at the end), and stand still otherwise. (2) On each working agent's strip (`src/engine/agents.js`, the straight line from the agent to the touched file row), send packets: small yellow points (3 pixels, `sizeAttenuation: false`, like the dust motes) that leave the agent and reach the file in 0.45 seconds, one about every 90 ms while the agent works, and a burst of 5 at each `tool.start`; green packets for the 5 seconds after a good run and red after a failure, as the strip's stripes are. Keep a fixed pool per agent (at most 24 points), allocate nothing per frame, and hide the pool when the strip is hidden. Under reduced motion no packet moves: the strip shows its stripes only. Put the timing in a pure exported helper (for example `packetSpots(now, sends, duration)` returning the fractions along the line of the packets still in flight) and test it: a packet at 0, 0.2 and 0.45 seconds after its send, a burst, the pool cap, and nothing under reduced motion; test that `strandPoints` puts every point on the line and is the same for the same seed. The `--replay` digests stay the same. Check the look through the orchestrator (you cannot see the screen; say so). Files: `src/engine/strings.js`, `src/engine/agents.js`, `tests/strands.test.js`, `tests/agent-strip.test.js` (or a new test file), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S4. A subagent that works beside its parent, from AGY (OpenCode 5, merged as 0.1.227).** Today a child appears only for a 6 second hold after `agent.end`, because the OpenCode and Hermes parsers learn of a child when it finishes. Make a child that works while its parent works show as its own agent for that whole time. (1) `src/model/parsers/agy.js` keys a step by `conversation_id` and `step_index` but credits every conversation to the run's agent. Treat the first `conversation_id` of a run as the parent; at the first step of any other `conversation_id` in the same run emit `agent.spawn` (child `PARENT~N`, numbered from 1 within the run, name from the step if it carries one, else `subagent N`), credit that conversation's tool calls to the child key, and emit `agent.end` for it at its last step's `DONE` when the parent acts again, or at the run's end (the parent's `run.end` already ends children still working). ## Run-lane as a service (2026-10-24, the owner's choice)

The owner chose to make `run-lane` the room's live source, beside Paperclip and later instead of it for lane work (see `docs/agents/SOURCES.md`). S6a is the first step.

**S6a. The live lane source (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.233).** Make the bridge show lane runs live, from the logs `npm run run-lane` writes, with no Paperclip. (1) A new module `server/lanes-source.js`: watch the runs folder (`%TEMP%\machineroom-runs` on Windows; take the folder as an option so tests use a temp folder) for a new `<lane>-<brief>-<stamp>.log`, read it from its first byte as it grows, and turn it into frames shaped as `framesFromRun` in `src/briefs/recording.js` builds them (`heartbeat.run.queued`, `heartbeat.run.status` running, one `heartbeat.run.log` per complete line with `stream: 'stdout'` and a growing `seq`, then `heartbeat.run.status` succeeded or failed). Share the line handling with `framesFromRun` instead of copying it (move what both need into an exported helper in `src/briefs/recording.js`). Hold a partial last line until its newline. End the run when its record appears in `runs.jsonl` (match on `log`), using its `exitCode` and `killed`, or after 10 minutes with no growth and no record (failed, `error: 'no record'`). (2) Agents: one per lane from `docs/agents/lanes.json` that has a `cli` block (`agentsFile` shows the shape), keyed by the lane name (`opencode` to `opencode5`), with `parser` from the lane's harness; retired lanes left out. Each lane works in its own worktree, so give each lane's parser that worktree as its project root, so a path inside it lands on the same relative path in the room's tree (the main checkout). (3) Wire it into `server/index.js` behind an option and an env var (`lanesSource: true`, `MACHINEROOM_LANES=1`): its frames go through `accept()` like Paperclip's (so they are masked, kept in the history and sent on `/events`), and `/api/agents` lists the lane agents beside Paperclip's (or alone when there is no Paperclip). With the option off nothing changes. Add `--lanes` to `bin/machine-room.mjs` (serve and up). (4) Tests with a temp runs folder and a fake log that grows over several writes (no real lane run, no real Paperclip, the Paperclip URL at the dead port 3999): a page on `/events?history=1` gets the run's frames in order with the lane's key; a partial line is held until its newline; the record ends the run with the right status; a log with no record ends after the quiet limit (inject a short one); two lanes at once keep their own `runId` and `seq`; a path inside the lane's worktree lands on the right relative path after the pipeline; with the option off no lane agent appears. Say in your report how a page would show a lane run today (`npm run serve -- up --lanes`), and what is left for AGY (S6b). Files: `server/lanes-source.js` (new), `server/index.js`, `bin/machine-room.mjs`, `src/briefs/recording.js`, `tests/lanes-source.test.js` (new), `tests/recording.test.js` (only if a moved helper needs it), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the Live mode section and the code layout), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**F8. The fixture builder masks across chunks (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.234).** `scripts/build-fixtures.mjs` runs `scrub` on each frame alone, so a Windows profile name or an e-mail address that Paperclip split across two log chunks of one run slips through as two harmless-looking prefixes (S5 found two such prefixes of the profile path in `scenario-g` and repaired them by hand). Mask the joined text of each run and stream instead: join the chunks of a run in `seq` order, mask, and cut the masked text back at the original chunk lengths where they still fall on the same characters (or carry a held tail into the next chunk, as `createSplitMasker` in `src/briefs/audit.js` does for keys; reuse it if it fits). Then check the whole output with `findLeaks` across the joined text, not per line, and refuse to write on a finding. Tests: a profile path split at every position across two chunks is masked whole; an e-mail split in two is masked; a key split in two is refused; `scenario-g` rebuilt from its own run file gives the same masked text as the committed fixture (no hand repair needed). The `--replay` digests stay the same. Files: `scripts/build-fixtures.mjs`, `src/briefs/audit.js` (only to share a helper), `tests/build-fixtures.test.js`, `docs/agents/RECORDING.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**P18. The scale probe lays out what the room draws (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.234).** `scripts/scale-probe.mjs` builds its replay layouts from `openChain(state)` and never calls `planOpenSet` or `stackOverflow`, so its layout digests and its `covered` and `tallest` rows describe a room without stacks (Q4a's fix changed the room and no digest moved). Make the probe run the same chain as `createCards`: `viewInput`, `planFolderView` at depth 2, `planOpenSet`, `stackOverflow` (drop the hidden views, add their count to `back`), `cardSize`, then `layoutSphere` with the open set. Share that chain as one exported pure function in `src/engine/cards.js` that both `createCards` and the probe call, instead of two copies. Expect every layout digest to change once (say so and give the before and after rows for the fixtures, 40,000 and 200,000 files), the activity and plan digests to stay the same, and a test that the shared function hides the same views `createCards` hides on the Q4a case. **S6e. A probe of OpenCode over ACP (OpenCode 5, merged as 0.1.234).** `SOURCES.md` found that OpenCode speaks the Agent Client Protocol (`opencode acp`). Before the runner uses it, see what it really sends. Write `scripts/acp-probe.mjs`, a dev tool left out of the pack: it starts `opencode acp` in a folder it is given (use a fresh temp copy of `public/fixtures/sandbox-tree.json`'s files, never this checkout), sends `initialize`, `session/new` and one `session/prompt` asking it to read two files, write a third, and start one subagent (OpenCode's task tool) to read a fourth, answers each permission request with allow, and writes every JSON-RPC message, both ways, to a JSON lines file, then stops the process tree with `killTree()`. Run it once with the lanes' model (`opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash`; check how ACP takes a model) and a 5 minute limit. Report what you saw: the `tool_call` and `tool_call_update` shapes, the `kind` and `locations` of each file call, whether the subagent shows as its own session, a tool call or nothing, how permission requests look, and anything the room's OpenCode parser gets today that ACP does not, or the other way round. Keep the recording out of git; put a short masked excerpt in your report. No change to the room's code. Files: `scripts/acp-probe.mjs` (new), `scripts/pack-check.mjs` (only if the new script must be named as left out), `docs/agents/SOURCES.md` (a short Results paragraph under the ACP section), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

### Sunday (2026-10-25): the three harnesses on any project

The owner moved the target to Sunday 2026-10-25, with Monday as pass or retire, and narrowed it to the harnesses that work: OpenCode, AGY and Claude Code, on the owner's other projects, with OpenAI-compatible models through OpenCode. The three briefs below run at once. They all touch `commandFor` in `src/briefs/run.js`, `ADAPTERS` and `createRunLineReader` in `src/briefs/recording.js` and `docs/agents/lanes.json`: add your harness as its own branch or entry and do not reorder or reformat the others, so the merges stay small.

**S6f. A lane run on another project (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.236).** Today `run-lane` only runs a brief from `TASKS.md` in a lane worktree of this repository, and the bridge's lane source reads `lanes.json` from the project root, so `npm run serve -- up --lanes --root C:\GitHub\Other` lists no lanes. Make both work on any folder. (1) `npm run run-lane -- LANE --project FOLDER --task "TEXT"` (and `--task-file PATH`, read as UTF-8) in place of a brief id: the folder must exist and be absolute after `resolve`; refuse this checkout and any lane worktree from `lanes.json`; when the folder is a git checkout with uncommitted changes, refuse unless `--allow-dirty`. It runs the lane's harness with the folder as its working folder (give `commandFor` an optional `cwd` that wins over `lane.worktree`), the prompt is the task text followed by `UNATTENDED_NOTE` (`src/briefs`), and it makes no branch, reads no brief and writes no recording. The record in `runs.jsonl` carries `project` (the folder), `task` (its first 200 characters), `briefs: []` and the usual fields; `summarizeRun` gets the project as its worktree. `--list`, `--stats` and `--outcomes` still read every record. Before the harness starts, write `<log name>.meta.json` beside the log with `{ lane, project }`. (2) `server/lanes-source.js`: read `lanes.json` from this repository (the folder of the server module, two levels up), not from `projectRoot`; take it as an option for tests. A log with a meta file whose `project` resolves to the room's root (case-insensitive on Windows) is shown with no path rewrite; a log with a meta file for another folder is skipped; a log with no meta file is a lane worktree run and works as today. (3) README: a short section "Use it on another project" (serve with `--root` and `--lanes`, then `run-lane --project`), and a paragraph on models: OpenCode reads any OpenAI-compatible provider from its own config, so a lane's `cli.model` can name `provider/model` for such a provider; the key stays in OpenCode's config or an environment variable and never in `lanes.json` or this repository. Do not set up a provider or touch any key. Tests: the argument checks (missing folder, this checkout, a lane worktree, a dirty git folder with and without `--allow-dirty`, `--task` and `--task-file`, a brief id and `--project` together refused); the command's working folder; the record's fields (write to a temp runs folder); the source shows a meta-file run for the room's root with paths kept, skips one for another folder, and lists the lanes when `projectRoot` has no `lanes.json`. No real harness run. Files: `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `src/briefs/run.js`, `server/lanes-source.js`, `tests/run-lane.test.js` or a new `tests/run-project.test.js`, `tests/lanes-source.test.js`, `README.md`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Commands and Live mode), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S6b. AGY as a lane (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.237).** The owner chose on 2026-10-24 to run AGY unattended like the OpenCode lanes. Give the `agy` entry in `docs/agents/lanes.json` a `cli` block (`harness: "agy"`, `bin` as AGY is found on PATH here, and the model if AGY takes one), and teach the runner and the recordings that harness: `commandFor` returns `agy --print PROMPT --output-format stream-json --dangerously-skip-permissions` in the lane's worktree (check the flags with `agy --help`, which is the only AGY command you may run; add the model flag only if the help lists one); `ADAPTERS` maps `agy` to `process`, the adapter the room's parser picker reads as AGY; `createRunLineReader('agy')` marks a line read when `createAgyParser` turns it into activities; `summarizeRun` counts AGY's tool steps and files; `scripts/recording-files.mjs` accepts the lane. `npm run hand-run` refuses a lane with a command line, so say in `ARCHITECTURE.md` that AGY runs through `run-lane` from now on. Once `agentsFile` knows the harness, the lane source of S6a lists AGY with no change; check that with a test. Tests: the command for the agy lane, `agentsFile('agy', lanes)`, the line reader on `scenario-g`'s AGY lines (read from the fixture), `framesFromRun` for an AGY log, and the lane source listing `agy`. No real AGY run: Claude does that after the merge. Files: `docs/agents/lanes.json`, `src/briefs/run.js`, `src/briefs/recording.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs` (only if the harness name is checked there), `scripts/recording-files.mjs`, `tests/run-lane.test.js`, `tests/recording.test.js`, `tests/lanes-source.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S6g. Claude Code as a lane (OpenCode 5, merged as 0.1.238).** The room's Claude parser (`src/model/parsers/claude.js`) reads only Paperclip's wrapped `acpx.tool_call` lines. Claude Code run headless (`claude -p PROMPT --output-format stream-json --verbose`) prints its own JSON lines instead, and those carry live subagents. (1) Extend the parser, keeping the `acpx` path as it is: an `assistant` line's `message.content` items of type `tool_use` (`id`, `name`, `input`) start a tool call; `Read`, `Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit` and `NotebookEdit` touch `input.file_path` (or `notebook_path`), `Glob` and `Grep` touch `input.path` as a folder (the root when absent), `LS` lists `input.path`, `Bash` uses `touchesForCommand` on `input.command`, and the rest are `other`; a `user` line's `tool_result` items (`tool_use_id`, `is_error`) end them. A `Task` or `Agent` tool call emits `agent.spawn` (`PARENT~N`, named from `input.subagent_type` or `input.description`, labelled with `input.description`), every later line whose `parent_tool_use_id` is that call's id carries the child's key, so the child gets its own tool calls and strip while it works, and that call's `tool_result` emits `agent.end` (failed when `is_error`). Paths are absolute or relative to the run's folder, cut to the project root the way the other parsers do. (2) A `claude` lane in `docs/agents/lanes.json` with a `cli` block (`harness: "claude"`, `bin: "claude"`, `model: "sonnet"`, which the owner may change), `commandFor` returning `claude -p PROMPT --output-format stream-json --verbose --model MODEL --dangerously-skip-permissions` in the lane's worktree (check the flags with `claude --help`, the only Claude command you may run), `ADAPTERS` `claude: 'claude_local'`, `createRunLineReader('claude')`, and `summarizeRun` for its tool calls. (3) A hand-written fixture of about 30 lines in that shape (an init line, reads and edits, a failed Bash, a `Task` child with two reads of its own while the parent reads too, the result line), with only sandbox paths, as `public/fixtures/scenario-h-*` in the same files as `scenario-g`, and the fake Paperclip plays it for its `claude_local` agent only if that is a one-line change. Tests: each tool name's touches, a failed result, the child's spawn, its own calls under its key, its end, a line split across two chunks, the `acpx` tests unchanged, the command for the lane, and the lane source listing `claude`. Do not run Claude Code headless: it spends the owner's Claude subscription; Claude checks a real run after the merge. Files: `src/model/parsers/claude.js`, `tests/parsers.test.js` or a new `tests/claude-stream.test.js`, `public/fixtures/scenario-h-*` (new), `scripts/fake-paperclip.mjs` (only for the one line), `docs/agents/lanes.json`, `src/briefs/run.js`, `src/briefs/recording.js`, `tests/run-lane.test.js`, `tests/lanes-source.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the harness table and the activity paragraph), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

### Lane mode: the drawer and the cost board without Paperclip (2026-10-24)

With `--lanes` and no Paperclip, the room's agents are the lanes, but the cost board says `Costs not available.` and the drawer's Tasks, Approvals and Meters tabs read only Paperclip. These two briefs make them read the lane records instead, whenever the lane source is on and Paperclip does not answer. Paperclip, when it answers, still wins, so nothing changes for a Paperclip room. Both briefs reach the lane source from `server/control.js`, which `server/index.js` creates before the lane source: pass a getter (`lanes: () => lanesSource`) into `createControlApi` and keep your own logic in your own new module, so the two merges touch `server/control.js` and `server/index.js` in only a few lines each.

**L1. Costs and meters from the lane records (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.242).** (1) A new pure module `server/lane-costs.js`: from the records in `runs.jsonl` (read with the helpers in `server/runs.js`) and the lane agents of the lane source, build the `Costs` shape of `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`: `byAgent` one row per lane agent (`agentId` the lane agent's id, `agentName` its name, `costCents` the sum of `costUsd * 100` of its records this calendar month, rounded, `inputTokens` and `outputTokens` null since the records hold none), `summary` with `spendCents` their sum, `budgetCents` 0 and `utilizationPercent` null, and `quota` an empty list. A record with `costUsd` null adds nothing (AGY and Claude Code report none). Project runs (`project` set) count under their lane like any other. (2) `GET /api/control/costs` and the spend part of `GET /api/control/agents`: when the lane source is on and Paperclip fails or does not answer, answer from that module instead of an error. (3) The cost board and the Meters tab then show the lanes' spend with no client change; check that `costBoard` and the board read `budgetCents` 0 and null tokens without `NaN` (the board shows `not reported` for null tokens; spend with no budget shows no `over`). In the Meters tab, when the lane source is on and Paperclip does not answer, the Backups section says `Backups belong to Paperclip, which is not running.` instead of an error. Tests: the module on a temp `runs.jsonl` (two lanes, a null cost, a run last month left out, a project run counted); the route answering from it with the dead Paperclip at port 3999 and the lane source on; the route unchanged with the lane source off; `boardLines` on that shape with no `NaN`. Files: `server/lane-costs.js` (new), `server/control.js`, `server/index.js` (only to pass the getter), `src/engine/board.js` and `src/model/costs.js` (only if a `NaN` shows), `src/ui/panels/MetersPanel.jsx` and `src/ui/panels/backup.js` (the Backups sentence), `tests/lane-costs.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md` (one line on lane mode), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the cost board line), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**L2. Tasks, approvals and the callout from the lane runs (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.243).** (1) A new pure module `server/lane-tasks.js`: from the lane source's open runs and the newest 30 records in `runs.jsonl`, build issues in the shape `issues()` returns (`docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`): one per run, `id` the run's log name, `identifier` the brief ids joined with `+` or `project` for a project run, `title` the brief titles from `docs/agents/TASKS.md` (`parseBriefs`) or the first 80 characters of the task, `status` `in_progress` while the run is open, `done` on exit 0, `blocked` on a failure, `assigneeAgentId` the lane agent's id, and the times. Give the lane source a small read-only accessor for its open runs (`openRuns()`: name, lane, startedAt, the meta's project) if it has none. (2) `GET /api/control/issues` and `GET /api/control/live-runs`: when the lane source is on and Paperclip fails or does not answer, answer from that module, with live runs as the open lane runs (`issueId` the run's id) so the Tasks tab reads `running opencode` and the callout above a working lane shows its brief id (`agentTasks` in `src/model/liveTasks.js` already joins `liveRuns()` with `issues()`). Comments on a lane task answer an empty list, and creating a task or a comment answers 409 `Lane runs start from Dispatch, not from a task.` (3) `GET /api/control/approvals` answers an empty list in that case, and the Approvals tab says `Lane runs need no approvals.` when the list is empty and the bridge's health says `lanes: true` with no Paperclip. (4) The lane source's open and finished runs send `issues` and `liveRuns` notices on `/events` the way Paperclip's run frames do (check `changeEvents` in `server/control.js`; the lane frames may already produce them, since they are shaped as Paperclip's). Tests: the module on a temp runs folder and records (an open run, a good one, a failed one, a project run with its task as title); the routes with the dead Paperclip and the lane source on; the routes unchanged with it off; `agentTasks` giving the brief id for a working lane. Files: `server/lane-tasks.js` (new), `server/lanes-source.js` (only the accessor), `server/control.js`, `server/index.js` (only to pass the getter), `src/ui/panels/ApprovalsPanel.jsx`, `tests/lane-tasks.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md` (one line on lane mode), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Live mode), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

Not dispatched until the owner says so. `docs/agents/HARNESSES.md` holds the plan and the list of places a harness is named today. ## Your own sessions (2026-10-24, the owner's choice)

The room shows only runs started through `run-lane`. The owner works on other projects in interactive Claude Code and OpenCode sessions, and wants those in the room too, read-only, from the files those harnesses already write under the home folder (the owner said yes to reading them). A sessions source follows the sessions whose folder is the room's project root and sends the same frames the lane source sends (`server/lanes-source.js`: `heartbeat.run.queued`, `heartbeat.run.status` running, `heartbeat.run.log` with `stream: 'stdout'` and a growing `seq`, then `heartbeat.run.status` succeeded or failed, same frame shape and `companyId` style), one run per turn. Each harness has a reader with one interface: `createXSessionsReader({ projectRoot, runsFolder, now, quietMs, emit, ... })` returns `{ agent, tick, stop }`, where `agent` is `{ id, key, name, adapterType, parser, role: null }`, `tick()` reads what is new (it may return a promise) and calls `emit(frame)` for each frame, and `stop()` closes what it opened. A reader never sends a user's prompt text or the assistant's prose, only tool calls and their results, and it skips the sessions that `run-lane` itself started, so a project run never shows twice.

**S7a. Follow Claude Code sessions on the room's project (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.255).** (1) `server/claude-sessions.js`: `createClaudeSessionsReader({ projectRoot, home = os.homedir(), runsFolder, now, quietMs = 30000, emit })`, agent `{ id: 'agent-claude-session', key: 'claude-session', name: 'Claude Code session', adapterType: 'claude_local', parser: 'claude', role: null }`. Claude Code writes each session to `<home>/.claude/projects/<slug>/<sessionId>.jsonl`, where the slug is the folder path with every character that is not a letter or digit turned into `-` (`C:\Users\<name>\work\app` becomes `C--Users--name--work-app`; build the expected slug in a test from a temp path). Read only that folder, and keep a line only when its `cwd` resolves to the project root or inside it. A file that exists when the reader starts is read from its current end, and a file that appears later from its first byte. Read appended bytes only and hold a partial last line (cap 1 MB, then skip to the next newline), as `lanes-source.js` does. (2) Keep only lines with `type` `assistant` or `user` whose `message.content` is an array; keep only its `tool_use` items and `tool_result` items (cut a `tool_result`'s text content to its first 2000 characters), drop every other item, and write the line as `{"type":"assistant"|"user","message":{"content":[...]}}` plus `parent_tool_use_id` for a child line: the stream-json shape `src/model/parsers/claude.js` reads. A line with no kept item sends nothing. (3) Turns: a top-level `user` line whose content is a prompt (a string, or text items with no `tool_result`) starts a new run (`queued`, then `running`) with `runId` `<sessionId>:<n>`; the run ends `succeeded` at a top-level `assistant` line whose `message.stop_reason` is `end_turn`, or after `quietMs` with no growth; a tool line with no open run opens one. (4) Subagents: a parent `tool_result` line whose `toolUseResult.agentId` is set names the child's file `<sessionId>/subagents/agent-<agentId>.jsonl`, and its `tool_use_id` is the `Agent` call. Read that file the same way and send its kept lines in the parent's open run with `parent_tool_use_id` set to that call id, so the parser shows the child with its own tool calls. (5) Skip run-lane's own Claude runs: `commandFor` in `src/briefs/run.js` takes a `sessionId` and adds `--session-id <id>` to the claude command, `scripts/run-lane.mjs` makes it with `crypto.randomUUID()` and writes it as `sessionId` in the `<log>.meta.json` it already writes, and the reader skips a session whose id is the `sessionId` of any `*.meta.json` in `runsFolder` (read them when it first sees a session file). (6) `server/sessions-source.js`: `createSessionsSource({ projectRoot, runsFolder, onFrame, readers, pollMs = 1000 })` builds the readers it is given (default: the Claude reader; S7b adds the OpenCode one at merge), exposes `agents` (each reader's agent), and runs each reader's `tick` on a timer, one reader at a time and never two ticks at once, catching a reader's error so the others go on; `start()` and `stop()`. (7) Wiring in `server/index.js` the way `lanesSource` is wired: option `sessionsSource` or `MACHINEROOM_SESSIONS=1`, frames through `accept()`, its agents in `/api/agents` beside the lane agents (in the Paperclip-down and Paperclip-up answers), their parsers registered beside the lane parsers, `start()` and `stop()` with the bridge, and `sessions: true` in `/api/health` when on. `bin/machine-room.mjs` gets `--sessions` (a usage line beside `--lanes`). `src/ui/healthNotice.js` stays quiet about a missing Paperclip when `sessions` is true, as it does for `lanes`. Tests: the reader on a temp home with hand-written lines (a turn with a Read and an Edit, a prompt line sending nothing, a child file, a skipped run-lane session, a file present at start read from its end, a split line), the source's tick order and error isolation, `--session-id` in `commandFor`, and the health field. Files: `server/claude-sessions.js`, `server/sessions-source.js`, `server/index.js`, `bin/machine-room.mjs`, `src/briefs/run.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `src/ui/healthNotice.js`, new `tests/claude-sessions.test.js`, `tests/run-lane.test.js`, `tests/health-notice.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Live mode), `README.md` (one paragraph under Use it on another project), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S7b. Follow OpenCode sessions on the room's project (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.256).** `server/opencode-sessions.js`: `createOpencodeSessionsReader({ projectRoot, runsFolder, dbPath, now, quietMs = 30000, emit })` with the reader interface above, agent `{ id: 'agent-opencode-session', key: 'opencode-session', name: 'OpenCode session', adapterType: 'opencode_local', parser: 'opencode', role: null }`. Do not wire it into the bridge; Claude adds it to `server/sessions-source.js` at merge. (1) OpenCode 2 keeps sessions in SQLite at `$XDG_DATA_HOME/opencode/opencode.db`, else `<home>/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db` (also on Windows); `dbPath` overrides. Open it with `node:sqlite` as `new DatabaseSync(path, { readOnly: true })`, only when the file exists (no database: no frames and no error), and close it in `stop()`. The database also holds account tokens and credentials: query only `session_v2` and `session_message`, naming each column, and never read another table. (2) Sessions: `session_v2` rows whose `directory` resolves to the project root (ignore case on Windows), plus their child sessions (`parent_id` set to one of them). Skip a session whose `title` names a log in `runsFolder` (`<title>.log` exists): `run-lane` passes its log name as the OpenCode title. (3) Each tick reads the `session_message` rows of those sessions with a `time_updated` newer than the last one handled for that row, since assistant rows are updated in place while they stream. A row of `type` `user` starts a new run (`queued`, `running`) with `runId` `<sessionId>:<seq>`. `assistant` rows carry `data.content`, a list whose `tool` items are `{ type: 'tool', id, name, state: { status, input, content, metadata } }`; when an item's `state.status` first reads `completed` or `error`, send one log line in the shape `opencode run --format json` prints and `src/model/parsers/opencode.js` reads: `{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":...,"sessionID":...,"part":{"id":ID,"tool":NAME,"state":{"status":...,"input":...,"output":TEXT}}}`, where TEXT is the item's text content joined and cut to 2000 characters, and never send the same item twice. A child session's tool items go into its root parent's open run. An `idle` row ends the run with its `data.outcome` (`succeeded`, else `failed`), and a run with no new rows for `quietMs` ends `succeeded`. A session present when the reader starts is read from its newest row on, with no replay of old turns. (4) Tests on a temp database built with `node:sqlite` holding just those two tables with the columns the reader uses (`session_v2`: `id`, `parent_id`, `directory`, `title`, `time_updated`; `session_message`: `id`, `session_id`, `type`, `seq`, `time_created`, `time_updated`, `data`): a turn with a read and an edit, an item sent once across two ticks while it streams, an `error` item, a child session's tool in the parent's run, a skipped run-lane session, another folder's session ignored, an existing session not replayed, and no database at all. Files: `server/opencode-sessions.js`, new `tests/opencode-sessions.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Task queues on a project (2026-10-24, the owner's choice)

The owner's milestone: MachineRoom runs a list of tasks on one of their projects automatically, one after another. The owner chose how results are kept: each task that ends well and passes the project's tests is committed on a new branch in the project; the queue never touches the project's main branch and never pushes.

**S8. Run a queue of tasks on a project, one commit per task on a new branch (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.258).** (1) `npm run run-lane -- <lane> --project <folder> --queue <file> [--test "<command>"] [--minutes N]`. The queue file is plain text: tasks separated by lines that hold only `---`, each task's text trimmed, empty tasks dropped, at most 20 tasks, each 1 to 4000 characters (the `--task` limit). `--queue` refuses `--task`, `--task-file`, `--allow-dirty` and a brief id, and refuses a project the existing project checks refuse (`projectProblems`). The project must be a git folder with no uncommitted changes and no untracked files: a queue needs a clean start. (2) The test command: `--test` when given, else `npm test` when the project's `package.json` has a `test` script, else none (then a task passes on the lane's exit code alone, and the summary says the project has no tests). Run it through the shell (`shell: true`, `windowsHide: true`), since on Windows `npm` is `npm.cmd`, which Node cannot start without one; the command is the owner's own `--test` text or the fixed `npm test`, never text from a task or a page. Run it in the project folder, with the time limit `--minutes` gives a task, and stop it with `killTree` on timeout. (3) Before the first task, make and switch to a new branch `machineroom/queue-<stamp>` (the stamp format the logs use) from the project's current `HEAD`, with git by `gitPath()`; refuse when that branch exists. Each task then runs as a project run does today (its own log, meta file with `queue` and `index`, record in `runs.jsonl` with `queue: <branch>` and `queueIndex`, so the room shows each task live as its own run), then: a non-zero exit or a kill stops the queue; else the test command runs, and a failing test stops the queue; else, when `git status --porcelain` shows changes, `git add -A` and `git commit` with the message `<first line of the task, cut to 72 characters>` and a body `Task N of M, run by MachineRoom on the <lane> lane.`; a task with no change is noted and the queue goes on. A stop leaves that task's changes uncommitted on the branch, so the owner can look at them. (4) After the queue, print a summary: the branch, then one line per task (`N. committed <short sha>`, `N. no change`, `N. failed: lane exit 1`, `N. failed: tests`, `N. not run`), and leave the project on the queue branch; say so in the summary, with the command to go back (`git switch -`). (5) Put the order of work in a pure function `runQueue({ tasks, runTask, runTests, git })` in a new `src/briefs/queue.js`, with `parseQueue(text)` and `commitMessage(task, index, count, lane)`; `scripts/run-lane.mjs` wires the real steps into it. (6) Tests: `parseQueue` (separators, trimming, empty tasks, the limits), `commitMessage`, and `runQueue` on a real temp git repository with a fake `runTask` that writes files: three tasks commit three times on the new branch with main untouched; a no-change task goes on; a failed task and a failed test each stop the queue with the later tasks `not run` and the failed task's change left uncommitted; an existing branch and a dirty start are refused; `--queue` with `--task` is refused (through `--dry-run`, which prints the parsed tasks, the branch it would make and the test command, and runs nothing). Build each test path from the temp helper, never from a real home path. Files: `src/briefs/queue.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, new `tests/run-queue.test.js`, `README.md` (a short paragraph under Use it on another project), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the run-lane line in Commands), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**S8b. A task queue from the Dispatch panel (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.259).** S8 adds `run-lane <lane> --project <folder> --queue <file> [--test "<command>"]` (plain text, tasks split by lines that hold only `---`, at most 20 tasks of 1 to 4000 characters each). The owner wants to start a queue from the room as a user does, without a terminal. (1) `server/runs.js`: `POST /api/control/runs` with `{ lane, tasks: [text, ...], confirmed: true }` and no `briefs` and no `task` starts a queue on the room's project: check the list (1 to 20 tasks, each a string of 1 to 4000 characters after trimming, no task that holds a line of only `---`), write the tasks joined by `\n---\n` to `<runs folder>/<lane>-queue-<stamp>.queue.txt`, and spawn `run-lane <lane> --project <root> --queue <that file>` the way the project run spawns today (node, `shell: false`, cwd the repository root). `allowDirty` with `tasks` is refused (a queue needs a clean project), and so are `task` and `tasks` together. The project checks that refuse a project run refuse a queue. The answer and the GET list carry `queue: true` and the task count. (2) `src/ui/panels/DispatchPanel.jsx` and `src/ui/panels/runs.js`: in the Run on this project section, the task box takes one task or several separated by a line of `---`; a pure `splitTasks(text)` in `runs.js` turns the box into the list, and `projectTaskProblem` checks each task. With more than one task the button reads `Run N tasks in order`, the allow-uncommitted switch is off and disabled with the note `A queue needs a clean project`, and the confirm card says the tasks run one after another on a new branch in the project, each committed when the project's tests pass, stopping at the first failure, never on main and never pushed. One task keeps today's behaviour exactly. (3) The control client (`src/control/client.js` and the mock) passes `tasks` through `startRun`. Tests: the route (a queue writes its file and spawns with `--queue`, the refusals, the GET entry), `splitTasks` (one task, three tasks, blank blocks, a `---` with spaces around it), and the panel helpers. Do not change `scripts/run-lane.mjs` or `src/briefs/queue.js`; they belong to S8. Files: `server/runs.js`, `src/ui/panels/DispatchPanel.jsx`, `src/ui/panels/runs.js`, `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, `tests/project-dispatch.test.js`, a panel helper test file, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Live mode, the L3 sentence), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## An orchestrator inside the room (2026-10-24, the owner's choice)

The owner wants to give requirements to an orchestrator from the room itself, the way they talk to Claude in Claude Code today: it asks what it needs to know, splits the work into tasks, delegates them to lanes in parallel, waits, reads each report, checks the work, merges what passes, and reports back. The orchestrator is a lane named `orchestrator` (Claude Code on Opus first; OpenCode can follow). The bridge runs one turn of it per message, resumes the same Claude session each time, and wakes it with a message of its own when a lane run on the project ends. The three briefs below fit together through the contract in K1; build to it exactly.

**K1. The orchestrator in the bridge (OpenCode 7, merged as 0.1.269).** (1) `docs/agents/lanes.json`: a lane `orchestrator`, name `Orchestrator`, harness `Claude Code`, `cli` `{ "harness": "claude", "bin": "claude", "model": "opus" }`, no worktree, no `role` text needed (the room already seats an agent named Orchestrator at the hub centre through `isOrchestrator`). `run-lane` must refuse to start it on a brief (`briefProblems` or the lane check: `The orchestrator lane runs from the room, not from run-lane.`). (2) A new `server/orchestrator.js`, `createOrchestrator({ projectRoot, runsFolder, lanes, spawn, now, onNotice })`, mounted in `server/index.js` with one call and one `orchestrator: true` field in `GET /api/health` when the lane source is on. Routes: `GET /api/orchestrator` answers `{ state: 'idle' | 'working' | 'paused', model, project, messages: [{ id, from: 'owner' | 'orchestrator' | 'room', text, at }] }` (the last 200). `POST /api/orchestrator/messages` with `{ text, confirmed: true }` adds an owner message (text trimmed, 1 to 8000 characters, else 400; no `confirmed: true`, 400; no project root, 409) and answers 202 `{ id }`; when a turn is running the message waits and the next turn takes all waiting messages together. `POST /api/orchestrator/stop` with `{ confirmed: true }` stops the running turn with `killTree`, sets `paused` and answers 200; the next owner message clears `paused`. (3) A turn: spawn the lane's Claude with `shell: false`, cwd the project root: the first turn `claude -p <text> --output-format stream-json --verbose --model <model> --dangerously-skip-permissions --session-id <new uuid> --append-system-prompt <prompt>`, later turns the same with `--resume <that uuid>` in place of `--session-id`. The prompt is `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` from this repository (K3 writes it) with `{{PROJECT}}`, `{{MACHINEROOM}}` and `{{RUNS}}` replaced by the absolute paths; when the file is missing use a three-sentence fallback that says who it is and that it must never push. Write stdout to `<runs>/orchestrator-chat-<stamp>.log` with a `<same name>.meta.json` holding `{ project, sessionId }` (so the lane source shows each turn at the hub centre and the sessions source skips the session), and when the turn ends append a run record to `runs.jsonl` with `runRecord` from `src/briefs/run.js` (lane `orchestrator`, `log`, `exitCode`, `killed`, `costUsd` from the result line) so the lane source ends the run. The reply is the text of the last top-level `result` line (export the existing `claudeReport` reader from `src/briefs/run.js`, or a small reader of your own); an empty reply becomes `The orchestrator ended its turn without a reply.` and a failed exit `The orchestrator stopped: exit N.` A turn has a 60 minute limit. (4) Waking: the bridge passes the lane source's frames to the orchestrator; when a `heartbeat.run.status` frame says `succeeded` or `failed` for a run whose lane is not `orchestrator`, and the orchestrator has a session and is not paused, add a `room` message `Run <log name> on lane <lane> ended: <succeeded or failed>.` followed by the run record's lane, briefs or project, exit code, branch, commits and cost when the record is there, and start a turn (or let it wait for the running one). At most 30 room-started turns in any rolling hour; past that the room messages wait for the owner. (5) Keep the conversation in `<runs>/orchestrator-<a short hash of the project path>.json` (session id, messages, state), read at start, so a restart keeps the chat and resumes the same session. Send an SSE notice `orchestrator` on every new message and state change, the way `liveRuns` is sent. (6) The control client: `orchestrator()` and `sendToOrchestrator(text, { confirmed })` and `stopOrchestrator({ confirmed })` in `src/control/client.js`, in `CLIENT_METHODS`, and in `src/control/mock.js` with a short canned chat; the contract test covers them. Tests (`tests/orchestrator.test.js`, new): build the spawn with a fake Claude, a small node script in the test's temp folder that prints stream-json lines and a result line, started for real; first turn uses `--session-id`, the second `--resume` with the same id; the prompt placeholders are filled; a message during a turn waits and goes in the next; a lane run end wakes a turn with the record in the text; the hourly cap; stop kills and pauses; the JSON file restores the chat; the routes' refusals; the lane source sees the turn's log (meta project) and ends it on the record. Never start the real `claude` in a test. Files: `server/orchestrator.js`, `server/index.js`, `src/briefs/run.js` (export only), `scripts/run-lane.mjs` and `src/briefs/run.js` (the refusal only), `docs/agents/lanes.json`, `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, the control contract files and test, `tests/orchestrator.test.js`, `docs/agents/CONTROL-CLIENT.md`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Live mode: one paragraph), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**K2. The orchestrator chat in the room (OpenCode 8, merged as 0.1.266).** A beginner must find it and use it without reading a manual. (1) A new `src/ui/OrchestratorChat.jsx` and its pure helpers in `src/ui/orchestratorChat.js`: a panel at the bottom left over the scene, in the room's style (Jost and Space Mono, 400 and 500, white, `#e5ff00` active, `#ff2a2a` failure, `#2aff80` finished, on black, 8px spacing), with the conversation (owner lines right aligned, orchestrator lines left, room lines dim and small: `room`), a status line (`Orchestrator is working`, `Orchestrator is waiting for you`, `Paused`), a text box (Enter sends, Shift+Enter is a new line, 8000 characters at most with a count past 7000), a Send button and a Stop button while it works. The first message of a chat shows a confirm card first: `The orchestrator runs Claude Code (Opus) on this project with full permissions. It plans, starts lanes, merges what passes on a branch and never pushes. It uses your Claude subscription.` with Start and Cancel; later messages send at once. Stop also asks first. It reads `client.orchestrator()` on open, on every `orchestrator` notice and every 10 seconds while open, and calls `client.sendToOrchestrator(text, { confirmed: true })` and `client.stopOrchestrator({ confirmed: true })` (K1 adds them; until then guard with `typeof client.orchestrator === 'function'`). (2) `src/ui/Hud.jsx`: when `health.orchestrator` is true, an `ORCHESTRATOR` button beside CONTROL opens and closes the chat, `/` opens the chat instead of the assistant terminal, and the help list says so (`/  Talk to the orchestrator`); the old assistant terminal stays for replays and when there is no orchestrator. (3) `src/ui/Waiting.jsx`: when the orchestrator is on, the waiting card says `Nothing is running yet. Press / or click ORCHESTRATOR and tell it what you want built.`; otherwise keep the lane wording but drop every mention of Paperclip. (4) Styles in `src/styles.css` under `.mr-orch` names. Tests: the pure helpers (which status line, which lines show, the counter, the confirm rule: first message only, the Enter rule) and a render test of the panel with a fake client if the repository already has one for panels. Do not touch `src/Stage.jsx` or `src/App.jsx`. Files: `src/ui/OrchestratorChat.jsx`, `src/ui/orchestratorChat.js`, `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/ui/Waiting.jsx`, `src/styles.css`, a test file, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (HUD and Assistant lines), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**K3. Worktrees for parallel lanes on a project, and the orchestrator's instructions (OpenCode 9, merged as 0.1.267).** (1) `scripts/run-lane.mjs` and `src/briefs/run.js`: `--worktree` with `--project <folder>` (with `--task`, `--task-file` or `--queue`) runs the lane in `<parent of the project>/<project folder name>-mr-<lane>`, a git worktree of the project: create it with `git worktree add --detach <path> HEAD` from the project when it is missing, reuse it when it is there and clean (refuse when it has uncommitted changes, with the path in the message), and give it a `node_modules` link to the project's own when the project has one (a directory junction on Windows, a symlink elsewhere). The run's meta file and record name the worktree as `project`, so the room started on the project shows it (0.1.263). A queue with `--worktree` makes its branch there. `--dry-run` prints the worktree path and whether it would be made. Pure helpers (`worktreeFor(project, lane)` and the argument checks) in `src/briefs/run.js` with tests; the git part tested on a real temp repository. (2) A new `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`, the system prompt K1 appends for the orchestrator lane, with the placeholders `{{PROJECT}}`, `{{MACHINEROOM}}` and `{{RUNS}}`. It says, in plain words and short sentences: you are the orchestrator for the project at {{PROJECT}}, talking to its owner through MachineRoom; first find out what the owner wants (ask short questions, at most three at a time, and wait for the answers; a message from `room` is a lane report, not the owner); read the project's own agent guide (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md or docs/agents) before planning; split the work into tasks that touch different files; write each task to a file under {{RUNS}} and start it with `node {{MACHINEROOM}}/scripts/run-lane.mjs <lane> --project {{PROJECT}} --worktree --queue <file>` in the background, at most one run per lane, lanes `opencode` to `opencode10` and `claude`, at least 3 seconds apart; end your turn after starting them and say what you started (the room wakes you when each run ends); when a run ends, read its record and log, check the branch (diff, the project's tests and build in that worktree), merge what passes into the project's current branch with a straight-line history, or write a follow-up task; report to the owner what landed, what failed and what is next; never push, never delete a branch you did not make, never touch MachineRoom's own files, never print a key. Keep it under 120 lines. (3) Tests for the helpers and the worktree. Files: `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `src/briefs/run.js`, `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`, `tests/run-worktree.test.js` (new), `README.md` (one paragraph under Use it on another project), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the run-lane line), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## A room a beginner can read (2026-10-24, the owner's report)

The owner opened the room and could not tell what pointed where. 0.1.264 made each agent's line one solid tunnel to its file and put the project name at the hub centre; this brief explains the room on screen.

**N1. A first-visit guide and a legend that says what each thing is (OpenCode 8, merged as 0.1.270).** (1) A new `src/ui/Guide.jsx` with pure helpers in `src/ui/guide.js`: on the first visit (a `machineroom.guide` flag through `src/storage.js`, wrapped so a blocked storage shows it every time and never throws) a card in the middle of the screen with five short steps, Next and Skip, each step one sentence and a small label of the part it names: the middle is your project, its name under the ring, and the folder cards branch out from it; each shape on the ring is an agent, with its chip (name and status) beside it; a working agent sends a yellow tunnel to the file it is touching, and packets run along it; colours: white is a read or write, yellow is working, green is finished, red is a failure; press / or click ORCHESTRATOR to say what you want built, C opens the control drawer, ? lists the keys. The last step has Done. A `Guide` button in the HUD's help panel opens it again. (2) The legend in the HUD's top left panel (the four colour words) gets one more line: `Line from an agent: the file it is working on`. (3) Styles under `.mr-guide` in `src/styles.css`, in the room's style (Jost and Space Mono, weights 400 and 500, never bold, white, `#e5ff00`, `#ff2a2a`, `#2aff80` on black, 8px spacing), keyboard reachable (Tab, Enter, Escape closes), and it never opens over a confirm card.
