## Every lane busy before the deadline (2026-10-24, the owner's request)

The owner asked that all ten OpenCode lanes keep working. These briefs touch different files, so they run side by side.

**R1. The pack holds what the room reads at run time (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.284).** `package.json` `files` lists `bin/`, `server/` and some of `src/`, but the bridge also reads files at run time that the pack leaves out, for example `docs/agents/lanes.json` (the lane agents) and `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` (the orchestrator's instructions), and maybe `docs/agents/TASKS.md`, `docs/agents/HOUSE-RULES.md` and `docs/agents/policy.json`. (1) Find every file under this repository that `server/`, `bin/` and the `src/` files they import read with `readFileSync`, `readFile` or a URL at run time, and add each one the packed room needs to `files`. (2) `scripts/pack-check.mjs` fails when a file in a fixed list of run-time reads (kept beside its import walk) is not in the pack, and names it. (3) `tests/pack-start.test.js` starts the unpacked room with `--lanes` and checks that `/api/agents` lists the `opencode` lane and `GET /api/orchestrator` answers 200. Files: `package.json`, `scripts/pack-check.mjs`, `tests/pack-start.test.js`, its pack-check test if there is one, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**R2. Token counts for OpenCode runs (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.279).** Every OpenCode record in `runs.jsonl` has `inputTokens: null` and `outputTokens: null`, so the cost board reads `? in / ? out`. `opencode run --format json` prints `step_finish` lines whose `part.tokens` holds `input`, `output`, `reasoning` and `cache: { read, write }`. In `src/briefs/run.js`, give `opencodeSummary` an `opencodeUsage(log)` that sums them over the run (input plus both cache counts as input, output plus reasoning as output, null when no line has them), the way `claudeUsage` does for Claude Code. Check that `scripts/run-lane.mjs` writes them into the record for brief, project and queue runs, and fix it where it does not. Take the line shape from a real log in `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs` (any `opencode*.log`), and put a short masked sample in the test, not a whole log. Tests: a log with two steps, a log with none, a step with only some fields. Do not touch `server/orchestrator.js`. Files: `src/briefs/run.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs` if needed, `tests/run-usage.test.js` (new) or the existing run test, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**R3. OpenCode can be the orchestrator (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.283).** The owner wants Claude Code and OpenCode first. `server/orchestrator.js` runs only `claude -p`. (1) Read the harness from the `orchestrator` entry in `docs/agents/lanes.json` (`cli.harness`, `claude` or `opencode`, and its model); keep `claude` as the entry's value. (2) For `opencode`, a turn runs `opencode run --format json --model MODEL --title LOGNAME` with the message, plus `--session ID` after the first turn, where ID is the `sessionID` the first turn's JSON lines carry (save it in the state file the way the Claude session id is saved). OpenCode has no appended system prompt, so the first turn's message starts with the filled `ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` text and then the owner's message. Check these flags against `opencode run --help` on this machine and say in your report what you found. The reply is the last assistant text of the turn (read what `opencodeReport` in `src/briefs/run.js` does). The lane source shows the turn with the OpenCode parser. (3) The record's cost and tokens come from `summarizeRun({ harness, log })` for both harnesses, in place of `costOf`. Tests in `tests/orchestrator.test.js` with the fake child the file already uses: the OpenCode command line on the first and a later turn, the session id read and saved, the reply, the record. Files: `server/orchestrator.js`, `tests/orchestrator.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the orchestrator paragraph in Live mode), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**R4. The orchestrator after a room restart (OpenCode 3, with R3, merged as 0.1.283).** When the bridge stops during a turn, the saved state can read `working` with no turn behind it, and the chat says the orchestrator is working forever. (1) When the orchestrator loads a state file whose state is `working`, it sets `idle` and adds a `room` message `The room restarted during a turn. Send the message again if it still matters.` (2) Stopping the bridge kills a running turn with `killTree` and saves the state. (3) An owner message that was waiting for the next turn is kept in the state file and starts a turn after a restart. Tests in `tests/orchestrator.test.js`. Files: as R3.

**R5. Dispatch can start a lane in its own worktree (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.281).** The route takes `worktree: true` for a project task or queue (`server/runs.js`), and the orchestrator uses it, but the Dispatch panel never sends it, so two lanes started from the panel on one project refuse each other. In `src/ui/panels/DispatchPanel.jsx` add a switch `Own worktree` beside the allow-uncommitted switch, on by default, which sends `worktree: true`; when it is on, the allow-uncommitted switch is off and hidden; the confirm card says the lane works in `<project folder>-mr-<lane>` beside the project, on its own branch, never on main. **R6. Audit of the orchestrator and the worktree runs (OpenCode 6, read only, merged as 0.1.288).** Read what changed from 0.1.255 to the newest commit (`git log` and `git diff` on `refs/heads/main`), above all `server/orchestrator.js`, `server/runs.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs` (`--worktree`), `server/lanes-source.js` (S9), `src/ui/OrchestratorChat.jsx` and `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`. ## Orchestrators in a ladder (2026-10-24, the owner's choice)

The plan is `docs/agents/HIERARCHY.md`. Read it first.

**O1. The ladder model (OpenCode 2, pure, merged as 0.1.282).** A new `src/model/hierarchy.js`, no DOM and no Node imports, with: `readLadder(value, { lanes })` that returns `{ ladder, problems }` and applies every rule in the Rules list of `HIERARCHY.md` (lanes is the list of lane keys from `docs/agents/lanes.json`); `ownerOf(ladder, laneKey)` (a sub id or `orchestrator`); `inScope(path, scope)` for the globs (`**`, `*`, a plain folder meaning everything under it, `/` separators, case insensitive on Windows paths given as a flag); `outsideScope(paths, sub)` (the paths a restricted sub may not change, empty for an open sub); `canChange(ladder, change, by)` (the main orchestrator cannot change an owner-set scope or restriction); `sectorArcs(ladder, roster)` returning each sector's start and end angle and its lane keys in roster order, the main orchestrator's arc for the rest, with a small gap at each end, and the same angles for the same input; `subPrompt(sub, parent)` returning the short instructions section `HIERARCHY.md` describes. **O2. Sub orchestrators on the bridge (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.305).** Read `docs/agents/HIERARCHY.md` and `src/model/hierarchy.js` (O1) first. (1) A registry, `server/orchestrators.js`, that holds the main orchestrator (`createOrchestrator` as today, id `orchestrator`) and one more `createOrchestrator` per sub in the ladder, each with its own id, chat file (`orchestrator-<hash>-<id>.json` for a sub), log names (`sub-<id>-chat-<stamp>.log`), harness and model from the sub, and its instructions: `ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` plus `subPrompt(sub, parent)`. The ladder lives in the main orchestrator's chat file under `ladder`, read through `readLadder` with the lane keys of `docs/agents/lanes.json`. (2) Routes, keeping today's ones working for the main orchestrator: `GET /api/orchestrators` lists `{ id, name, harness, model, state, lanes, scope, restricted, setBy }` for the main and each sub; `GET /api/orchestrator?id=ID`, `POST /api/orchestrator/messages` and `POST /api/orchestrator/stop` take an optional `id`; `POST /api/orchestrator/ladder` with `{ ladder, by, confirmed: true }` replaces the ladder after `readLadder` (answer 400 with the problems) and `canChange` (answer 403 when the main orchestrator, `by: "orchestrator"`, changes what the owner set; a request from a page is always `by: "owner"`, so only a request with no Origin may say `orchestrator`), starts the new subs and stops the removed ones. A message from the main orchestrator to a sub is `POST /api/orchestrator/messages` with `{ id, text, from: "orchestrator", confirmed: true }`, also only from a request with no Origin. (3) `POST /api/control/runs` takes `startedBy` (an orchestrator id): a sub may start only lanes in its own sector (answer 403 naming the sector), and `run-lane` gets `--started-by ID`, which it writes into the run record as `startedBy`. A run's end wakes only the orchestrator named by `startedBy` (the main one when there is none). (4) For a restricted sub, when its lane run ends, the bridge lists the files the lane branch changed (`git diff --name-only` against the branch the worktree started from) and, when `outsideScope` finds any, the wake message starts with `Outside the scope, do not merge:` and the files, and the main orchestrator gets the same room message. (5) When a sub's turn ends, its reply goes into its parent's chat as a message from `sub:<id>` and wakes the parent, counted in the parent's 30 room turns an hour. (6) `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` gets a short section for the main orchestrator: how to attach, change and remove a sub with the ladder route, how to send it a goal, that the owner's settings win, and that a sub's report arrives as a `sub:` message. (7) `GET /api/agents` lists each sub as an agent with key `sub-<id>`, name, the parser of its harness and `subOrchestrator: true`, and `server/lanes-source.js` reads its `sub-<id>-chat-*` logs as that agent. Tests with fake children in the style of `tests/orchestrator.test.js`: the routes, a refused ladder, an owner-set scope the main orchestrator cannot change, a sub that cannot start a lane outside its sector, a wake that goes only to the starter, the scope warning, a sub report waking the parent, and the main orchestrator unchanged with no ladder. Files: `server/orchestrators.js` (new), `server/orchestrator.js`, `server/index.js` (mounting only), `server/runs.js`, `server/lanes-source.js` (the sub logs only), `scripts/run-lane.mjs` and `src/briefs/run.js` (`--started-by` only), `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`, `tests/orchestrators.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the orchestrator paragraph), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**O3. Ring sectors in the room (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.295).** Read `docs/agents/HIERARCHY.md` (How it looks) and `sectorArcs` in `src/model/hierarchy.js` first. (1) `room.setLadder(ladder)` in `src/engine/room.js`: with a ladder of one or more subs, the hub ring (`src/engine/hub.js`) draws one arc per sector from `sectorArcs` with a gap at each end and a small text label with the sector's name outside the ring, and each lane parks at a slot inside its sector's arc, in roster order (`src/engine/agents.js`); with no ladder or an empty one, the ring and slots are exactly as today. (2) A sub orchestrator is an agent whose key starts with `sub-` or that carries `subOrchestrator: true`: it floats above the middle of its arc at 0.7 of the main orchestrator's size, with the orchestrator's shape family (`src/engine/shapes.js`), a thin white line to the main orchestrator, and its chip and callout like any agent; `isOrchestrator` must stay true only for the main orchestrator (today it matches any name with `orchestrat`; keep that for the main one and leave subs out). (3) The layout stays deterministic and calm under reduced motion. (4) For a check by eye with no bridge, `?ladder=demo` in replay sets a fixed ladder of two subs over the replay's agents (`src/Stage.jsx`, a few lines). Pure helpers with tests: the slot angles per lane from a ladder, and the place of a sub orchestrator; `tests/hub.test.js` or a new `tests/sectors.test.js`; the existing hub and agent tests pass unchanged. Check by eye with `?replay&fixture=scenario-e&ladder=demo` and without `ladder`. Do not touch `src/ui/Hud.jsx`. Files: `src/engine/hub.js`, `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/engine/shapes.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, `src/Stage.jsx` (the demo flag only), `tests/sectors.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Hub and Agents in Room behaviour), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**O4. Chat tabs and the attach form (OpenCode 5, merged as 0.1.291).** Read `docs/agents/HIERARCHY.md` and the O2 brief above: O2 builds the routes on the bridge, this brief builds the page against those exact shapes, tested with the mock. (1) The control client (`src/control/client.js`, the contract list in `src/control/`, and `src/control/mock.js`): `orchestrators()` (GET `/api/orchestrators`), `orchestrator(id)`, `sendOrchestrator(text, { id })`, `stopOrchestrator({ id })` with the id optional, and `setLadder(ladder)` (POST `/api/orchestrator/ladder` with `by: "owner"` and `confirmed: true`); the mock keeps a ladder in memory, applies `readLadder` and answers like O2. (2) `src/ui/OrchestratorChat.jsx`: when there is at least one sub, a row of tabs at the top of the chat, Main first and then one per sub with its name and a state dot; each tab shows that orchestrator's chat and sends to it. (3) An Attach button opens a small form: name, id (filled from the name, editable), harness (`claude` or `opencode`), model, lanes (a checkbox per lane that is in no other sub), scope (one glob per line), and a Restricted switch on by default; a confirm card names the sub, its lanes and scope; Save calls `setLadder` with the whole new ladder and shows the problems the bridge answers. Each sub tab has Detach behind a confirm card. (4) Pure helpers in a new `src/ui/ladderForm.js`: the form to a sub, the id from a name, the free lanes, the ladder with a sub added, changed or removed, each tested. (5) Styles in the chat section of `src/styles.css`. Do not touch `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/Stage.jsx` or `server/`. Tests: `tests/ladder-form.test.js` (new) and the client contract tests for the new methods. **O5. Context use and autocompact (OpenCode 8, merged as 0.1.311).** The Context and autocompact section of `docs/agents/HIERARCHY.md`, on the bridge (O2 merged as 0.1.305). (1) After each turn, read the context use of the session: for Claude Code the last top-level `result` line's `usage` (`input_tokens` plus both cache counts), for OpenCode the last `step_finish` tokens (input plus cache read and write). The window comes from a `contextWindow` number in the orchestrator's `cli` block in `docs/agents/lanes.json` (add `200000` there) or from the sub, else 200000. `GET /api/orchestrator` and `GET /api/orchestrators` add `contextTokens`, `contextWindow` and `autocompact` (true by default) per orchestrator, saved in its chat file. (2) `POST /api/orchestrator/autocompact` with `{ id, on, confirmed: true }` sets it. (3) With autocompact on, when a turn ends with the context at 80 percent or more, the room starts one room-started turn in the same session that asks for a summary of the work so far, the open runs and the next steps in at most 40 lines, saves the reply as the session summary, and clears the session id, so the next turn starts a new session whose first message begins with that summary. The chat gets a `room` message saying the session was compacted. (4) The page: `src/ui/agentContext.js` already shows Context when the data carries it; `src/ui/AgentDock.jsx` adds an Autocompact switch for an orchestrator that calls a new client method `setAutocompact(id, on)` (`src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, the contract), behind no confirm card. Tests: the context read for each harness, the 80 percent switch with a fake child, the route, the client and mock method, the dock switch. Do not touch the code that writes or restores the chat file, or masks text (Q6b changes it at the same time); add your fields through the existing persist and restore. ## The room as a wide lens with a dock below (2026-10-24, the owner's concept)

The owner's newest concept still (kept out of git) shows the look: a black page; a wide viewport across the top whose top and bottom edges bow outward a little, like a lens, with black bars above and below; thin one pixel lines and small Space Mono labels at its corners (a title bar with a code at the top left, a status line and a thin progress bar at the top right, a short line of small text at the bottom right). Keep the house colours: white lines at low opacity for the frame, yellow only for what is active, red only for failure.

**I1. The lens and the dock (OpenCode 8, merged as 0.1.287).** Change the page layout. (1) A menu row fixed at the top: the Replay and Live switch, CONTROL, ORCHESTRATOR, Settings, Help and the health chip, as they are today. (2) Below it, the 3D room in a viewport about 58 percent of the window height (at least 360 pixels), framed as described above: its top and bottom edges bow outward (a CSS `clip-path` with a curved path, or an SVG mask; the room must still take clicks everywhere inside it), black above and below, the corner labels showing the project name and version at the top left, the number of working lanes out of all lanes with a thin bar at the top right, and the current activity line (the newest log line) at the bottom right. Clicking an agent in the viewport selects it as today. The screen effects (fish eye and the rest) apply inside the viewport only. (3) Below the viewport, a dock that scrolls: three columns at 1280 pixels and wider (the agent list from N2 on the left, an empty middle column with the class `hud-dock-context` for the agent's context, which I2 fills, and the settings on the right, moved from the Settings panel into the dock), stacked at narrower widths. (4) The orchestrator chat is fixed at the bottom right over the dock and never scrolls with it; the menu never scrolls. Nothing else is fixed. (5) Keep every DOM contract class in `ARCHITECTURE.md`; the cost board moves into the dock's right column under the settings. Check by eye at 1280 by 800, 1920 by 1080 and 900 by 700 in replay with `?fixture=scenario-e`, and say what you checked. Do not touch `src/ui/OrchestratorChat.jsx` beyond its outer class names, or `src/ui/orchestratorChat.js`. Files: `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/ui/Settings.jsx`, `src/Stage.jsx` (the layout only), `src/engine/board.js` (only where the board mounts), `src/styles.css`, a pure helper and its test if you add logic, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (Room behaviour: Settings, HUD, Cost board), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**I2. The agent's context in the dock (OpenCode 5, merged as 0.1.286, mounted in the dock at 0.1.287).** A new `src/ui/AgentDock.jsx` and a pure `src/ui/agentContext.js`. `agentContext({ agent, lanes, costs, liveRuns, tasks, log })` returns what the dock shows for the selected agent (or the orchestrator when none is selected): name, key, harness and model (from the agent list the bridge sends), status word, the task it works on (as the callout does, from `agentTasks` in `src/model/liveTasks.js`), its current command, this month's cost and tokens (from the cost board rows), its last 12 log lines, and for an orchestrator the context use when the data carries it (`contextTokens` and `contextWindow`, shown as a share; absent until O5). `AgentDock` draws it in plain rows with Space Mono values, and buttons Focus (calls `onFocus(key)`), Log (calls `onToggleTerminal(key)`) and, for an orchestrator, Open chat (calls `onOpenChat()`). It takes all data as props and fetches nothing. Tests in a new `tests/agent-context.test.js`: an idle lane, a working lane with a task, a lane with no cost row (`not reported`), the orchestrator with and without context data, and the log cut to 12 lines. Do not mount it anywhere: I1 builds the dock and the orchestrator wires it in at the merge. Files: `src/ui/AgentDock.jsx`, `src/ui/agentContext.js`, `tests/agent-context.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**B2. A first-time user's README (OpenCode 10, merged as 0.1.296).** `README.md` should let someone who has never seen the room start it on their own project in five minutes. Rewrite its top part, keep the history line at the end: what the room is in two sentences; what you need (Node 24, git, OpenCode or Claude Code signed in); the steps (`npm install`, `npm run build`, `npm run serve -- up --root <your project folder>`, open the address it prints); what you see on screen (the hub and its agents, the folder cards, a tunnel to the file an agent works on, the colours and what each means, the chips, the agent list, CONTROL, ORCHESTRATOR); how to ask the orchestrator for work and what it does with it (plans, starts lanes in their own worktrees, reviews, merges, never pushes); how to start one lane by hand from the Dispatch panel; how to replay a recorded run; where the logs and run records are (`%TEMP%\machineroom-runs`); and the five most common problems with their fixes (a lane program not found, the doctor's FAIL lines, a dirty project refused, a port taken, no agents shown). Check every command and flag against `bin/machine-room.mjs`, `package.json` and `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and run `npm run serve -- doctor --root .` to see what the doctor prints. Plain words, short sentences, no em dashes. Files: `README.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**R10. Reviews for lanes that work on the room's project (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.289).** The Reviews tab (`server/reviews.js`, `src/ui/panels/ReviewsPanel.jsx`) reads each lane's branch in the worktree that `docs/agents/lanes.json` names, which is a worktree of MachineRoom itself. When the room runs on another project, lanes started with `worktree: true` work in `<project parent>/<project folder>-mr-<lane>` (see `worktreeFor` in `src/briefs/run.js`), and the tab shows nothing useful. (1) When the room's root is not this repository (compare `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, as `server/lanes-source.js` does), the reviews route lists each lane whose project worktree exists, with the same fields as today, comparing against the project's current branch (`git symbolic-ref --short HEAD` in the project) in place of `refs/heads/main`; the diff and the merge work the same way, the merge is a fast forward of the project's current branch to the lane branch (refuse it with a plain sentence when it is not a fast forward, and say to rebase), and it still needs `confirmed: true` and the exact head sha. (2) The panel says which branch of the project it compares against. (3) A lane with no project worktree is left out. Tests with a throwaway git project in the system temp folder, in the style of `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js`: a lane worktree with one commit listed, its diff, a fast forward merge, a refused merge when the project moved on, and the room on MachineRoom itself unchanged. **T1. A faster test suite (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.307).** Ten lanes and the orchestrator run `npm test` many times an hour, and a full run takes about two minutes on this machine. (1) Measure: the total time of three full runs, and the 15 slowest files and tests (`npx vitest run --reporter=json`, or the duration lines). (2) Make the suite faster by at least 30 percent with every test still running: for example `isolate: false` in `vite.config.js` for the pure test files that share no global state (vitest's own hint says about 7.5 seconds), one bridge or git repository per file in `beforeAll` where tests only read it, fake timers in place of real waits, smaller fixtures where a test only needs a few items. **H5. The harness registry, step H1 of the plan (OpenCode 9, merged as 0.1.292).** Step H1 of `docs/agents/HARNESSES.md`, read it first. Add `src/harnesses/index.js` with `harnessByKey` and `harnessByAdapter`, and one browser-safe descriptor per harness we have (`claude.js`, `opencode.js`, `hermes.js`, `agy.js`) holding `key`, `name`, `adapterType`, `createParser`, `command(...)`, `readLine`, `report(log)`, `reply(log)` and `usage(log)` as the plan says. ## Fixes from the V6 audit (2026-10-25)

`docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-V6.md` lists W1 to W12. W10 was fixed by R3 (the orchestrator expands `%VAR%` and honours `cli.harness`), and R4 added the room message of W4.

**Q6a. One run per project, briefs only here, git first, and no stray dev origin (OpenCode 6, merged as 0.1.300).** Fix W1, W5, W6 and W7 as the audit's Fix column says, and the bridge half of W9. W1: `run-lane` takes a lock per project for every `--project` run (queue, task, worktree or not), named from a hash of the resolved folder in the runs folder, held with the run's pid like the lane lock, and refuses a second run on the same project with one sentence; a worktree run locks its worktree folder, not the project, so lanes in their own worktrees still run side by side; the queue branch name carries the lane (`machineroom/queue-<lane>-<stamp>`), and update every test and doc that names the old pattern. W5: a brief run on a room whose root is another repository answers 400 `Briefs run in this repository. Use a task or a queue on another project.` W6 and W9: a queue or a `worktree: true` run on a folder that is not a git checkout answers 400 with the sentence `run-lane` would print, before anything starts or any file is written. W7: the Vite origin `http://localhost:5173` (and its 127.0.0.1 form) is allowed only when the bridge has no `staticDir`. Tests for each in the files that already test those routes and the runner. Do not touch `server/orchestrator.js`, `server/orchestrators.js`, `server/lanes-source.js` or `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` (O2 owns them). Files: `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `src/briefs/queue.js`, `src/briefs/run.js` (only if the lock or branch helpers live there), `server/runs.js`, `server/index.js` (`isAllowed` only), their tests, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-V6.md` (mark each fixed), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q6b. The orchestrator's chat file and its words (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.315).** Fix W2, W3, the rest of W4, W8, W9 and W12 from `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-V6.md` as its Fix column says, for the main orchestrator and every sub (O2 merged as 0.1.305, so the code is in `server/orchestrator.js` and `server/orchestrators.js`). W2: every message text passes `maskKeys` (`src/briefs/audit.js`) before it is stored or sent, and the count joins the health `masked` total. W3: the chat file is written to `<file>.tmp` and renamed over the file; a file that fails to parse is renamed to `<file>.bad` and kept; a second room on the same project is refused with a lock in the runs folder holding the bridge pid, as the lane lock does. W4: on a restore whose saved state is `working`, also write a failed record for the newest orchestrator log that has none. W12: the room-started turn times are saved beside the messages and the old ones dropped on restore. W8 and W9: `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` says that a single task leaves its work uncommitted in a detached worktree, so the orchestrator always sends `tasks` (a queue of one is fine), and that on a project with no `.git` it starts tasks without `worktree` and works in one tree. Mark each fixed in the audit. Tests in `tests/orchestrator.test.js` and `tests/orchestrators.test.js`. Do not touch the code that starts a turn or builds its command (O5 changes it at the same time). **I4. The dock shows real cost, task and run data (OpenCode 8, merged as 0.1.299).** `AgentDock` in the dock's middle column (`src/ui/Hud.jsx`) gets only the agents and the log, so Cost and Tokens read `not reported` and Task reads `none` even when the cost board and the callout know them. `src/Stage.jsx` already reads the costs (`costBoard`), the issues and the live runs (`agentTasks`). Pass the cost rows, the live runs and the issues from Stage to Hud and on to `AgentDock` as its `costs`, `liveRuns` and `tasks` props, in the shapes `src/ui/agentContext.js` reads (check its tests; change the helper only if a shape does not match, with tests). Clicking an agent shape in the room selects it in the dock already through `focused`; check that it does. Tests: a Hud render test, or a pure helper test, showing that the dock gets a lane's cost and task. Do not touch the legend or the lens (I3 owns them). Files: `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/ui/Hud.jsx`, `src/ui/agentContext.js` if needed, a test, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

Start a real bridge with the lane source on a throwaway git project in the system temp folder, send the owner's first message with `confirmed: true`, and check, with time limits: the turn starts, the lane run starts in `<project>-mr-<lane>`, its queue branch gets one commit, the run's end wakes the orchestrator with a `room` message naming the branch, and the second turn's reply reaches `GET /api/orchestrator`. Use the room's existing options for the runs folder, the lane file and the program paths; if one is missing, add the smallest option and say so. Skip the test with a clear reason where a needed program (git) is missing. Do not change `server/orchestrator.js` or `server/orchestrators.js` beyond such an option (O2 owns them). Files: `tests/orchestrator-e2e.test.js`, `tests/fixtures/fake-claude*`, `tests/fixtures/fake-lane*`, any option you add, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## A first boot a beginner can follow, and the last audit points (2026-10-25, the owner's request)

**G9. A tour on first boot (OpenCode 8, merged as 0.1.345).** AGY started this brief on 2026-10-25 and was stopped part way; its unfinished work is the patch `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs\g9-agy-start.patch`. Apply it first with `git apply --3way` on your new branch (its docs hunks may not apply; redo those by hand), read it, keep what is right, and finish the brief. Today a first visit opens the connect screen, and the five step guide card is plain text in the middle of the screen. Make the first boot teach the room. (1) The connect screen (`src/ui/Connect.jsx`) opens with two short sentences on what MachineRoom is and a `Take the tour` button first, above the bridge form and the replay and demo choices; it opens `?replay&fixture=scenario-i&tour` (the demo where two sub orchestrators and their lanes work) and saves no connection. A first boot is a visit with no saved connection and no `machineroom.guide` flag; then `Take the tour` is the focused button. (2) With `tour` the guide becomes a tour: about eight steps, each naming the element it explains by a selector the page already has (the lens, the hub and an agent, a working tunnel, the dock's agent list, the agent context, the settings and costs, the transport with its run markers, the CONTROL and ORCHESTRATOR buttons). The step draws a thin `#e5ff00` outline around that element and places the card beside it, inside the window; an element that is missing or hidden gets the card in the middle. The replay starts at the beginning at 1x and the tour steps wait for the moment they talk about (for example the tunnel step waits until an agent is working), with Next always available. (3) The last step offers `Connect to my project` (the connect screen, `?connect`) and `Keep watching`. Skip, Escape and Done set the guide flag as today; the Guide button in the help panel still opens the tour. (4) Keep the plain guide for a visit that is not a tour. Keep it calm under reduced motion (no animated outline) and never over a confirm card. Put the step list, the selector lookup and the card placement in pure helpers in `src/ui/guide.js`, tested in `tests/guide.test.js`. Read the `tour` parameter in `src/startup.js` and let the guide read it there, so `src/App.jsx` and `src/Stage.jsx` need no change; if the patch changed them, move that into the files below. The check by eye at 1280 by 800, 900 by 700, 768 by 1024 and 375 by 812 is left to the orchestrator. Write down each step that fails, needs a step the README does not name, or prints something the README does not say. Fix the README for each, in its voice (plain words, short sentences, no em dashes), and fix code only when a step fails on a plain bug, with a test. **O8. Each sub orchestrator gets its own token (AGY, merged as 0.1.356).** After Q13a a request with no Origin is an orchestrator, but the bridge cannot tell the main orchestrator from a sub, so a sub can start another sub's lane by leaving out `startedBy` or naming another sub (HIERARCHY.md says the sector gate is advisory until each sub has a token). Give each orchestrator a random token when its instance starts (`crypto.randomBytes(24)`, base64url), kept in memory only, never in the chat file, a log or an answer, and put it in that orchestrator's instructions with one line telling it to send the header `X-MachineRoom-Orchestrator: <token>` on every bridge request. The bridge maps the header to the orchestrator id: a no-Origin request with a known token acts as that orchestrator, so `startedBy` is taken from the token (a body `startedBy` that differs is refused with 403 and one sentence), a sub may start only its own lanes, and a ladder change or a message to a sub needs the main orchestrator's token. A no-Origin request with no token or an unknown one is refused for those three routes with 403 and one sentence; page requests are unchanged. A restarted bridge makes new tokens. `maskKeys` must never print a token: add the header value to what the bridge masks in frames. Tests in `tests/orchestrators.test.js` and the runs tests: main token, sub token, a sub naming another sub, no token, a wrong token, and a page request. Change `tests/orchestrator-e2e.test.js` and its fake `claude` only as far as they must send the header. Files: `server/orchestrators.js`, `server/orchestrator.js` (the instructions and the token only), `server/runs.js` (the `startedBy` check only), `server/index.js` if the header must be passed in, `src/briefs/audit.js` (the mask only), `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`, `docs/agents/HIERARCHY.md`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the ladder sentences), their tests, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**T23. If a test needs the script's internals, export the smallest helper. Change no behaviour. Files: `tests/history-audit.test.js`, `scripts/history-audit.mjs` (an export only), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## A sleek room with a compact info strip (2026-10-25, the owner's sketch)

The owner drew the target layout at 900 pixels and wider: a thin top band, the lens filling most of the window with its top and bottom edges curving inward (Claude made that change in the lens clip), and one compact info strip under the lens instead of the full dock. Phones and narrow windows (below 900 pixels) keep today's menu and stacked dock.

**U29. The compact info strip under the lens (OpenCode 8, merged as 0.1.362).** At 900 pixels and wider, replace the always-open dock with a strip about 72 pixels tall under the lens, in two halves split by a thin line. The left half shows a status dot and the shown agent's name (the selected agent, else the first working agent, else the orchestrator, as `agentContext.js` already picks it); clicking the name opens a small list of the agents to pick from. Under it, `Model: NAME` with a `V` that opens the model list for that agent's harness from `client.models()`; picking one calls `client.setOrchestratorModel?.({ id, model, confirmed: true })` for an orchestrator after a confirm card, and the list is read-only for a lane or when the method is missing. Beside them, for an orchestrator, `Context size N / WINDOW` with two buttons: Compact, which opens a small card with `Compact now` (an optional box `What to keep`, then `client.compactOrchestrator?.({ id, keep, confirmed: true })` after a confirm card) and the autocompact switch with its percent (`client.setAutocompact`, with `percent` from 50 to 95 when the bridge accepts it); and Settings, which opens the existing settings panel. For a lane the left half shows its tokens and cost this month instead of the context line. The right half shows that agent's last three log lines in Space Mono, newest at the bottom, prefixed with `>`. A small expand control at the strip's right end, or a click on the log, opens today's full dock (agent list, context, settings and costs) above the strip, and the same control or Escape closes it; it starts closed and remembers its state in `machineroom.settings`. The lens then fills from the top band to the strip (`--lens-h` follows the strip when the dock is closed). Below 900 pixels nothing changes. Keep the number keys, `L`, `T` and the tour steps working, and point the tour's dock steps at the strip when the dock is closed. Pure helpers for what the strip shows (the context line, the three log lines, the model choices) go in `src/ui/agentStrip.js` with tests in `tests/agent-strip.test.js`. Check what you can with tests; the look by eye is left to the orchestrator, so say in your report what to look at. The page rule stays: a request with an Origin is the owner, and these three are owner routes, so a request with no Origin gets 403. Add `compactOrchestrator` and `setOrchestratorModel` to the control client, its mock and `CLIENT_METHODS`, and pass `percent` through `setAutocompact`. ## A beginner's first hour, the strip that opens downward, and sessions (2026-10-25, the owner's second sketch)

The owner's report: a first start is an information overload; a beginner should choose how many agents work, and each tutorial step must outline the thing it talks about. The info strip is cluttered and must open downward, below the lens, with room for the settings and the orchestrator's chat input, never squeezed inside the window. A sessions drawer lists the agents (with how many can be added) on top and the orchestrator's sessions below, with pin, star, rename and delete. Five lanes work at once, so these names are fixed for all of them:

- Client methods (O10 and O11 add them; the UI calls each as `client.name?.(...)` and shows a short dim line when it is missing): `lanes()` answers `{ lanes: [{ key, name, harness, model, active, working }], max, chosen }`; `setActiveLanes({ lanes, confirmed: true })`; `setOrchestratorEffort({ id, effort, confirmed: true })`; `orchestratorSessions({ id })` answers `{ current: { title, startedAt, count }, sessions: [{ id, title, startedAt, endedAt, pinned, starred, count }] }`; `orchestratorSession({ id, session })` answers `{ id, title, messages }`; `newOrchestratorSession({ id, confirmed: true })`; `updateOrchestratorSession({ id, session, pinned, starred, title, confirmed: true })`; `openOrchestratorSession({ id, session, confirmed: true })`; `deleteOrchestratorSession({ id, session, confirmed: true })`. The orchestrator answers (`GET /api/orchestrator`, `/api/orchestrators`) add `effort` (null when unset).
- Class names: `.hud-strip-model`, `.hud-strip-effort`, `.hud-strip-compact`, `.hud-strip-settings`, `.hud-strip-expand`, `.hud-strip-panel` (the opened area under the strip), `.hud-strip-chat` (the chat input inside it), `.hud-sessions-button` (the top band's `V` conversation button), `.mr-sessions` (the drawer), `.mr-setup` (the first run card).
- New CSS goes in one block at the end of `src/styles.css` that starts with a comment naming the task, so merges do not collide.

**O10. Sessions and reasoning effort for each orchestrator (AGY, merged as 0.1.374).** Effort: `POST /api/orchestrator/effort` takes `{ id, effort, confirmed: true }`; for a `claude` harness the effort is one of low, medium, high, xhigh, max and a turn passes `--effort EFFORT`; for `opencode` it is one of low, medium, high, max and a turn passes `--model MODEL#EFFORT`; null clears it; any other value is 400 with one sentence; it is saved in the chat file and the answers add `effort`. Sessions: the chat file keeps archived sessions under `sessions`, each `{ id, title, startedAt, endedAt, pinned, starred, sessionId, opencodeSessionId, summary, messages }`, at most 50 (the oldest unpinned one goes first) with at most 200 messages each. `GET /api/orchestrator/sessions?id=ORCH` answers the shape above, pinned first, then newest. `GET /api/orchestrator/sessions/SID?id=ORCH` answers its messages. `POST /api/orchestrator/sessions/new` archives the current conversation when it has an owner message (title: the first owner message cut to 60 characters), then clears the messages, the harness session ids and the summary, so the next turn opens a new session. `POST /api/orchestrator/sessions/SID` changes `pinned`, `starred` or `title` (1 to 80 characters). `POST /api/orchestrator/sessions/SID/open` archives the current conversation the same way and makes that session current again with its messages and harness session ids, so the next turn resumes it. `POST /api/orchestrator/sessions/SID/delete` removes it. New and open answer 409 while a turn runs; an unknown session is 404. All of these, like O9's routes, need `confirmed: true` for a write and refuse a request with no Origin with 403. Add the client methods above to `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js` (the mock keeps two sample sessions) and `CLIENT_METHODS`. Tests in `tests/orchestrator.test.js`, `tests/orchestrators.test.js` and the contract test. Files: `server/orchestrator.js`, `server/orchestrators.js`, `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, their tests, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the orchestrator sentences), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**O11. The owner chooses which lanes are active (AGY 2, merged as 0.1.372).** `GET /api/lanes` answers `{ lanes, max, chosen }`: every lane in `docs/agents/lanes.json` that has a `cli` block, is not retired and is not the orchestrator, in the file's order, with `key`, `name`, `harness` and `model` from its `cli` block, `active` and `working` (a live run); `max` is their count; `chosen` is false until the owner has saved a choice for this project. The choice lives in `<runs>/active-lanes-<hash of the resolved project root>.json`; with no file every lane counts as active. `POST /api/lanes/active` takes `{ lanes, confirmed: true }`, needs a page Origin (403 without), refuses an unknown or repeated key or an empty list with 400 and one sentence, saves the list and sends an `agents` notice. After a choice, `/api/agents` lists only the active lanes (the orchestrator, sub orchestrators and session readers stay), and `POST /api/control/runs` refuses an inactive lane with 400 `The lane KEY is not active. Add it in Agents first.` Add one line to `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`: `curl -s {{BRIDGE}}/api/lanes` lists the lanes, and only the active ones may be started. Add `lanes` and `setActiveLanes` to the control client, its mock (all mock lanes active, `chosen: false`) and `CLIENT_METHODS`. Do not touch `server/orchestrator.js` or `server/orchestrators.js` (O10 changes them). Tests for the route, the file, the agents list and the run refusal. Files: `server/active-lanes.js` (new), `server/index.js` (mounting the route and filtering the agents only), `server/runs.js` (the refusal only), `src/control/client.js`, `src/control/mock.js`, `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`, their tests, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U31. The strip opens downward and carries model, effort, compact and settings (OpenCode, stopped and replaced by U34 and U35 after the owner's Antigravity sketch).** At 900 pixels and wider the collapsed strip holds only: on the left the status dot, the agent's name (its picker stays), `Model: NAME V` (`.hud-strip-model`) and, for an orchestrator, `Effort: LEVEL V` (`.hud-strip-effort`, the levels O10 lists for its harness, set through `client.setOrchestratorEffort` after a confirm card); in the middle Compact (`.hud-strip-compact`); on the right Settings (`.hud-strip-settings`) and the expand control (`.hud-strip-expand`). Move the log lines out of the collapsed strip. Expand, Settings or Compact opens `.hud-strip-panel` below the strip: the lens keeps its full height, the page grows and scrolls, and the panel scrolls into view; nothing opens inside or over the lens, and the old dock never squeezes the lens again. The panel holds, top to bottom: the open part (the Compact card or the settings: folder depth and screen effects, with room to read them), the shown agent's context and its last log lines, the cost board, and for an orchestrator its chat (messages and the input box, `.hud-strip-chat`), using `OrchestratorChat` in an inline mode instead of the floating panel while the strip panel is open. Close, the expand control again or Escape closes it and scrolls back to the top. Keep the live waiting card inside the lens, never over the strip. Below 900 pixels nothing changes. Do not touch `src/ui/guide.js`, `src/ui/Guide.jsx` or the top band (U32 and U33 change them). Pure helpers and tests in `src/ui/agentStrip.js` and `tests/agent-strip.test.js`. The sessions drawer (OpenCode 2, stopped and replaced by U34 and U35 after the owner's Antigravity sketch).** The top band's `V` conversation button (`.hud-sessions-button`) opens `.mr-sessions`, a drawer from the left under the top band, 320 pixels wide and the full height (full width below 900 pixels), closed by the button, a close control or Escape. Below it, Sessions: a row of orchestrator tabs (Main and each sub from `client.orchestrators()`), a New session button (`client.newOrchestratorSession` after a confirm card), then Pinned, Starred and Recent groups of `client.orchestratorSessions` rows, each with its title, date and message count and actions to pin or unpin, star or unstar, rename inline, and delete behind a confirm card. ## Autocompact the owner sets by number (2026-10-25, the owner's request)

The owner wants autocompact set by typing any number, as tokens or as a percent of the context window, never by a 50 to 95 slider, and a default set of compact rules when the owner writes none. Shared names: the orchestrator answers add `autocompactMode` (`percent` or `tokens`), `autocompactValue`, `compactRules` (the owner's text, empty when none) and `defaultCompactRules`, and keep `autocompactPercent` (the rule as a whole percent) for older pages; `client.setAutocompact` also takes `{ id, on, mode, value, rules, confirmed: true }`.

A turn that ends with the context at or past the rule (tokens, or the share of the window times 100) starts the compact turn, as now. Unset means percent 80. A compact turn never starts another compact turn: when the context is still past the rule after a compact, the next one waits for an owner or room turn. `POST /api/orchestrator/autocompact` takes `{ id, on, mode, value, rules, confirmed: true }` and still takes the old `percent`; refuse a mode, value or rules out of range with 400 and one sentence. ## Sound: a music player, agents that dance and spatial beeps (2026-10-25, the owner's request)

**U41. Music, dancing agents and spatial beeps (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.383).** The owner keeps music files in `C:\GitHub\MachineRoom\music` (in `.gitignore`, so nothing in it reaches git). Bridge: `GET /api/music` lists the audio files there (`.mp3`, `.ogg`, `.wav`, `.m4a`, `.flac`, `.opus`, by name, at most 500), with the folder resolved from the bridge's own install folder, not the project root, and overridden by `MACHINEROOM_MUSIC`; `GET /music/NAME` serves one file with its content type and HTTP Range support, refuses a name with a path separator or `..` with 400, and answers 404 for a missing file; both follow the bridge's page rules. Settings gains a Sound section saved in `machineroom.settings` (`sound: { on, volume, music, musicVolume, dance }`; sound starts on at a low volume, music starts off): a switch and a number volume for the beeps, and a small music player (play and pause, previous and next, the track name, shuffle and repeat, a volume) over the `/api/music` list, with the line `Put music files in the music folder of MachineRoom.` and that folder's path when the list is empty. Dancing: while music plays and dance is on, a Web Audio analyser reads the beat (bass energy over a short rolling average) and each agent shape pulses its scale by at most 8 percent and turns a little on each beat, the orchestrator less; nothing moves under reduced motion. Beeps: a short soft synthesized sound (no sample files) for each file touch, with its own tone for a read, a write, a file created (a write to a path the tree did not have) and a file deleted (a shell command that removes a path: `rm`, `del`, `Remove-Item`, `git rm`), panned left or right by where the touched card sits on screen in the lens and quieter the further the camera is from it (no sound for a card off screen or beyond twice the overview distance), at most 8 sounds a second in all so a burst does not stack up. Sound starts only after the first click or key on the page, as browsers require. Pure helpers (the pan and the gain from screen place and distance, the beat detector over an array of levels, the delete command test, the rate limit) with tests. ## An agent family tree (2026-10-25, the owner's request, Paperclip's company of agents)

The owner wants the agents as a family tree: the orchestrator at the root (any harness or a direct API key, any model, a role label the owner writes), children under it (code agents, each labelled by the owner), and a child that gets children of its own becomes a sub orchestrator that leads them. An orchestrator may propose new agents, and the owner approves or declines each one. The tree maps onto what exists: the main orchestrator (O15's harness and model), the ladder's sub orchestrators (O2), and the lanes, including owner-made ones (O13). Shared client methods (O16 adds them, U43 calls them as `client.name?.(...)`): `family()` answers `{ nodes: [{ id, kind, label, harness, model, effort, parent, children, active, working, custom }], keys: [{ name }], proposals: [{ id, from, parent, label, harness, model, effort, reason, createdAt }] }` (`kind` is `orchestrator`, `sub` or `lane`); `setAgentLabel({ id, label, confirmed: true })`; `keys()`; `setKey({ name, value, confirmed: true })`; `deleteKey({ name, confirmed: true })`; `approveProposal({ id, confirmed: true })`; `declineProposal({ id, confirmed: true })`. Lanes get 60 minutes.

**O16. The family tree on the bridge, key slots and agent proposals (AGY 2).** Write `server/family.js` and mount it. `GET /api/family` builds the nodes: the main orchestrator (root), each ladder sub (parent the main, children its lanes), and each active lane from `readAllLanes` (parent the sub whose lanes hold it, else the main), with labels from `<runs>/labels-<hash of the project root>.json` and defaults `Orchestrator`, `Sub orchestrator` and `Code agent`. `POST /api/family/label` takes `{ id, label, confirmed: true }` (1 to 40 characters). Key slots: `POST /api/keys` takes `{ name, value, confirmed: true }` (name 1 to 40 letters, digits, space, `-` or `_`; value 1 to 500 characters), saves to `<runs>/keys.json` written with mode 0o600, and never returns, logs or puts a value in an error; `GET /api/keys` answers only the names; `POST /api/keys/NAME/delete` removes one; export `readKey(runsFolder, name)`. Proposals: `POST /api/family/proposals` takes `{ parent, label, harness, model, effort, reason }` from an orchestrator (a no-Origin request with a valid `X-MachineRoom-Orchestrator` token, `from` taken from the token) or a page, refuses an unavailable harness or an unlisted model with 400, keeps at most 20 pending, and sends a `family` notice; `POST /api/family/proposals/ID/approve` creates the lane through the same code as `POST /api/lanes/create` (export a function from `server/agents-config.js` for it, changing nothing else there), adds it to the parent sub's lanes when the parent is a sub (through the ladder save, as the owner), and `.../decline` drops it; both send a `room` message to the proposing orchestrator (`The owner approved LABEL.` or `The owner declined LABEL.`). Label, key and approve or decline routes need a page Origin (403 without). Add a short paragraph to `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`: to add an agent, post a proposal with your token and a reason, tell the owner in your reply, and never act as if it was approved until the room says so. Add the methods to the control client, its mock and `CLIENT_METHODS`. Do not touch `server/orchestrator.js`. Tests for the tree, labels, each key rule (the value never in any answer), proposals from a token and a page, approve, decline and the room message. **V30. Specks travel along the yellow line, never straight to the file (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.388).** When an agent reads or touches a file, `updatePackets` in `src/engine/agents.js` sends white packets in a straight line from the agent to the file's card edge (`view.stripEnds`), while the yellow line the owner sees is the chain of lit links drawn by `src/engine/strings.js` (`linkOnPath`, `currentMask`). The specks must always ride that yellow chain. Find how the lit path is built (the mask from `room.js` into `strings.update`, the link list with `from` and `to` paths, and the agent's strip) and expose a pure helper that returns the ordered polyline of points an agent's speck must follow: from the agent's shape, along the strip to the hub or root as the yellow line is drawn today, then down each lit folder link to the card holding the file, ending at the file's edge point. `updatePackets` samples that polyline by arc length instead of the straight `a` to `b` span; a speck that has not yet reached the end is always on a drawn yellow segment. If the strip itself is a single straight line to the file, then the strip is the yellow line and the packets must stay exactly on it: say which case holds in your report and make the line and the speck path the same points. Packets must also stay on the line when the camera or cards move (recompute each frame from the current points). Reduced motion and `still()` behave as before. Tests: the polyline helper (order, ends, a file at root, a file two folders deep, a missing card falls back to the straight strip), the arc length sampling (t 0, 0.5, 1 land on the line), and that no sampled point is off the polyline by more than 1e-6. Files: `src/engine/agents.js`, `src/engine/strings.js` (only if the path needs exposing), `src/engine/room.js` (wiring only), their tests, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`. Do not touch `src/engine/void.js`.

**V31. Dust specks drift slowly instead of sitting still (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.388).** In `src/engine/void.js` the far dust (`dust`) only rotates a hair and the fine dust (`fineDust`, `speckBands`, `motePoints`) is static points on a slowly turning group, so specks look frozen. Give every speck its own slow drift: a small per-point velocity from a seeded generator (`seeded`), a gentle sinusoidal wander, and wrap or soft-bounce so the cloud keeps its overall shape and count; speeds a few units per second at most so it reads as floating dust, not motion. Update the position attribute on a throttle (for example every second frame, or in a small number of chunks per frame) so the cost stays low at the existing counts, never allocate per frame, and keep `moteCount`, `moteOpacity`, `dustPulse`, the fine dust settings and `dispose` working. `still()` (reduced motion) stops all drift and keeps the points where they are. Pure helpers (`driftPoint(seed, index, seconds)` or similar, the wrap rule) with tests: the same input gives the same position, positions stay inside the dust shell, zero seconds equals the start, and still mode does not move. Files: `src/engine/void.js`, its tests (add `tests/void.test.js` or the existing void test file), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`. Do not touch `src/engine/agents.js`.

**V32. Rolling session recorder that starts at the first conversation (OpenCode 5, merged as 0.1.388).** The replay today loads a sample fixture. Build the pure core of a real replay: `src/model/sessionRecorder.js` exporting `RECORD_MINUTES_DEFAULT = 10`, `RECORD_MINUTES_MIN = 1`, `RECORD_MINUTES_MAX = 240`, `clampRecordMinutes(value)` (non numbers give the default, clamped and floored), and `createSessionRecorder({ minutes = RECORD_MINUTES_DEFAULT } = {})` returning `{ push(frame), frames(), started(), clear(), setMinutes(minutes), minutes(), span() }`. A frame is the same raw frame object `parseFrames` and the live source already pass around (read `src/model/source.js` and `src/live/` for its shape and its time field). Nothing is kept until the first frame that is a conversation or agent activity arrives (decide from the frame shape which frames count, and say so); from that frame on every frame is kept, and frames older than `minutes` before the newest frame are dropped, so the log stretches back at most that window. `frames()` returns a copy in time order. `setMinutes` trims at once. Also export `recorderToReplay(recorder, { agentsFile, treeFile })` that returns the object `prepare({ frames, agentsFile, treeFile })` in `src/model/source.js` would build, with time starting at 0 at the oldest kept frame, and `null` when nothing has been recorded. Do not touch the UI and do not wire it in; another lane does that. Tests: nothing kept before the first activity, the window trim at 10 minutes and at 1 and 240, `setMinutes` shrinking and growing, order, copies not aliases, `clear`, the clamp, and `recorderToReplay` times starting at 0 and null when empty. Files: `src/model/sessionRecorder.js` (new), `tests/session-recorder.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

Instead build the replay from the session recorder (`src/model/sessionRecorder.js`, R1): feed it every frame the live source receives for the whole page session (hook it where `loadLive` or the live source in `src/live/` and `src/Stage.jsx` receive frames; keep one recorder per page, kept across mode switches), size it with `settings.recordMinutes` (V33) and call `setMinutes` when the setting changes. Switching to Replay plays `recorderToReplay(...)`; before the first conversation there is nothing, so show the existing empty state with the sentence `Nothing recorded yet. Replay starts with the first conversation.` Explicit `?recording=NAME`, `?fixture=NAME`, `?tour` and `?scale=N` keep working exactly as today, so tests and the tour still use fixtures. Update the tests that assumed the default fixture and the lines in `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` that describe the sample replay. **V35. Individual dust speck pulsing and multi-directional drift (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.388).** (1) In `src/engine/void.js`, remove the whole-group rotations (`dust.rotation.y += delta * 0.004`, `fineDust.rotation.y += delta * 0.002`, `fineDust.rotation.x += delta * 0.001`) inside `update(delta)` so dust clouds do not orbit in one shared direction. (2) Increase `DRIFT_SPEED = 14` and `DRIFT_WANDER = 18` (and pass `speed: 28` for the far `dustLayer`). In `driftSpecks(points, seed, options)`, give each speck independent 3-axis velocity (`vx, vy, vz` on a random unit sphere scaled by `(0.5 + rand() * 0.8) * speed`), independent 3-axis wander rates (`rx: 0.4 + rand() * 0.9`, `ry: 0.4 + rand() * 0.9`, `rz: 0.4 + rand() * 0.9`) and phases (`px, py, pz` in `[0, 2*PI)`), plus per-speck pulse parameters `pulsePhase = rand() * Math.PI * 2` and `pulseRate = 0.6 + rand() * 1.4`. In `driftPoint(speck, seconds, options)`, compute `wx = Math.sin(t * (speck.rx ?? speck.rate ?? 0.5) + (speck.px ?? speck.phase ?? 0)) * wander`, `wy = Math.sin(t * (speck.ry ?? (speck.rate ?? 0.5) * 0.8) + (speck.py ?? (speck.phase ?? 0) + 1.7)) * wander`, and `wz = Math.sin(t * (speck.rz ?? (speck.rate ?? 0.5) * 0.6) + (speck.pz ?? (speck.phase ?? 0) + 3.1)) * wander` so specks drift and wander in all directions independently. (3) Export `speckPulse(seconds, phase = 0, rate = 1.2)` returning `0.2 + 0.8 * (0.5 + 0.5 * Math.sin((Number(seconds) || 0) * rate + phase))` (keeping `dustPulse(seconds)` exported for compatibility). In `createVoid`, enable `vertexColors: true` on each material in `fineMaterials` and attach a `color` `THREE.Float32BufferAttribute(new Float32Array(points.length * 3).fill(1), 3)` to each fine dust geometry (`colorAttr` stored on the layer). In `applyDustOpacity()`, set `mat.opacity = base * fineDustOptions.opacity` without multiplying all specks by a single shared scalar; instead, in `driftLayer(layer, seconds)` when `layer.colorAttr` exists, compute `const b = (fineDustOptions.pulse && !still()) ? speckPulse(seconds, speck.pulsePhase, speck.pulseRate) : 1` for each drawn speck `i`, write `colorArr[i * 3] = b; colorArr[i * 3 + 1] = b; colorArr[i * 3 + 2] = b;`, and set `layer.colorAttr.needsUpdate = true` (when `!fineDustOptions.pulse || still()`, reset `colorArr.fill(1)` once). Add tests in `tests/void.test.js` verifying `speckPulse` varies across specks at the same timestamp, `driftSpecks` assigns distinct velocity directions (mean unit velocity magnitude `< 0.25` across 200 specks), and `dust.rotation.y` / `fineDust.rotation.y` remain `0` after `update(1)`. Files: `src/engine/void.js`, `tests/void.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V36. Base ring cap of 12 folders and 2D square-like shotgun spray for overflow and child folders (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.388).** (1) In `src/engine/ring.js`, export `RING_MAX_TOP = 12` and `SHOTGUN_ROW_STEP = 380` (keeping `RING_MIN_RADIUS = 1350`, `RING_ARC_GAP = 500`, `CHILD_STEP = 440`, `FAN_STEP = 0.22`). Export `shotgunOffset(index, count)` that arranges `count` folders in a 2D square-like shotgun grid branching outward from the center: for `count <= 1`, return `{ angleOffset: 0, radiusStep: CHILD_STEP, row: 0, col: 0, cols: 1, rows: 1 }`; for `count > 1`, let `cols = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(Math.sqrt(count)))`, `rows = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(count / cols))`, `row = Math.floor(index / cols)`, `col = index % cols`, `colsInRow = row === rows - 1 ? count - row * cols : cols`, `centered = col - (colsInRow - 1) / 2`, `spreadFactor = 1 + Math.min(count, 20) * 0.035`, `stepAngle = Math.min(0.38, FAN_STEP * spreadFactor)`, `angleOffset = colsInRow <= 1 ? 0 : centered * stepAngle`, and `radiusStep = CHILD_STEP + row * SHOTGUN_ROW_STEP`, returning `{ angleOffset, radiusStep, row, col, cols, rows }`. Keep `fanAngle(index, count)` exported for compatibility (`shotgunOffset(index, count).angleOffset`). (2) In `layoutRing(entries, options = {})`, place the first `ringCount = Math.min(RING_MAX_TOP, tops.length)` top-level folders evenly around the base ring (`radius = ringRadius(ringCount)`). When `tops.length > RING_MAX_TOP`, place the overflow top-level folders (`tops.slice(RING_MAX_TOP)`) in a shotgun spray outside the base ring using `shotgunOffset(i, overflow.length)` anchored at angle `0` and radius `radius + off.radiusStep`. (3) In `placeChildren`, place each parent folder's child folders (`kids`) using `shotgunOffset(index, kids.length)`: `angle = parentPos.angle + off.angleOffset`, `radius = parentPos.radius + off.radiusStep`, so e.g. 4 child folders form a `2x2` square-like shotgun spray (`2` columns by `2` rows) and larger folder arrays spread wider and deeper. Update `tests/ring.test.js`, `tests/e2e-room.test.js`, and the layout digests in `tests/scenario-e.test.js`. Files: `src/engine/ring.js`, `tests/ring.test.js`, `tests/e2e-room.test.js`, `tests/scenario-e.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

'folder' : 'folders'}` (for example `3 items  2 folders`), otherwise return `${items} items`. For non-`'files'` states keep the existing text (`empty`, `${total} items${sub}`). **H14. MCP server registry, JSON-RPC client, and direct API routes (OpenCode 6, merged as 0.1.388).** Create `server/mcp.js` and mount its routes in `server/index.js` so both the direct HTTP API and harnesses can manage and call Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers and tools. (1) In `server/mcp.js`, store MCP server configuration in `<runsDir>/mcp.json` (`{ servers: [{ name, transport: 'stdio' | 'http', command, args: string[], env: object, url, enabled: boolean }] }`). Export `readMcpServers(runsDir)`, `writeMcpServers(runsDir, servers)`, `upsertMcpServer(runsDir, entry)` (validating `name` as `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,40}`, `transport` as `'stdio'` with non-empty `command` or `'http'` with valid `http(s)://` `url`), and `removeMcpServer(runsDir, name)`. (2) Export `listMcpServerTools(serverConfig, { timeoutMs = 8000, spawnImpl, fetchImpl } = {})` and `callMcpServerTool(serverConfig, toolName, toolArgs = {}, { timeoutMs = 15000, spawnImpl, fetchImpl } = {})` implementing MCP JSON-RPC 2.0 (`initialize` with `protocolVersion: '2024-11-05'`, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list`, and `tools/call`) over both `'stdio'` (newline-delimited JSON-RPC over child process stdin/stdout) and `'http'` (`POST` JSON-RPC to `serverConfig.url`). Export `listAllMcpTools(runsDir, options)` (querying all enabled servers and returning `{ ok: true, tools: [{ server, name, description, inputSchema }], errors: [] }`) and `callMcpTool(runsDir, { server, tool, arguments: toolArgs }, options)`. (3) In `server/index.js`, add direct API routes: `GET /api/mcp/servers` (`{ ok: true, servers }`), `POST /api/mcp/servers` (body `{ name, transport, command, args, env, url, enabled }`, requires `confirmed: true`, saves and broadcasts `mcp` notice), `POST /api/mcp/servers/:name/delete` (requires `confirmed: true`, deletes and broadcasts `mcp` notice), `GET /api/mcp/tools` (returns `listAllMcpTools(runsDir)`), and `POST /api/mcp/call` (body `{ server, tool, arguments }`, calls `callMcpTool` and returns `{ ok, result, problem }`). Add comprehensive unit and HTTP route tests in `tests/mcp-server.test.js` using mocked `spawnImpl` / `fetchImpl`. Files: `server/mcp.js` (new), `server/index.js`, `tests/mcp-server.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/HARNESSES.md`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**H15. MCP tools integration for harnesses in mr-agent.mjs, acp-run.mjs, and activity parsers (OpenCode 7, merged as 0.1.388).** (1) Create `scripts/mcp-bridge.mjs` (self-contained or reading `<runsDir>/mcp.json` via `MACHINEROOM_RUNS_DIR` or default `%TEMP%/machineroom-runs/mcp.json`) so `scripts/mr-agent.mjs` and `scripts/acp-run.mjs` can list and call configured MCP servers without depending on the HTTP server being up. (3) In `scripts/acp-run.mjs`, load enabled MCP servers and pass them in `session/new` as `mcpServers` (`[{ name, command, args, env }]` for stdio or `[{ name, type: 'http', url }]` for http)instead of a hardcoded empty `mcpServers: []`. (4) In `src/model/parsers/claude.js` and `src/model/parsers/opencode.js`, recognize `mcp__*` and `mcp_call_tool` / `mcp_list_tools` tool invocations so any `path` or `file` argument is extracted as a `touch` / `read` file event and the tool call is counted in agent activity. Add unit tests in `tests/mr-agent-mcp.test.js` (new). Scrollable folder card file list capped to maxFiles rows instead of + N more (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.388).** (1) In `src/model/folderView.js`, add option `limitItems = true` to `planFolderView({ ..., limitItems = true } = {})`. In `cardRows(view)`, when `view.state !== 'files'` return `0`; let `total = view.items.length + (view.hiddenItems > 0 ? 1 : 0)`; when `Number.isFinite(view.maxFiles)` return `Math.min(total, clampFileLimit(view.maxFiles))`, otherwise return `total`. In `cardPlan(state, { pinned = [], alwaysDepth = 2, maxFiles = FILE_LIMIT_DEFAULT, previous = new Map(), sprayed = new Set(), rootName = 'root' } = {})`, call `planFolderView({ ...input, alwaysDepth, expanded: sprays, limitItems: false })`, set `const fileLimit = clampFileLimit(maxFiles)`, and store `{ ...view, maxFiles: fileLimit, back: view.path === '' ? 0 : view.back }` in `drawn`. (3) In `createCards` (`src/engine/cards.js`), track `let maxFilesLevel = clampFileLimit(options.maxFiles)` alongside `alwaysLevel`, pass `maxFiles: maxFilesLevel` to `cardPlan` inside `recompute`, and update `setOptions(options = {})` to accept both `alwaysDepth` and `maxFiles` (returning `true` and invalidating `planKey` and `layoutKey` when either changes). In `paintRows(card, view)`, set `const limit = clampFileLimit(view.maxFiles ?? maxFilesLevel)`, set `card.items.style.maxHeight = `${limit * ROW_H}px``, toggle class `is-scrollable` on `card.items` when `wanted.length > limit`, and render all `card.rows` without `.mr-card-more` when `hidden === 0`. In `makeCard(path)`, attach a `wheel` listener on `items` that calls `event.stopPropagation()` when `items.scrollHeight > items.clientHeight` so wheel-scrolling a card's file list does not zoom the 3D camera. In `slotPosition(dir, file, out)`, clamp `index` to `Math.min(index, Math.max(0, cardRows(card.view) - 1))` so tunnel lines to scrolled items stay on the card body. (4) In `src/styles.css`, style `.mr-card-items.is-scrollable` with `overflow-y: auto; overflow-x: hidden; overscroll-behavior: contain; pointer-events: auto; scrollbar-width: thin; scrollbar-color: rgb(255 255 255 / 0.35) transparent;`. Update `tests/cards.test.js` and `tests/scenario-e.test.js` (updating fixture digests in `OTHER_FIXTURES` and `scenario-e` for the full-item `cardPlan` views). Files: `src/model/folderView.js`, `src/engine/cards.js`, `src/styles.css`, `tests/folder-view.test.js`, `tests/cards.test.js`, `tests/scenario-e.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## The last fixes and the harness judgement (2026-10-26, the owner's request)

**V39. Full folder blocks stacked behind a folder card, stepped 270 degrees outward (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.389).** The owner looked again at the pile behind a folder card and asked for real blocks instead of the sketch's strips: "if a folder has extra folders beneath, there should be proper blocks behind it, not just mimic my drawing completely, and always point 270 degrees outward, like a pile of folders in a cabinet." Today `paintBacks` in `src/engine/cards.js` adds `view.back` divs of `.mr-card-back`, each 56 pixels tall and moved `translate(10px, -8px)` in `src/styles.css`, so they read as thin strips, and `occluderParts` builds matching parts of the same size for the WebGL occluder. Replace both with full folder blocks: each back is a rectangle the size of that card's own face (the width `makeCard` already computes with `cardBaseWidth`, and `cardHeight(cardRows(view))`, both in `src/engine/cards.js`; export `cardBaseWidth` if the paint needs it), with a folder tab along its top edge drawn by a `::before` on `.mr-card-back`, placed behind the face one step at a time along a fixed 270 degree direction on screen (straight left, never the ring's radial direction) and pushed a little further from the camera so the pile reads as folders in a cabinet, at most 3 backs (`view.back` is already capped at 3 by V37), no pointer events, and the front face and its brackets still paint over every back. Add and export a pure `backOffsets(count, step = BACK_STEP)` in `src/engine/cards.js` that returns `{ x, y }` for each back index, with back 0 at `{ x: 0, y: 0 }` and every later back one step further along the same 270 degree line; use it in `paintBacks` and in `occluderParts` so the occluder and the paint agree, keep the `.is-back-N` class names, and test it in `tests/cards.test.js` (counts 0, 1 and 3, every step on one line, and the existing `cardPlan` digests unchanged because the backs are paint only). Check by eye at 1280 by 800 on `/?replay` and in the wide overview: a folder that hides children shows a full folder block with its tab at its left, a folder with no hidden children shows none, and the pile covers neither the name row nor the file rows. Files: `src/engine/cards.js`, `src/styles.css` (the `.mr-card-back` rules in place, no new block at the end), `tests/cards.test.js`, `tests/occluder.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U67. A selected folder holds the camera, and empty space returns to the park ring (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.389).** The owner: "if a folder is selected, the camera anchors itself back towards it, and when pressing the environment space, it automatically moves the camera back to the park space ring, additionally when the mouse is hovering above the folder and scroll, the contents should be scrolled than the window itself." Today a click on a folder card only ends follow (`onCardClick` and `cardClickEndsFollow` in `src/engine/room.js`) and the rig has `focus`, `followSpheres` and `overview` but nothing that holds a card. (1) In `src/engine/camera.js`, add `hold(point, dist)` to `createCameraRig`: it sets the rig's existing follow slot to `() => ({ target: point(), dist })` and eases to it through `startTransition`, and it returns nothing; `overview()` already clears that slot and stays the park gesture. (2) In `src/engine/room.js`, add `anchor(path)` which takes the card for `path` or its nearest drawn ancestor, calls `rig.hold(() => card.object.position, ANCHOR_DIST)` with `ANCHOR_DIST` a new exported constant near `FOLLOW_EASE_MS` (about the distance at which a card fills a third of the lens), sets `followFocus` to nothing so an agent follow and a folder hold are never both on, and stops the next `refit` from moving the camera off the card until the hold ends; add `park()` which drops the hold and calls `rig.overview()`; and make a click on a card body call `anchor(path)` where `onCardClick` runs today (the head still sprays, the click on an item still ends follow). (3) Make picking an agent frame what it reads: in `focusAgent`, when the agent has a touch (`view.touch`, the file or folder it last touched) and the style is `wide`, frame the midpoint of the agent and that touch instead of the chip, so a click on a chip or on U68's console goes to the space the agent is reading. (4) In `src/Stage.jsx`, call `room.park()` on a double click on empty environment space (a double click on the gl layer that hits neither a card nor an agent, so a single click there still only ends follow as it does today) and on the `Escape` key when no panel is open, and keep the `0` key calling the same park. (5) The wheel over a folder card scrolls its rows and never the window while they overflow, as V38 does for `.mr-card-items`, including when the pointer sits on the card face beside the rows. Check by eye at 1280 by 800: click a folder, orbit a little, and the camera stays on that folder; double click the empty room and it returns to the park ring view; hover a folder with more rows than fit, scroll, and the rows move while the page does not. Files: `src/engine/camera.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, `src/Stage.jsx`, `tests/camera.test.js`, `tests/e2e-room.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U68. A cyan console above an agent that runs a command (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.390).** The owner: "when an agent calls a terminal to run tests and outputs ports, the agent creates a cyan blue terminal above its head where it reads the contents like an agent opening a browser or checking an mcp server. Clicking on the terminal where a browser address is open routes them to the space where the agent is looking and reading." Today `src/engine/terminals.js` draws one chip per agent (`.mr-term`, a title and body lines) beside its shape and nothing above its head. Add a second element per agent, `.mr-term-console`, as its own CSS3D object placed above the head (about `CHIP_W * SCALE` up `+Y` from the agent's anchor, and clear of the chip), shown while that agent's latest activity is a `tool.start` or `tool.end` whose `tool` is `shell` or `other` (the activity types are in `src/model/state.js` and the schema in `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`), and for 6 seconds of wall time after it ends, reading the same `entry.lines` the chip reads, capped at 5 rows, in cyan (add one `--cyan` custom property on `:root` with `#38e8ff`, since no cyan exists today) with a 1 pixel border, a `rgb(0 0 0 / 0.72)` fill and a small `CONSOLE` label, fading with the agent through `setFade` and under reduced motion the same way the chip behaves. A click on the console calls the same `onPick(key)` the chip calls, so the room frames that agent and what it reads (U67), and set the console's `title` to the address when `addressIn` finds one, with `is-address` on the element while it does. Check by eye at 1280 by 800 in the demo room and on a live lane run: a shell call shows a cyan console over the agent's head with the port line, it goes 6 seconds after the call, and clicking it selects the agent. Files: `src/engine/terminals.js`, `src/engine/room.js` (the `note` and `clearNotes` calls only), `src/styles.css` (one block at the end that starts with a comment naming U68), `tests/terminals.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U69. Chromatic aberration stays centred on the park ring (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.390).** The owner: "make chromatic aberration always center at the park hub ring's center, not wherever the camera is looking at." Today `src/Stage.jsx` mounts the `#mr-screen-filter` filter on `.surface-layers` and its `<feImage href={chromaMap} preserveAspectRatio="none" />` stretches the map from `radialMapDataUrl()` across the whole filter region, so the neutral centre of the map is the centre of the lens and never moves. (3) In `src/engine/room.js`, add `parkScreen()` that projects `HUB_CENTER` from `src/engine/hub.js` through the room's camera at the surface's current size, and answer null when it cannot. (4) In `src/Stage.jsx`, call `room.parkScreen()` inside the `beforeFrame` callback the room already runs and store the point in a ref, writing React state only when either coordinate moved by 2 pixels or more since the last write, and set the `feImage` `x`, `y`, `width` and `height` from `mapPlacement` with the lens's measured size. Check by eye at 1280 by 800 with aberration at full strength: the fringes vanish at the park ring's centre and grow toward the lens edges, they stay centred while the camera orbits and zooms, and following an agent moves the centre with the ring rather than with the camera.
