**U70. The aberration map keeps its strength when the camera leaves the ring (OpenCode 4, merged as 0.1.391).** U69 merged at 0.1.390 puts the radial map's centre on the park ring, but `mapPlacement` sizes the square to reach the farthest corner of the lens from that centre. At the park view that is about 1,330 pixels and reads well; with the camera anchored on a folder card, or following an agent, the ring's projected centre leaves the lens and the square grows to tens of thousands of pixels (measured in the browser at 0.1.390 on `/?recording=opencode-V39-20261026-234246`: centre 3,383, 42,329 and side 84,928 for a 1,247 by 460 lens), so the part of the map over the lens sits near its neutral middle and the fringes disappear exactly when the owner is looking at a card. (1) In `src/ui/aberration.js`, change `mapPlacement({ width, height, centerX, centerY, radius = 0, limit = 2 })` so the square's side is twice the ring's on-screen radius scaled by `limit` (a new constant `ABERRATION_REACH = 2.5`, meaning the fringes reach full strength at 2.5 ring radii from the centre) and never more than `limit` times the lens's diagonal, and so the centre is the ring's point when the whole square still covers the lens and is pulled back toward the lens, no further than it must be, when the bounded square would leave a corner of the lens uncovered. Answer `{ x, y, width, height }` as today. Test it in `tests/aberration.test.js`: a ring at the lens centre with a 300 pixel radius covers the lens and its centre stays put; a ring far to the right of the lens keeps the square over the lens with its size at the limit; a ring below the lens does the same downwards; a zero radius falls back to today's lens-covering square. (2) In `src/engine/room.js`, have `parkScreen()` answer `{ x, y, radius }`, where `radius` is the projected distance from the ring's centre to a point `HUB_RADIUS` out from `HUB_CENTER` on the ring's plane (project both with `projectPoint` from `src/engine/camera.js` and answer null when either is behind the camera), and keep the answer to `{ x, y }` consumers working by returning radius as an extra field. (3) In `src/Stage.jsx`, pass `radius: parkCentre?.radius ?? 0` into `mapPlacement` and keep the 2 pixel write threshold. Check by eye at 1280 by 800 with aberration at full strength on `/?recording=opencode-V39-20261026-234246`: the fringes are strong at the lens edges and neutral at the park ring's centre, clicking a folder card keeps visible fringes at the lens edges instead of clearing them, and orbiting at the park view keeps the neutral point on the ring. Give the console check that reads the `feImage` `x`, `y` and `width` at the park view and again with a card anchored, and say what each number is. Files: `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/ui/aberration.js`, `src/engine/room.js`, `tests/aberration.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.


**U71. The bridge asks who is calling before it merges, spends or starts a program (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.395).** Audit A8 (merged as 0.1.391, `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-HARNESS.md`) found one High and three Medium gaps of the same kind: routes that write, spend or start a program trust a caller that sends no `Origin`, so a lane agent (or a web page through an `<img>` GET) can call them with no owner in the loop. Fix A1, A2 and A3 in that audit. (1) A1, the merge route: `POST /api/control/reviews/LANE/merge` in `server/reviews.js` trusts `confirmed: true` from the body and never checks who sent it, so a lane can merge its own head into `main` in the owner's checkout. Require the page's `Origin` for this route and refuse a request with no `Origin` and no `X-MachineRoom-Orchestrator` token, using the same actor test the orchestrator routes already use (`server/orchestrators.js`), and answer 403 with a sentence that says the caller is not the owner. Keep the existing head, clean-checkout and conflict checks. (2) A2, the MCP routes in `server/mcp.js` mounted in `server/index.js`: `GET /api/mcp/tools` starts every enabled stdio server and `POST /api/mcp/call` runs a tool, both with no `confirmed` and no `Origin` check. Require the page's `Origin` on every MCP route, and add `confirmed: true` to the call route, refusing without it with a sentence that names the owner's confirm. Keep the in-process path `scripts/mcp-bridge.mjs` uses working, since it never goes over HTTP. (3) A3, the routes that spend or start a program: `POST /api/assistant` (runs `opencode run`), `POST /api/decide` (spends `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) and `GET /api/harnesses/KEY/models?refresh=1` (runs a harness command) have no `Origin` and no `confirmed` check. Require the page's `Origin` on all three, and `confirmed: true` on the assistant and decide routes, with a sentence naming the owner's confirm. (4) Every route you gate gets a test that sends no `Origin` and no token and expects 403, and a test that sends the page's own `Origin` and still succeeds: extend `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js`, `tests/mcp-server.test.js`, `tests/assistant*.test.js`, `tests/decide*.test.js` and `tests/harness-models*.test.js` where they exist, and say which test proves each route. Keep every existing passing behaviour: a page call with its own `Origin` on the serving port must still work, and the orchestrator's own token path must still work. Check by hand with the built app on one port (`npm run build`, then `npm run serve -- up --lanes`) that the page's merge, MCP and assistant calls still work from the room, and with `curl` that the same routes answer 403 with no `Origin`; paste both commands and their status codes. Files: `server/reviews.js`, `server/mcp.js`, `server/index.js`, `server/assistant.js`, `server/decide.js`, `server/harness-models.js`, `tests/bridge-reviews.test.js`, `tests/mcp-server.test.js`, `tests/assistant-routes.test.js`, `tests/decide-eval.test.js`, `tests/harness-models.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U72. A lane gets only the environment it needs, and its log is masked (OpenCode 2, merged as 0.1.392).** Fix A5 from `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-HARNESS.md` (merged as 0.1.391). Today `childEnvironment` in `src/briefs/run.js` deletes only `PWD`, `OLDPWD` and `INIT_CWD`, and `scripts/run-lane.mjs` starts the lane with the whole parent environment, so `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` and any other secret reach every lane, and the lane's log under `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs` is the raw output, not masked, so an agent that runs `env` writes the keys to disk. Keep a lane able to start OpenCode and a test that proves `run-lane --dry-run` still starts on this machine. (2) Mask the lane log: write the run's log through the same split-key masker the bridge uses for its frames (`createSplitMasker` in `src/briefs/audit.js`), so a key that appears in a lane's output, split across chunks or not, is written as the mask the bridge uses, and add a test that a fake key passed in the environment does not appear in the log file and the mask does. (4) Say in `README.md`, in the section about keys, that a lane sees only the names in the allow-list, and name the harness key exception. **U73. The camera hold lets go when its card goes, and the review worktree leaves the OS temp folder (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.392).** Fix the five findings of audit A7 (merged as 0.1.391, `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-V8.md`), plus one fault the orchestrator hit on every review. (1) W1, Medium: a click on a card body holds the camera at `ANCHOR_DIST` and nothing releases the hold when the card leaves the plan or the replay seeks away, so an arrow key, a scrub or a Space at the end of a replay leaves the camera 700 units from a card that is gone and the room stops refitting until the owner parks. In `src/engine/room.js` `sync`, release the hold and refit when the held card is no longer drawn or is `leaving`, and release it on a project or tree change and on a replay seek or restart (`src/model/player.js` the room already watches); a `park()` from that state must still work. Add a test to `tests/e2e-room.test.js` that holds a card, then syncs a state with that card gone, and expects the hold released and the fit restored. (2) W2, Low: while a hold is on, `refit` returns before it computes anything, so `fitted` keeps an old target and distance and the fit after a resize or a `Content files` change is stale. Record the fresh fit while held (compute it and store `fitted`) without moving the camera, so the park after the hold is right, and test it. (3) W3, Low: while a card is held, the wheel over the lens and the Zoom rail do nothing because `rig.update` overwrites `goal` from the follow slot. Let the owner zoom while held: keep the held point, take the new distance from `zoomBy` and `setZoomShare`, and test that a wheel while held changes the camera's distance from the card while its target stays the card. (4) W4, Low: `focusAgent` clears the focus marker and returns early when the room has no view for the key, so the rig keeps following the last agent. Make that path stop the follow, and test it. (5) W5, Low: `occluderParts` uses `CARD_W` while the drawn card is `cardBaseWidth(path) + 2` wide, so the occluder misses the last 2 pixels of every card on one side. Size the parts from `cardBaseWidth` and the face border, and update `tests/occluder.test.js`. (6) The review harness fault: `scripts/review-lane.mjs` puts its merged-result worktree under the OS temp folder, so `tests/run-project.test.js`'s temp-folder case fails there on every branch and every review reports `merged result` as a failure while the build and the other 3,080 tests pass. Put the review worktree in a folder beside the checkout (for example `<parent of the checkout>\MachineRoom-review-<random>`) instead of the OS temp folder, and add a test in `tests/review-lane.test.js` (new, or the file that already covers the script) that the path it picks is not inside `os.tmpdir()`. Check by hand by running `npm run review-lane -- opencode3` on a clean lane and pasting the summary line; it must end `10 checks` with no `merged result` failure. Files: `src/engine/room.js`, `src/engine/camera.js`, `src/engine/cards.js`, `scripts/review-lane.mjs`, `tests/camera.test.js`, `tests/e2e-room.test.js`, `tests/occluder.test.js`, `tests/review-lane.test.js` (new), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.


**U74. A lane starts from the default branch, not from whatever branch its worktree was left on (OpenCode, merged as 0.1.393).** The paused U72 run opened by noticing "The branch is still on u67": `startLane` in `scripts/run-lane.mjs` only checks that the lane worktree is clean (line 674) and then starts the lane in it, so a worktree left on another lane's branch builds that brief on top of work that may never merge, and `runs.jsonl` records no starting branch. Fix it. (1) Before a run starts, read the worktree's branch (`git -C <worktree> branch --show-current`) and put it back on the project's default branch (`git -C <worktree> checkout --detach refs/heads/main`, letting the existing clean check guard the way; keep the worktree detached, which is how this repo already leaves a lane after a merge). Refuse with a sentence naming the branch and the commits when the worktree sits on a branch that has commits `main` does not have (`git rev-list --count refs/heads/main..HEAD` above zero), because that is another lane's unfinished work and resetting it would lose it. (2) Record the branch the run started from in the record the runner writes (`record.branch`, next to `lane`, `briefs` and `minutes`) and print it in the run summary line, so a merge review can see what the run was built on. (3) Add tests to `tests/run-project.test.js` (or the file that covers the runner today): a worktree on a stale branch with no commits ahead is moved to `main` before the child starts, a worktree on a branch one commit ahead is refused with that sentence, and the record carries the starting branch. (4) Say it in `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR.md` under the lane rules, in one sentence, replacing any sentence that implies a lane folder is reset by hand. Check by hand: leave a scratch worktree on a branch with no commits, run `npm run run-lane -- opencode3 --dry-run U73`, and paste what the summary says about the starting branch; then put it on a branch with one commit and paste the refusal. Files: `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `tests/run-project.test.js`, `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U75. A stopped lane stays stopped (OpenCode, after U74, merged as 0.1.397).** When the owner paused the lanes at 00:52 on 2026-10-27, the U71, U72 and U73 runs wrote their run records with exit code 4294967295, yet about 13 minutes later, at 01:03 to 01:05, something still running in those worktrees left 15 uncommitted files in the U71 worktree and committed U72 and U73 on branches that had been deleted (`f9c04e9`, `2f29ad2`). The harness had left a process outside the tree `killTree` reached (OpenCode starts a background server), so the work went on after the run had ended and its record said it was over. Make a stop real: (1) `run-lane` writes the harness child's pid into the lane lock beside its own (one JSON line, read by the old plain pid format too), and on SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP, the time limit and its own exit it calls `killTree` on that child. (2) After the child closes, `run-lane` lists every process whose parent chain reaches the child's pid or whose command line names the lane's worktree folder (Windows: one `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` call through `powershell -NoProfile -Command` with an argument list, no shell string; elsewhere `ps -eo pid,ppid,args`), kills each with `killTree`, and prints one line per process it stopped. (3) `npm run run-lane -- --stop LANE` reads the lock, kills both pids' trees and the leftovers of (2), removes the lock, and prints what it stopped; it is the documented way to pause a lane, and `ORCHESTRATOR.md` says so. (4) A new run refuses to start when the lock's child pid, or a leftover from (2), is still alive, with one sentence naming the pid and `--stop`. (5) The run record gains `stoppedLeftovers` (the count) when (2) found any. Pure helpers for the lock line, the process list parse and the leftover filter, with tests; the stop itself tested with a fake harness (a Node script that starts a detached grandchild which writes a file after 2 seconds) that `--stop` and the time limit both prevent from writing. Files: `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `scripts/lane-lock.mjs`, `server/processTree.js` (a leftover lister beside `killTree`, only if it fits there), `src/briefs/run.js` (the record field), their tests, `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR.md` (the stop line), `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**U76. The runs folder, the candidate port and the key promise say what is true (OpenCode 3, merged as 0.1.394).** Fix A8, A10 and A11 from `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-HARNESS.md`. (1) A11: `scripts/run-lane.mjs` reads `MACHINEROOM_RUNS` to pick the runs folder, but `server/lanes-source.js` and `server/doctor.js` always use `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs`, so a terminal run with the variable set writes where the room never looks and the doctor checks the wrong folder. Make the lane source and the doctor read `MACHINEROOM_RUNS` the same way the runner does (one shared helper if the three `runsFolder` functions can share it without a new dependency between `server/` and `scripts/` that the pack check refuses), and test that a set variable moves all three. (2) A10: `scripts/candidate.mjs` checks the port with `portIsFree` before a build that can take five minutes; check it again just before `startCandidate` and stop with the same `Port N is in use. Pass --port.` sentence, with a test. (3) A8: `README.md` still says `MachineRoom prints and stores no key.` Say instead that it prints no key and puts none in the repository or `lanes.json`, that the Settings key slots are saved in `<runs>/keys.json` outside the repository (mode 0o600), and keep U72's sentence about the masked lane log. Files: `server/lanes-source.js`, `server/doctor.js`, `scripts/run-lane.mjs` (the runs folder helper only), `scripts/candidate.mjs`, `README.md` (the keys section only), their tests, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

(1) A4: a brief run starts in `lane.worktree` from `lanes.json` after checking only that it is clean, so a lane entry pointing at the main checkout or another lane's worktree is accepted (the audit's dry run printed `cwd: C:\GitHub\MachineRoom`). Before a brief run, refuse a worktree that is the main checkout, another lane's worktree from `lanes.json`, the home folder, the temp folder or the runs folder, using `projectProblems` or its checks in `src/briefs/run.js`, and refuse one that is not under `.worktrees/` of this repository, with one sentence each; `--dry-run` refuses the same way. (2) A7: parse the brief's `Files:` list in `briefProblems` and refuse an entry that is absolute, holds `..`, or matches the lane's `forbidden` globs, naming the entry. **U78. A queue's branch is written where people look for it (OpenCode, after U75, merged as 0.1.398).** Fix A6, A9 and A12 from `docs/agents/AUDIT-2026-10-HARNESS.md`. (1) A6: in the terminal path, `run-lane --project X --task T --worktree` runs as a queue of one, as Dispatch already does, so the work is committed on a branch after the tests pass instead of left uncommitted in a detached worktree that blocks the next run; fix the line in `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md` that says the next run loses the work. (2) A9: a queue run writes its branch to `branch` as well as `queue` in `runs.jsonl`, so `--list` and `npm run outcome -- BRANCH` find it. (3) A12: with `--worktree`, `formatQueueSummary` says `The worktree PATH stays on BRANCH.` instead of `The project stays on BRANCH. Run git switch - to go back.`, which stays for the project case. Update the tests that expect `branch: ''` or a detached HEAD for a single task, and the README lines on the terminal path and the `branch` field. Files: `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `src/briefs/queue.js`, `src/briefs/run.js`, `tests/run-project.test.js`, `tests/run-worktree.test.js`, `tests/queue.test.js` (or the file that covers the summary), `README.md`, `docs/agents/ORCHESTRATOR-AGENT.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**Q13. CI green again after 0.1.399 (OpenCode, merged 0.1.400).** CI run 40727058701 on `37b81a0` fails five tests that pass on the owner's machine. (1) `tests/run-lane.test.js` "refuses a merged brief before it prints the dry run" and "refuses a retired lane before anything starts, and runs it with --force" run the real runner with the real `docs/agents/lanes.json`, whose worktrees name `C:\GitHub\MachineRoom\.worktrees\*`, so on a checkout anywhere else U77's `briefWorktreeProblems` refuses them with `The lane worktree ... is not under .worktrees of this repository.` Keep the runner's check as it is; make those tests use a temp `MACHINEROOM_LANES_FILE` whose lanes point under the checkout's own `.worktrees` (or otherwise stop depending on where the checkout lives). (2) `tests/run-worktree.test.js` "commits the task on a queue branch and writes that branch to the record" exits 1 on Windows and Ubuntu. Find out why before you change anything: the likely cause is that the queue's commit needs a git identity the CI runner lacks (reproduce with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` pointing at an empty file and no `user.name` in the project), so set the identity in the test project's own git config the way the other queue tests do; change the runner only if it is wrong. (3) `tests/run-lane-stop.test.js` on Ubuntu: the time-limit test finds the grandchild's late file, and the `--stop` test gets `code: null`. On Linux the detached grandchild leaves the harness's process group, and once the harness dies its parent becomes 1, so `leftoverProcesses` no longer reaches it by the parent chain, and a project run without its own worktree never matches by name, by design. Fix the runner: before `killTree` on the time limit, on a signal and in `--stop`, list the harness's descendants while the chain is still intact and stop them too after the harness; keep the rule that a project run without its own worktree never matches by command line. In the test, accept `code: null` for a run killed by a signal. Run the five tests, `npx vitest related` on what you change, and `npm test`, and say in the report which cause you found for (2). Files: `scripts/run-lane.mjs`, `server/processTree.js` (only if the snapshot belongs there), `tests/run-lane.test.js`, `tests/run-worktree.test.js`, `tests/run-lane-stop.test.js`, `tests/process-tree.test.js`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

## Orchestrators that talk, and the family tree in the room (2026-10-29, the owner's request)

The owner sets the family tree: for example three orchestrators with one coder each (the main orchestrator and two subs, or the main and three subs). Today the main orchestrator messages a sub through `POST /api/orchestrator/messages`, a sub answers as `sub:<id>` in the main chat, and a lane run's end wakes whoever started it with a `room` message. A lane never talks; subs never talk to each other; the ladder is one level deep (every sub's parent is the main). None of that talking shows in the room, every lane's line runs to the main orchestrator even when a sub leads it, and the room shows lane keys instead of the owner's labels. These ten briefs make the tree and its talk visible. They run at the same time, so keep to the files each one names.

The message frame and activity shared by O17, V41 and U79: the bridge sends a frame of type `orchestrator.message` with `payload: { from, to, kind, ts }`, where `from` and `to` are agent keys as `/api/agents` names them (`orchestrator`, `sub-<id>`, a lane key) and `kind` is `order` (main to sub), `report` (sub to main), `dispatch` (an orchestrator starts a lane run) or `wake` (a lane run's end wakes its starter). The pipeline turns it into the activity `agent.message` with `agent` set to `from` and the extra fields `to` and `kind`. No message text travels in the frame.

**O17. The bridge sends a frame when one agent talks to another (OpenCode, merged 0.1.401).** Add an `onMessage({ from, to, kind })` option to `createOrchestrators` and call it at each of the four points: a message from the main to a sub (`order`), a sub's reply delivered to the main (`report`), a lane run started with a `startedBy` (`dispatch`, from the starter to the lane; find where `server/runs.js` or the control route knows both), and a run end that wakes an orchestrator (`wake`, from the lane to the woken orchestrator). `server/index.js` builds the frame described above and passes it through `accept()` like any other frame, so it is masked, kept in the history and sent on `/events`. In `src/model/pipeline.js` turn the frame into the `agent.message` activity; in `src/model/log.js` give it one dim log line such as `orchestrator > sub-front: order`; `state.js` ignores it. Add the activity to the schema table in ARCHITECTURE.md. Tests: each of the four points produces exactly one frame (in `tests/orchestrators.test.js` and the runs tests), and the pipeline and log tests read one frame. Files: `server/orchestrators.js`, `server/orchestrator.js` (the wake call only), `server/runs.js` (the dispatch call only), `server/index.js`, `src/model/pipeline.js`, `src/model/log.js`, their tests, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the activity table and one sentence in the ladder paragraph), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V40. A lane's line runs to the orchestrator that leads it (OpenCode 2, merged 0.1.401).** In `src/engine/agents.js` each lane draws a faint grey line to the main orchestrator. When the ladder puts the lane in a sub (`ownerOf` in `src/model/hierarchy.js`), draw that line to the sub's view instead, with the same grey and opacity; a lane in no sub keeps its line to the main, and the sub's own white line to the main stays. Write the choice as a pure exported function `lineParentKey(key, ladder)` that answers `orchestrator` or `sub-<id>`, and fall back to the main when the sub's view does not exist yet. `setLadder` must re-aim the lines without rebuilding views. Tests for `lineParentKey`: no ladder, a lane in a sub, a lane in no sub, an unknown key. Files: `src/engine/agents.js` (the orchestrator line part and `setLadder` only), `tests/agents*.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the line sentences in "What it shows" and "Agents"), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V41. Packets fly between agents when they talk (OpenCode 3, merged 0.1.401).** Write `src/engine/messages.js`: on an `agent.message` activity (shape above; O17 sends it, so test with a hand-made activity), send six packets in a straight line from the `agent` view's position to the `to` view's position, 3 pixel points like the strip packets, each taking 0.6 seconds, 60 ms apart, from a fixed pool of 48 points shared by the room, white for `order`, `report` and `wake`, yellow for `dispatch`. When either view is missing, do nothing. Under reduced motion send none. Export a pure `messageSpots(from, to, startedAt, now, out)` that gives the packet positions and is tested (start, middle, end, nothing after the last packet). In `src/engine/room.js` create it with the scene and call it from the activity handler; dispose it with the room. Files: `src/engine/messages.js`, `src/engine/room.js` (the create, the activity call and the dispose only), `tests/messages.test.js`, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (one bullet in "Room behaviour" and the layout list), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V42. The room shows the owner's labels (OpenCode 4, merged 0.1.401).** The owner names agents in the family tree (`client.family()`, each node has `id` and `label`), but chips, callouts and the HUD agent list show the key. In `src/Stage.jsx` read `client.family?.()` at start and on each `family` notice (the same way the ladder is read), build a map from key to label (a sub node's id `X` is the agent `sub-X`; the main is `orchestrator`), and pass it to a new `room.setLabels(map)`. The terminal chip title, the callout title and the agent list row show the label when one is set and the key otherwise; a child chip keeps `PARENT > NAME` with the parent's label. Keep a pure `displayName(key, labels)` in `src/ui/agentList.js`, tested. A replay and a client without `family` keep the keys. Files: `src/Stage.jsx`, `src/engine/room.js` (`setLabels` only), `src/engine/terminals.js` (the title only), `src/engine/agents.js` (the callout title only), `src/ui/agentList.js`, `src/ui/Hud.jsx` (the row name only), their tests, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**V43. A sector reads as a team (OpenCode 5, merged 0.1.401).** With one coder per sub the lane sits somewhere on a wide arc and the team does not read as one. In `src/engine/hub.js`: when a sector holds one lane, park it at the middle of the arc, directly under its sub; with two or more, spread them evenly inside the arc with the same gap at each end as today. The sector's name outside the arc uses the sub's `name` from the ladder, in capitals, as now. While any agent in a sector works (the sub or one of its lanes), its arc brightens to full white and returns over 1 second when none works (at once under reduced motion); the rest of the ring stays as it is. Keep the placement in a pure exported function and test it: one lane, three lanes, an empty sector. ### Clean projects (2026-10-29, the owner's request)

The owner opened a project that showed every lane (ten OpenCode and three AGY) switched on, and the Projects tab listed only test folders: all 20 entries of `<runs>/projects.json` were test temp folders and lane worktrees, written by test bridges that started without a runs folder and so used the owner's real `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs`. The orchestrator cleared that file by hand on 2026-10-29 (a backup is beside it). These three briefs keep it from happening again.

**T25. Tests never write the owner's runs folder (AGY, merged 0.1.401).** `runsFolder()` in `server/lanes-source.js` falls back to `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs` when `MACHINEROOM_RUNS` is unset, and many tests start a bridge or the runner without a runs folder (for example `startBridge` in `tests/project-dispatch.test.js`), so they write `projects.json`, chat files and locks into the owner's real folder. Add a Vitest setup file (`tests/helpers/isolate.js`, listed in `setupFiles` in `vite.config.js`) that sets `process.env.MACHINEROOM_RUNS` to a folder under the per-process test temp root from `tests/helpers/tempRoot.js` when it is unset, so every bridge and every child process a test starts inherits it. Fix any test that relied on the real default, without weakening what it checks; a test that checks the default itself passes its own `env` to `runsFolder`. Add one test that starts a bridge with no runs folder and checks that `projects.json` lands under the test temp root and not under `%TEMP%\machineroom-runs`. Run `npm test` twice and report the times. Files: `tests/helpers/isolate.js`, `tests/helpers/tempRoot.js` if it needs an export, `vite.config.js` (`setupFiles` only), the tests that break, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the `npm test` line), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**O19. A project starts with no lanes on (AGY 2, merged 0.1.401).** In `server/active-lanes.js` a project with no saved choice treats every lane as active (`active: chosen ? activeSet.has(lane.key) : true`), so a new project shows all thirteen lanes in the room and `/api/agents` lists them all. Make no choice mean no lanes: `GET /api/lanes` answers every lane `active: false` with `chosen: false`, `/api/agents` lists the orchestrator, the subs and the session readers but no lane, `GET /api/family` shows each lane with its off dot, and `POST /api/control/runs` refuses a lane on a project with no choice with 400 and `Pick the lanes for this project first.` The setup card already asks how many agents should work when `chosen` is false, so nothing changes in the page. `run-lane` from the command line is not affected. Update the tests that expect every lane on before a choice. Files: `server/active-lanes.js`, `server/lanes-source.js` (the agent list only), `server/family.js` (the `active` value only), `server/runs.js` (the refusal only), their tests, `docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md` (the `/api/lanes` and `/api/agents` sentences), `docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md`.

**O20. The recent projects list holds only real projects (AGY 3, merged 0.1.401).** `recordProject` in `server/projects.js` adds any folder a bridge starts on. Skip a folder that is inside the operating system's temp folder (`os.tmpdir()` and `TEMP`), a lane worktree (a path with a `.worktrees` folder in it, or a folder named in `docs/agents/lanes.json` as a lane worktree), or a runs folder (one that holds `runs.jsonl`). `recentProjects` already hides folders that no longer exist; also drop them from the file when it is read, at most once a minute. Add `POST /api/projects/forget` with `{ path, confirmed: true }`, a page Origin (403 without) and 400 for a path not in the list, that removes one entry and answers the new list; add `forgetProject` to the control client. Tests in `tests/projects.test.js`: a temp folder, a worktree and a runs folder are not recorded, a missing folder leaves the file, forget removes one and refuses without an Origin. ### Agents that can look at what they build (2026-10-29, the owner's request)

**H16. Lanes and orchestrators get the room's MCP servers (AGY, merged 0.1.401).** Today only the `machineroom` and `acp` harnesses get the MCP servers the owner adds in Settings, MCP tools (`<runs>/mcp.json`, read by `enabledMcpServers` in `scripts/mcp-bridge.mjs`). Claude Code and OpenCode lanes and the orchestrator turns get none, so no lane can open a browser even when the owner adds a browser MCP server there. Pass the enabled servers to both harnesses. Keep `src/harnesses/*.js` browser-safe: give `command()` a new optional `mcpConfig` path and let it return an optional `env` beside `file`, `args` and `cwd`. Claude Code: when `mcpConfig` is set, add `--mcp-config PATH` (do not add `--strict-mcp-config`, so the owner's own servers stay). The file holds `{ "mcpServers": { NAME: { "command", "args", "env" } or { "type": "http", "url" } } }`. OpenCode: when set, return `env: { OPENCODE_CONFIG: PATH }` with a file of `{ "mcp": { NAME: { "type": "local", "command": [command, ...args], "environment": env, "enabled": true } or { "type": "remote", "url", "enabled": true } } }`. Check the installed OpenCode reads `OPENCODE_CONFIG` and merges it with the user's config (run `opencode --help` or its docs, read only) before you rely on it, and if it does not, say so in your report and leave OpenCode out rather than guess. `scripts/run-lane.mjs` writes the file as `<runs>/<log name>.mcp.json` with mode 0o600 only when at least one server is enabled, passes it in, honours the returned `env` when it spawns, and deletes the file when the run ends; a dry run prints the path and the server names, never an env value. `server/orchestrator.js` does the same for each turn, as `<runs>/<chat log name>.mcp.json`. No enabled server means no file and the command is exactly as now. Never print, log or put an env value of a server in an error. Add no MCP server and no dependency yourself. Tests: the Claude and OpenCode command with and without `mcpConfig`, the file shapes for a stdio and an http server, no file when none is enabled, and the file removed after a run. Update ARCHITECTURE (the run-lane and orchestrator lines and the Settings MCP tools line) and the changelog. Files: src/harnesses/claude.js, src/harnesses/opencode.js, src/briefs/run.js, scripts/run-lane.mjs, scripts/mcp-bridge.mjs, server/orchestrator.js, tests/run-lane.test.js, tests/harnesses.test.js, tests/orchestrator.test.js, docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md.

**U83. The console's address opens in a new tab (OpenCode, merged 0.1.401).** The cyan `CONSOLE` above an agent finds an address in its newest lines (`addressIn` in `src/engine/terminals.js`) and only puts it in the title, and a click selects the agent. Make the address a link the owner can open. Add a pure `addressUrl(value)` beside `addressIn` that answers a full `http:` or `https:` URL or null: a value that already starts with `http://` or `https://` stays, a host and port such as `localhost:5173`, `127.0.0.1:8080` or `[::1]:3000` gets `http://`, a bare port gets `http://localhost:PORT`, and anything else (another scheme, `javascript:`, `file:`, a value with spaces) is null. When the console carries `is-address`, show the address as an `a.mr-term-console-link` in `.mr-term-console-label` with `href` from `addressUrl`, `target="_blank"` and `rel="noopener noreferrer"`; its click stops propagation, so it opens the tab and does not select the agent, and a click elsewhere on the console still selects it. The address is text from agent output, so set it with `textContent` and the `href` property, never `innerHTML`. Style the link in the console's cyan with an underline on hover, Space Mono 400, no bold. Also add an `Open ADDRESS` button to the selected agent's dock (`AgentDock.jsx`, `agentContext.js`) when its console has an address, which calls `window.open(url, '_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer')`. Tests: `addressUrl` for each case above, and the dock row. Update the DOM contract and the room behaviour line for the console in ARCHITECTURE, and the changelog. Files: src/engine/terminals.js, src/styles.css, src/ui/AgentDock.jsx, src/ui/agentContext.js, src/Stage.jsx, tests/terminals.test.js, tests/agent-context.test.js, docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md.

### Gaps found in the live check of the family tree (2026-10-29)

**V44. A sector lights while its team works (OpenCode 2, merged 0.1.401).** `setWorking(keys)` in `src/engine/hub.js` (V43) brightens a sector's arc while one of its agents works, but the room never calls it, so no arc ever lights. In `src/engine/room.js`, before `hub.update` each frame, pass the keys of the agents whose status is `working` in the room state, a sub orchestrator and its lanes included, and call `setWorking` only when that set changes, with no allocation per frame when it does not. Put the chip of the main orchestrator and of each sub on the side of the agent away from the hub centre on screen: project the agent and the hub centre each frame and pick the side by the sign of their screen x difference, with a small dead band so a chip does not flip when the two are almost in line. Lanes keep their side as now. Keep the choice in a pure exported helper in `src/engine/terminals.js` and test it, including the dead band. Check it by eye in `?replay&fixture=scenario-i` from the overview and after a quarter turn. Update the terminal chip line in ARCHITECTURE and the changelog. ### Findings from the live OpenCode team test (2026-10-29)

**O22. A failed turn or run says why (AGY, merged 0.1.401).** When OpenCode's free model hit its quota, the chat said only `The orchestrator stopped: exit 1.`, and the room message to a sub said `ended: failed ... exit 1`, though the harness printed the reason in its JSON output: a line `{"type":"error", ..., "error":{"type":"provider.quota","message":"Free usage limit reached. ..."}}` (OpenCode), and Claude Code prints a `result` line with `is_error` and its error text. Add an optional pure `failure(log)` to the harness descriptors in `src/harnesses/opencode.js` and `src/harnesses/claude.js` that answers the last error message the harness printed, or null. In `server/orchestrator.js`, a failed turn with no reply says `The orchestrator stopped: MESSAGE` with that text, and the room message for a lane run that ended failed (the wake built near `Run ${logName} on lane ${laneKey} ended`) adds a line `Error: MESSAGE` when the run's log has one. Pass the text through `maskKeys`, cut it to 300 characters, and keep `exit N` when there is none. Tests: both parsers on a recorded error line and on a log with none, the chat text for a failed OpenCode turn with a quota error, and the wake line. Update ARCHITECTURE and the changelog. Files: src/harnesses/opencode.js, src/harnesses/claude.js, server/orchestrator.js, tests/harnesses.test.js, tests/orchestrator.test.js, docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md.

**L5. A project run records its commits (OpenCode 5, merged 0.1.401).** In `scripts/run-lane.mjs` the run record of a `--project` run always has `branch: ''` and `commits: []` (`const commitsText = project ? '' : ...`), so the room told the subs `commits none` for two queue runs that each committed one change on `machineroom/queue-<lane>-<stamp>`. For a project run, record the folder's branch when it is not the project's starting branch and the commits made during the run: the `git log --oneline START..HEAD` in the folder the run worked in (its worktree with `--worktree`), where START is the head the runner read before the harness started. A plain run on the project's own branch with no commit records none, as now. Read git by `gitPath()` and name refs in full. Tests: a queue run with one committed task records its branch and one commit, a worktree run records the commits on its own branch, and a run with no commit records none. Update the run record line in ARCHITECTURE and the changelog. Files: scripts/run-lane.mjs, src/briefs/run.js, tests/run-lane.test.js, docs/agents/ARCHITECTURE.md, docs/agents/CHANGELOG.md.
